A duality between Lie algebroids and infinitesimal foliations
Jiaqi Fu

Abstract
There are two natural analogues of algebraic foliations in derived algebraic geometry, called partition Lie algebroids and infinitesimal derived foliations, and both make sense in general characteristics. We construct an equivalence between these two notions under some finiteness conditions. Our method is refining the PD Koszul duality in [ BM19 ] [ BCN21 ] using the (completed) Hodge filtration.



1
Introduction

Let
X
be a smooth variety of dimension
n
over
\mathbb{C}
. A
holomorphic foliation on
X
consists of a holomorphic atlas
\{\phi_{i}:U_{i}^{\subset X}\to V_{i}^{\subset\mathbb{C}^{k}}\times W_{i}^{\subset\mathbb{C}^{n-k}}\}
such that the transition maps are of the form
\phi_{j}\circ\phi^{-1}_{i}(x,y)=(f(x),g(x,y))
with
x\in V_{i}
and
y\in W_{i}
. The level set of each
x_{0}\in V_{i}
is a complex submanifold of
U_{i}
, and gluing up the overlapping ones gives rise to immersed complex submanifolds of
X
, which are called
leaves
. Holomorphic foliations have two natural algebraic analogues: one is given by smooth Lie algebroids, i.e.
k
-codimensional subbundles
\mathfrak{g}\hookrightarrow T_{X/k}
of the tangent bundle which are closed under the Lie bracket
[-,-]

[
Pra67
]
; the other is given by smooth algebraic foliations, namely quotient bundles
\Omega_{X/k}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
with a rank
k
kernel
I
being a differential ideal
[
Ayo18
]
, i.e. the composition
\bar{d}_{DR}:\mathcal{O}_{X}\to\Omega_{X/k}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
induces a quotient map of
commutative differential graded algebras

({\wedge}^{\bullet}_{R}\Omega_{X/k},d_{DR})\twoheadrightarrow({\wedge}^{\bullet}_{R}\Omega_{\mathscr{F}},\bar{d}_{DR})
. These two notions are shown to be equivalent using Chevalley-Eilenberg algebras
[
Mei17
, Proposition 12.4]
. However, we often need to consider their generalization with singularities, i.e. drop the locally free condition on
\mathfrak{g}
or
\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
, where a disharmony appears between the tangent formalism (e.g.
[
Miy85
, §3]
) and the cotangent formalism (e.g.
[
Bau75
, §1]
,
[
Mal77
, §3]
).

A well-known philosophy suggests that many algebro-geometric objects have well-behaved derived analogues. This paper is devoted to exploring the connection between the above two formalisms of algebraic foliations in derived algebraic geometry.

1.1
Formal geometry of foliations

An algebraic leaf of some algebraic foliation
\mathscr{F}
on
X/R
is a subvariety
i:Z\hookrightarrow X
such that
\Omega_{Z/R}
and
i^{*}\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
are isomorphic as quotient sheaves of
i^{*}\Omega_{X/R}
. Unfortunately, even a smooth algebraic foliation over
\mathbb{C}
might not admit any algebraic leaves. For instance, the smooth closed
1
-form
dx/x+dy
on
\mathrm{Spec}(\mathbb{C}[x,x^{-1},y])
, generating a subbundle differential ideal of rank
1
, has only transcendental leaves in the form of
\{xe^{y}=c\}
. When one works on a singular scheme or over a base ring without embedding into
\mathbb{C}
, even the definition of leaves can be tricky, not to mention their existence or uniqueness. One approach to fixing this is finding a formal version of Frobenius theorem, i.e. determining the infinitesimal deformations along the foliations. Some smooth cases of this problem were solved before the rise of derived geometry, e.g.
[
Eke87
, Proposition 2.4]
and
[
Miy85
, §6]
. However, proper complex varieties rarely foliate without singularity (e.g.
\mathbb{P}^{n}_{\mathbb{C}}
), while the singular foliations exist in great abundance
[
BB72
, p.287]
.

Complex derived foliations.

The notion of
derived foliations
, recently proposed by Toën and Vezzosi in
[
TV23a
, §1.2]
, offers a new framework for studying singular algebraic foliations over
\mathbb{C}
. Their definition could be motivated by the hidden smoothness philosophy: a reasonable singular foliation should be the truncation of some “regular” higher notion of foliations.

Recall that an algebraic foliation
\mathscr{F}

[
Ayo18
, §6.1]
on a scheme
\mathrm{Spec}(R)/\mathbb{C}
is determined by a quotient of quasi-coherent sheaves
f:\Omega_{R/\mathbb{C}}\twoheadrightarrow\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
, with the “integrability” condition that
f
gives rise to a
commutative differential graded algebra
(cdga) over
\mathbb{C}

R\xrightarrow{\bar{d}_{DR}}\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}\xrightarrow{\bar{d}_{DR}}\wedge^{2}_{R}\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}\xrightarrow{\bar{d}_{DR}}\wedge^{3}_{R}\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}\xrightarrow{\bar{d}_{DR}}\ldots,

whose cdga structure is inherited from the de Rham algebra
(\wedge^{\bullet}_{R}\Omega_{R/\mathbb{C}},d_{DR})
.

In derived geometry in characteristic 0, the ambient categories of rings and modules are replaced by the
\infty
-category of cdgas and their derived
\infty
-categories
[
TV08
]
. To express a foliation structure here, the strict cdga in modules as above should be replaced by a notion of
homotopy-coherent cdgas
in
\m_{\mathbb{C}}
, the derived
\infty
-category of chain complexes over
\mathbb{C}
. Here a
homotopy-coherent cochain complex
(Definition
2.6
) refers to
C^{\bullet}
a
\mathbb{Z}
-indexed collection of chain complexes and morphisms
d:C^{\bullet}\to C^{\bullet+1}
in
\m_{\mathbb{C}}
, where
d^{2}
vanishes up to a coherent homotopy. The homotopy-coherent cdgas are then
\eoo
-algebras in homotopy-coherent chain complexes by definition. A primary example is the
derived de Rham algebra

(\wedge^{\bullet}_{R}\mathbb{L}_{R/k},d_{DR})
in
[
Bha12
]
[
TV23a
, §1.2]
.

Definition 1.1
.

[
TV23a
, §1.2]

Let
\mathrm{Spec}(R)/\mathbb{C}
be an affine derived scheme of finite presentation. A
derived foliation

\mathscr{F}
on
\mathrm{Spec}(R)
is a homotopy-coherent cdga
1
1

1

It is modeled as a
graded mixed cdga
in
[
TV23a
]
.

(A^{\bullet},d)
over
\mathbb{C}
such that
A^{0}\simeq R
,
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}:=A^{1}
is a connective perfect
R
-module, and there is a natural equivalence of graded cdgas
\sym_{R}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}[-1])\simeq A^{\bullet}[-\text{\small\textbullet}]
(via
2.7
). The
R
-module
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}
is called the
cotangent complex
of
\mathscr{F}
.

The derived de Rham algebra
(\wedge^{\bullet}_{R}\mathbb{L}_{R/k},d_{DR})
is initial among the derived foliations in this sense.
If the truncation map
\Omega_{R/\mathbb{C}}\to\pi_{0}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}})=\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
is surjective, then
\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}
determines an algebraic foliation, and
\mathscr{F}
could be thought as a derived enhancement.

Formal deformation theory.
A principle introduced by Deligne and Drinfeld postulates that every formal moduli problem in characteristic
0
is controlled by a dg-Lie algebra. It was then further developed in
[
GM88
]

[
Kon03
]

[
Man09
]
, and eventually was upgraded to an equivalence by the work of Hinich
[
Hin01
]
, Pridham
[
Pri10
]
, and Lurie
[
Lur11
]
. This equivalence is generalized into a “many-object” version using
dg-Lie algebroids

[
GR19
]
[
Nui19b
]
.

Meanwhile, the existence of a (2-term) derived enhancement is related to the integrability of algebraic foliations
[
TV23a
, Corollary 1.5.4, 2.3.3]
by formal schemes. This also suggests that the higher structure of a derived foliation should determine the formal deformation along the “imaginary leaves”. Here, an advantage of
\mathscr{F}
compared to its truncation
\Omega_{\mathscr{F}}=\pi_{0}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}})
is that
\mathscr{F}
could be related to dg-Lie algebroids. Given a dg-Lie algebroid
\rho:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathbb{T}_{R/\mathbb{C}}

[
Nui19b
, Definition 2.1]
such that the underlying
R
-module
\mathfrak{g}
is perfect and of Tor-amplitude
(-\infty,0]
, the graded cdga
\sym_{R}(\mathfrak{g}^{\vee}[-1])
admits a natural derived foliation structure, whose extra differential is defined in terms of the Lie bracket and
\rho
. Toën and Vezzosi conjecture that this assignment is fully faithful
[
TV23a
, §1.3.1]
. Our goal is to prove this conjecture, as well as a more general statement in arbitrary characteristics.

Interlude: Some Conventions.

Until now, everything happens in characteristic 0. However, once we are equipped with some more refined machinery, most of the discussion also holds in general characteristics. We will return to this in §
1.3
with more details. Here, we only make necessary clarification and variation on the notions to state our main theorem.

The
derived commutative algebras
are chosen as our preferred generalization of cdgas, which are the algebras of the monad
\lsym
, cf.
[
Ill06
, I§4]

[
Kal15
]
for a definition using simplicial-cosimplicial algebras, or
[
Rak20
, §4.2]
applying the method of left-right extension in
[
BM19
, §3]
to
\sym
. For a map of simplicial commutative rings
R\to B
, a
foliation-like algebras

A
over
B
relative to
R
is a completely filtered derived
R
-algebra such that
\gr_{0}A\simeq B
and the natural map
\lsym_{B}(\gr_{1}A)\to\gr A
is an equivalence of graded algebras, see Notation
4.15
(2).

Alternatively, Proposition
5.1
shows that each foliation-like algebra
A
can be regarded as

B\xrightarrow{d}\mathbb{L}_{A}\xrightarrow{d}\lsym_{B}^{2}(\mathbb{L}_{A}[-1])[2]\xrightarrow{d}\ldots

(1)

a free graded derived algebra with a compatible homotopy-coherent differential
d
, where
\mathbb{L}_{A}:=\gr_{1}A[1]
is the
cotangent complex
of
A
. The foliation-like algebras are then a generalization of Toën and Vezzosi’s derived foliations (Remark
5.2
(2)) to general characteristics. Let
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap}
be the
\infty
-category of foliation-like algebras whose cotangent is an almost perfect
B
-module.

Write
\lagd
for the
\infty
-category of
(derived) partition Lie algebroids
over
B
relative to
R
(Definition
4.4
), which generalizes dg-Lie algebroids into general characteristics. One application of
\lagd
is to generalize Lurie and Pridham’s theorem into general characteristics
[
BM19
]

[
BMN
]
.

Main Theorem 1.2

(Affine case,
4.26
)
.

Let
R\to B
be a morphism of coherent simplicial commutative rings such that
\mathbb{L}_{B/R}
is an almost perfect
B
-module. Then, there is an equivalence

\aplagd\xrightarrow{\simeq}\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{B/R,\ap},

from the
\infty
-category of
dually almost perfect
(
2.28
) partition Lie algebroids to the
\infty
-category of
almost perfect
foliation-like algebras.

Remark 1.3
.

Here
B/R
is not necessarily almost of finite presentation. For instance, the inclusion
k\hookrightarrow k^{\mathrm{sep}}
from a field into its separable closure satisfies the conditions.

Infinitesimal derived foliations.
We will now globalize Theorem
1.2
to a geometric statement. Let
\mathbb{V}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}[-1])
be the
non-connective affine space
corepresented by
\lsym_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}[-1])
. As a “stacky vector bundle”, it admits a natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action by scaling. Meanwhile, a
R
-linear complete filtration could be regarded as a comultiplication by
R[\epsilon]
(with
|\epsilon|=1
and the Hopf structure
\Delta\epsilon=\epsilon\otimes 1+1\otimes\epsilon
), i.e. an action of
\Omega_{0}\mathbb{G}_{a}:=\mathrm{Spec}(R[\epsilon])
, see
[
PTVV13a
, Remark 1.1]
.

Motivated by these observations, an
infinitesimal derived foliations

[
TV23b
, §2]
(or Definition
5.7
)
\mathscr{F}
is by definition a
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\Omega_{0}\mathbb{G}_{a}
-equivariant
R
-stack such that the underlying graded stack being the
\mathbb{V}(\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}[-1])
for some
B
-module
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}
. Let
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
denote the
\infty
-category of infinitesimal derived foliations for which
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}
is
almost perfect
. Taking the non-connective affine space induces a categorical equivalence
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{B/R,\ap}\simeq\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
(Theorem
5.13
), which shows that our switch of convention causes no confusion.

The notion
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(-/R)
can be extended to
quasi-compact and quasi-separated
(qcqs) derived schemes
X
with
\mathbb{L}_{X/R}
almost perfect
[
Lur18
, Definition 2.8.4.4]
, by considering the “stacky vector bundle”
\mathbb{V}(E)
for quasi-coherent sheaves
E
and global
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\Omega_{0}\mathbb{G}_{a}
-actions on
\mathbb{V}(E)
compatible with the scaling.

Main Theorem 1.4

(Global case,
5.15
)
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme over an eventually coconnective coherent simplicial commutative ring
R
, whose cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{X/R}
is an almost perfect quasi-coherent sheaf. Then, there is an equivalence

\aplagdX\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(X/R),

from the
\infty
-category of
dually almost perfect
partition Lie algebroids to the
\infty
-category of infinitesimal derived foliations with
almost perfect cotangent complexes
.

1.2
Strategy of proof

Our main tool is the right-left extension and divided power Koszul duality presented in
[
BM19
]
[
BCN21
]
.

We first review filtrations in stable
\infty
-categories and the theory of pro-coherent modules in §
2
. Let
R
be a coherent simplicial commutative ring
[
Lur17
, Definition 7.2.4.16]
. The pro-coherent module category
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
enjoys the pleasant property that the
R
-linear dual
(-)^{\vee}
induces a symmetric monoidal equivalence of full subcategories between
\aperf_{R}
consisting of almost perfect modules and
\aperf^{\vee}_{R}
its essential image under
(-)^{\vee}
. We then recall the theory of (derived)
\infty
-operads (Definition
2.55
) in the pro-coherent context, and construct the
\infty
-categories of filtered and graded algebras.

Section
3
is devoted to a filtered PD Koszul duality. Let
\mathcal{P}
be a reduced derived
\infty
-operad (Proposition
3.9
) with PD Koszul dual
\kd(\mathcal{P})
. We construct an adjunction of filtered algebras

\kd:\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})\rightleftarrows\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}:\aq,

where
\aq
takes an increasingly filtered
\kd(\mathcal{P})
-algebra
L
to a decreasingly filtered
\mathcal{P}
-algebra
\aq(L)
, which is automatically complete (
4
). Applying
\aq
to the constantly filtered algebras gives rise to a sifted-colimit-preserving functor
\aqf:\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})\to\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}
.

When
\mathcal{P}
is the derived
\infty
-operad of
non-unital derived commutative algebras

\com^{nu}
, and its Koszul dual is by definition the derived PD
\infty
-operad of
(derived) partition Lie algebras

\dlie
, the functor
\aqf
is preferably written as
\cet
for (completed) Hodge filtered Chevalley-Eilenberg complex. If
R
is a regular
\mathbb{Q}
-algebra,
\dlie
is the operad of (shifted) dg-Lie algebras over
R
, and
\cet
is the ordinary Chevalley-Eilenberg functor filtered by the degree of forms. For each
R
-augmented algebra
S
,
\cet(\kd(S))
is exactly the Hodge-completed de Rham cohomology
\widehat{\mathrm{DR}}(R/S)
(Proposition
4.29
).

Theorem 1.5

(Theorem
3.25
)
.

If
\mathcal{P}
is
almost finitely presented

[
BCN21
, Definition 3.52]
so that
\kd(\mathcal{P})
is dually almost perfect, then the functor
\aqf
restricts to a fully faithful embedding

\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\aperf^{\vee}_{R})\hookrightarrow\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op},

whose essential image consists of complete
\mathcal{P}
-algebras
A
such that the graded pieces form a free graded
\mathcal{P}
-algebra generated by an almost perfect
R
-module
\gr_{1}A
concentrating at weight
1
.

Theorem 1.15 in
[
BM19
]
establishes a non-filtered Koszul duality for coconnective partition Lie algebras (of finite type). The novelty here is that we do not assume coconnectivity, for which the filtered structure plays a crucial role (even in characteristic
0
).

Example 1.6
.

Over
\mathbb{R}
, the Lie algebra cohomology of
\mathfrak{su}(2)
(or
\mathfrak{su}(2)[1]
as a shifted dg-Lie algebra) is the free cdga
\ce(\mathfrak{su}(2))\simeq\wedge_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}.e_{3})
with
|e_{3}|=-3
, cf.
[
MT91
, Vol.I Theorem 6.5.(2)]

[
CE48
, Theorem 15.2]
. Therefore, the corresponding shifted dg-Lie algebra of
\ce(\mathfrak{su}(2))
is abelian and has the underlying module
\mathbb{R}[3]
, which differs from
\mathfrak{su}(2)[1]
. It means that
\ce
without filtration does not embed the ordinary Lie algebras into the cdgas. At the same time,
\cet(\mathfrak{su}(2)[1])
as a graded mixed complex, is the strict cochain complex of the Lie algebra cohomology of
\mathfrak{su}(2)
, which loses no information.

From a geometric perspective, the formal moduli problem determined by
\mathfrak{su}(2)[1]
is
B\widehat{\mathrm{SU}}(2)
, the classifying stack of the formal group associated to
\mathrm{SU}(2)
. As a highly stacky object,
B\widehat{\mathrm{SU}}(2)
is not (pro-)corepresented by
\Gamma(B\widehat{\mathrm{SU}}(2),\mathcal{O})\simeq\ce(\mathfrak{su}(2)[1])
. However, as an infinitesimal derived foliation, the
\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\Omega_{0}\mathbb{G}_{a}
-action on
B\widehat{\mathrm{SU}}(2)
induces a complete filtered structure
[
Mou21
]
, where the graded stack is an affine stack corepresented by
\sym_{\mathbb{R}}(\mathbb{R}^{\oplus 3}[-1])
.

In §
4
, we construct
\lagd
the
\infty
-category of
partition Lie algebroids over
B
(relative to
R
)
for
R\to B
a morphism of simplicial commutative rings with some finiteness conditions, in the spirit of
[
BW20
]
. When
R
and
B
are regular
\mathbb{Q}
-algebras, the
\infty
-category
\lagd
is equivalent to the
\infty
-category of dg-Lie algebroids in
[
Nui19a
, §3]
.

Theorem 1.7

(Theorem
4.26
)
.

Let
R\to B
be a map of coherent simplicial commutative rings such that
\mathbb{L}_{B/R}
is almost perfect as an
B
-module. There is a fully faithful embedding, by a “many-object” variant of
\cet
,

\tc:\aplagd\hookrightarrow(\dafilbr)^{op}

from dually almost perfect partition Lie algebroids, to filtered
R
-algebras
A
with a natural augmentation
\gr_{0}A\simeq B
. Moreover, its essential image consists of almost perfect foliation-like algebras.

Then we construct in §
5
a functor
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
realizing foliation-like algebras as infinitesimal derived foliations, which relies on the fact that taking non-connective spectra
\dalg(\m_{R})\to(\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}})^{op}
is symmetric monoidal with respect to the cocartesian monoidal structures. Finally, we use a reasoning inspired by
[
Mon21
]
to prove that:

Theorem 1.8

(Theorem
5.13
)
.

Further assuming that
R
is eventually coconnective, there is a categorical equivalence

\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}:\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{B/R,\ap}\simeq\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)

between almost perfect foliation-like algebras and almost perfect infinitesimal derived foliations.

The required equivalence in the main theorem is gluing up
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc
on each affine chart.

1.3
Foliations in general characteristics

Ordinary algebraic geometry has two inequivalent generalizations in general characteristics, namely
derived algebraic geometry
based on simplicial commutative rings and
spectral algebraic geometry
based on
\eoo
-ring spectra. The aforementioned derived algebras serve as a non-connective generalization of simplicial commutative rings. The infinitesimal derived foliations also live in the world of derived algebraic geometry.

Now we exemplify the interest and subtlety of foliation theory away from characteristic
0
:

Example 1.9

(Kronecker’s criterion of root of unity)
.

Consider some
x=\exp(2\pi i\lambda)\in\mathbb{C}^{\times}
with
\lambda\in\bar{\mathbb{Q}}
. Here
x
is a root of unity (or equivalently
\lambda\in\mathbb{Q}
) precisely when, for almost every non-zero prime
\mathfrak{p}
of
\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}(\lambda)}
, the residue class of
\lambda
in
\kappa(\mathfrak{p})
belongs to the prime field
\mathbb{F}_{p}\subset\kappa(\mathfrak{p})

[
Esn23
, §2]
.

Bost gave a foliation-theoretic proof
[
Bos01
, Corollary 2.4]
by observing that, setting
G:=\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{Q}(\lambda)}\times\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{Q}(\lambda)}
, the graph of the multiplication by
\lambda
defines a sub-Lie algebra
\mathfrak{h}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Lie}(G)
and then a smooth algebraic foliation by
\mathrm{Lie}(G)\cong\Gamma(G,T_{G/\mathbb{Q}(\lambda)})
, and
x
is a root of unity if and only if
\mathfrak{h}
has an algebraic leaf, since the only codimension
1
subgroups of
G
are defined by one equation of the form
X^{a}Y^{b}=1
. On the other hand, the residue condition on
\lambda
is translated into, for almost all non-zero prime
\mathfrak{p}\subset\mathcal{O}_{\mathbb{Q}(\lambda)}
, the reduction of
\mathfrak{h}
to
\kappa(\mathfrak{p})
is closed under
restriction
. The question is then reduced to a special case of the generalized Grothendieck-Katz p-curvature conjecture. This conjecture is formulated in
[
ESBT99
, conjecture F]
and proved, under the condition that the analytic leaves contain open subsets satisfying the Liouville property, in
[
Bos01
, Theorem 2.1]
.

This example shows that, even in smooth case, a good notion of foliations away from characteristic
0
should include operations beyond the Lie bracket (e.g. the restriction) to fully capture the formal deformation along the “imaginary leaves”.

Here is an example containing higher restrictions.

Example 1.10
.

Let
L
be a derived partition Lie algebra over
\mathbb{F}_{2}
such that
\pi_{n}(L)=0
for
n\neq 0,1
. The homotopy operations on
L
consist of
([-,-],(-)^{\{2\}})
a restricted Lie structure on
\mathfrak{g}_{1}:=\pi_{1}(L)
, a
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
-representation structure on
\mathfrak{g}_{0}:=\pi_{0}(L)
and an additive higher restriction
R^{1}:\mathfrak{g}_{1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{0}
, cf. Proposition
3.35
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[-,-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\{2\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathfrak{g}_{1},-]}

In particular, the partition Lie algebra
\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}}
of the Frobenius kernel
\mu_{2}:=\ker((-)^{2}:\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{2}}\to\mathbb{G}_{m,\mathbb{F}_{2}})
2
2

2

encoding the formal moduli problem
B\mu_{2}
has
\mathfrak{g}_{i}\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}.D_{i}
(
i=0,1
), where the operations are given by
[D_{1},D_{i}]=0
(
i=0,1
),
(D_{1})^{\{2\}}=D_{1}
and
R^{1}(D_{1})=D_{0}
, see Proposition
3.38
.

A foliation-like algebra
A\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap}
captures the higher restrictions in a more subtle way. Following Notation
4.15
(2), the graded algebra
\gr A
is
\lsym_{B}(\mathbb{L}_{A}[-1])
rather than the wedge power
(\wedge_{B}\mathbb{L}_{A})[-*]
. The point is that, unlike in characteristic
0
, the derived symmetric power
\lsym
is no longer compatible with the degree shifting:

Example 1.11
.

Let
v
and
\tau
be the generators of
E:=\mathbb{L}_{\mu_{2}/\mathbb{F}_{2}}
at degree
1
and
0
respectively. One has easily
\wedge_{\mathbb{F}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{2}.\tau)[-*]\simeq\mathbb{F}_{2}\oplus\mathbb{F}_{2}.\tau[-1]
. However,
\pi_{*}(\lsym_{\mathbb{F}_{2}}(\mathbb{F}_{2}[-1].\tau))\cong\mathbb{F}_{2}[\tau,\st^{0}\tau,(\st^{0})^{\circ 2}\tau,\ldots]
is a polynomial algebra generated by
(\st^{0})^{\circ n}\tau
lies in degree
-1
and weight
2^{n}

[
Pri73
, Theorem 4.0.1]
.

Considering
d:E\to\lsym^{2}(E[-1])[2]
the extra differential from
\tc(\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}})
, the formulas
(D_{1})^{2}=D_{1}
and
R^{1}(D_{1})=D_{0}
are dual to the fact that
d:\tau\mapsto\tau^{2}
and
d:v\mapsto\st^{0}\tau
, Lemma
3.36
.

1.4
Related works

Partition Lie algebroids.
The filtered PD Koszul duality in §
3.3
is a continuation of
[
BM19
]
[
BCN21
]
. Here we add a filtered structure on the Koszul duality of (PD)
\infty
-operads and discuss the convergence of arity
0
. Our definition of partition Lie algebroids is in the spirit of
[
BW20
]
, and agrees with that in
[
BMN
]
. In characteristic
0
, a detailed discussion of Lie algebroids in pro-coherent modules could be found in
[
Nui19b
]
.

Derived foliations.
There is more than one natural notion of derived foliations away from characteristic
0
. For instance, there is a notion of
derived foliations
in §2
[
Toë20
]
other than infinitesimal derived foliations.

The scope of this paper is limited to the small Zariski site of some derived scheme, but most of the content should still hold on the small étale site of reasonable Deligne-Mumford stacks. For example, Alfieri proves a theorem similar to Theorem
1.8
for relative derived algebraic spaces in characteristic
0
in an ongoing project.

Conventions.
Unless stated conversely, we implicitly fix a Grothendieck universe
\mathbb{U}
and work with
\mathbb{U}
-small objects.

Every category mentioned will be, by default, an
\infty
-category, for which we mainly use the model of quasicategories detailed in
[
Lur09
]
. Similarly, every notion in algebra or topology should be understood in the
derived
or
homotopical
sense unless stated conversely. For instance, the module category
\m_{R}
means the derived
\infty
-category, while
\sym_{R}
and
\lsym_{R}
refer to the homotopical and derived symmetric power.

Two different notions of higher operads are involved in this paper. To avoid ambiguity, the ones through (generalized) symmetric sequences are referred as (generalized)
\infty
-operads, while the ones through fibrations of
\infty
-categories are called
(\infty,1)
-operads.

The homotopy-coherent chain complexes appear as the comodules of a derived Hopf algebra, which will take different forms as
R[\epsilon]
,
\dD^{\vee}_{-}
or
1_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{1_{\mathcal{C}}[t]}1_{\mathcal{C}}
depending on the context.

We are also grateful to Joan Millès and Joost Nuiten for introducing us to the world of homotopical algebras. We want to thank Benjamin Antieau, Tasos Moulinos and Marco Robalo for helpful conversations.

We wish to thank Institut de Mathématiques de Toulouse and LabEx CIMI for their funding and support.
We also thank Yifei Zhu and Southern University of Science and Technology for their hospitality during my visit in the summer of 2023.

2
Preliminaries

This section is dedicated to reviewing filtered algebras in stable
\infty
-categories and fixing notations.

2.1
Filtrations in module categories

Notation 2.1
.

For an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, let
\fil\mathcal{C}
(resp.
\gr\mathcal{C}
) be the
\infty
-category of filtered (resp. graded) objects in
\mathcal{C}
. A filtered object can be written as
F_{\star}X=(\ldots\to F_{1}X\to F_{0}X\to F_{-1}X\to\ldots)
. Evaluating at degree
n
admits fully faithful left adjoint in both cases,

(-)_{n}:\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\fil\mathcal{C},\ \ \ \ [-]_{n}:\mathcal{C}\hookrightarrow\gr\mathcal{C},

where
(-)_{n}
is given by
F_{i}(X)_{n}\simeq X
for
i\leq n
and
F_{i}(X)_{n}\simeq 0
otherwise,
[-]_{n}
is the embedding into weight
n
. A detailed discussion of filtrations in stable
\infty
-category could be find in
[
GP18
, §2]
.

Definition 2.2
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category, the
functor of associated graded objects

\gr:\fil\mathcal{C}\to\gr\mathcal{C}

takes
X=\{F_{i}X\}_{i\in\mathbb{Z}}
to
\gr X
such that
\gr_{i}X\simeq\mathrm{cofib}(F_{i+1}X\to F_{i}X)
.

Recall that the
\infty
-category
\mathcal{P}r^{L}
of presentable
\infty
-categories is endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure
[
Lur17
, Proposition 4.8.1.15]
such that
\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{L})
consists of presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories. The
\infty
-category
\pr^{\st}
of presentable stable
\infty
-categories is a symmetric monoidal localization of
\pr^{L}

[
Lur17
, Proposition 4.8.2.18]
.

Remark 2.3
.

The addition of
\mathbb{Z}
can be understood as a symmetric monoidal structure on
\mathbb{Z}^{\leq}

the
1
-category defined by the linear order
\leq
or
\mathbb{Z}^{dis}

the discrete
1
-category
. By Day convolution
[
Lur17
, §2.2.6]
, we can enhance
\fil(-)
and
\gr(-)
into endofunctors of
\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{\st})
, while the embeddings
(-)_{0}
,
[-]_{0}
and associated graded objects
\gr
can be regarded as natural transformations between
\fil(-)
,
\gr(-)
and
id_{\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{\st})}
.

Definition 2.4
.

Assume that
\mathcal{C}
is a stable
\infty
-category admitting sequential limits.

(1) Let
\gr_{\geq a}\mathcal{C}\subset\gr\mathcal{C}
(
\gr_{\leq-a}\mathcal{C}\subset\gr\mathcal{C}
) be the full subcategory spanned by
X_{\star}
such that
X_{i}\simeq 0
for all
i<a
(resp.
i>-a
).

(2) A filtered object
\{F_{i}X\}
in
\mathcal{C}
is said to be
complete
if
\lim F_{i}X\simeq 0
. By
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}
denote the full subcategory of
\fil\mathcal{C}
spanned by the complete filtered objects in
\mathcal{C}
.

(3) Denote the full subcategory of
\fil\mathcal{C}
spanned by (complete)
X
such that
\gr(X)\in\gr_{\geq a}\mathcal{C}
(resp.
\gr(X)\in\gr_{\leq-a}\mathcal{C}
) by
\fil_{\geq a}\mathcal{C}
(resp.
\fil_{\leq-a}\mathcal{C}
).

Remark 2.5
.

For each
(\mathcal{C},\otimes)\in\calg(\pr^{\st})
, Proposition 2.2.1.9 in
[
Lur17
]
shows that there is a symmetric monoidal structure
\widehat{\otimes}
on
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}
such that
\gr
admits a factorization of symmetric monoidal left adjoints

\fil\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\wedge}}\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\gr|_{\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}}}\gr\mathcal{C}.

Now we interpret
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}
as a generalization of cochain complexes. Recall that a cochain complex in some abelian category
\mathscr{A}
consists of a sequence of objects
C^{\bullet}
in
\mathscr{A}
and morphisms

\ldots\xrightarrow{d}C^{n-1}\xrightarrow{d}C^{n}\xrightarrow{d}C^{n+1}\xrightarrow{d}\ldots

such that
d^{2}=0
. More categorically, one can consider the pointed 1-category
\ch
given by

\displaystyle\mathrm{obj}\ch=

\displaystyle\ \mathbb{Z}\cup\{*\},

\displaystyle\ch(n,m)=

\displaystyle\begin{cases}\{id,\mathbf{0}\},&\text{if }m=n;\\
\{\partial,\mathbf{0}\},&\text{if }m=n-1;\\
\{0\},&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

A cochain complex in
\mathscr{A}
is then equivalent to a contravariant functor
\ch^{op}\to\mathscr{A}
preserving the zero object. This observation motivates the following definition:

Definition 2.6
.

For a stable
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
, we define the
\infty
-category of
homotopy-coherent cochain complexes
in
\mathcal{C}
as
\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}:=\mathrm{Fun}_{*}(N(\ch^{op}),\mathcal{C})
the
\infty
-category of pointed functors.

The natural inclusion
\mathbb{Z}^{dis}\hookrightarrow\ch
induces a conservative functor
u:\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}\to\gr\mathcal{C}
. If
\mathcal{C}
is equipped with a t-structure,
\gr\mathcal{C}
has a
negative t-structure
such that
X_{\star}\in(\gr\mathcal{C})^{-}_{\geq 0}
precisely when
X_{n}\in\mathcal{C}_{\geq-n}
for all
n\in\mathbb{Z}
. The preferred t-structure on
\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}
is obtained by transferring the negative t-structure on
\gr\mathcal{C}
along
[-*]\circ u
, where the
shearing functor

[-*]:\gr\mathcal{C}\to\gr\mathcal{C}
sends
X_{\star}
to
X_{\star}[-*]
. In particular,
(\dg_{-}\mathcal{C})^{\heartsuit}
is exactly the 1-category of cochain complexes in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
.

Theorem 2.7

(Theorem 4.7, Proposition 6.9 and 6.13
[
Ari21
]
)
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a stable
\infty
-category with sequential limits. There is an equivalence of
\infty
-categories
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}
that maps
F_{\star}X
to a homotopy-coherent cochain complex

\ldots\xrightarrow{d}\gr_{n-1}X[n-1]\xrightarrow{d}\gr_{n}X[n]\xrightarrow{d}\gr_{n+1}X[n+1]\xrightarrow{d}\ldots,

where the underlying map of
d
are given by the cofibre sequences
F_{n-1}X/F_{n+1}X\to\gr_{n-1}X\to\gr_{n}X[1]
.
Moreover, if
\mathcal{C}
is presentably symmetric monoidal, then this equivalence induces a symmetric monoidal structure on
\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}
and
[-*]\circ u:\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}\to\gr\mathcal{C}
is symmetric monoidal.

Additionally, if
\mathcal{C}
is endowed with a compatible t-structure, this equivalence identifies the Beilinson t-structure on
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}
and the above t-structure on
\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}
. The heart
(\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C})^{Bei,\heartsuit}
of Beilinson t-structure is symmetric monoidally equivalent to the 1-category
(\dg_{-}\mathcal{C})^{\heartsuit}
of cochain complexes in
\mathcal{C}^{\heartsuit}
with the ordinary tensor product.

Remark 2.8
.

For
\mathcal{C}\in\calg(\pr^{\st})
, there is another approach to
\dg_{-}\mathcal{C}
using comodules in
[
Rak20
]
. Let
1_{\mathcal{C}}[t]:=1_{\mathcal{C}}[\mathbb{N}]
be the algebra whose multiplication is given by the commutative monoid
\mathbb{N}
. There is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\fil\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathrm{LMod}_{1_{\mathcal{C}}[t]}(\gr\mathcal{C})

[
Mou21
, 3.4]
identifying filtered objects with modules over
1_{\mathcal{C}}[t]
. Denote
\dD^{\vee}_{-}:=1_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{1_{\mathcal{C}}[t]}1_{\mathcal{C}}
, the graded bicommutative algebra given by bar construction. Further, Theorem 3.2.14 in
[
Rak20
]
shows that there is a factorization of
\gr

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}_{\dD^{\vee}_{-}}(\gr\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gr\mathcal{C}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces 1_{\mathcal{C}}\otimes_{1_{\mathcal{C}}[t]}-}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}

that induces an equivalence
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{C}\simeq\mathrm{LComod}_{\dD^{\vee}_{-}}(\gr\mathcal{C})
in
\calg(\pr^{\st})
. Besides, the method of
[
Ari21
, Theorem 7.8]
provides an equivalence of these two approaches. In particular, if
\mathcal{C}=\m_{k}
for some cdga over
\mathbb{Q}
,
\dg_{-}\m_{k}
has an explicit model given by
graded mixed complexes
, refer to
[
PTVV13b
, §1.1]

[
TV23a
, §1]
for details and applications in derived geometry.

Many presentable stable
\infty
-categories appear as the module categories of certain additive
\infty
-categories. Recall that an additive
\infty
-category is an
\infty
-category which admits finite products and finite coproducts and whose homotopy category is an additive category in the classical sense.

Definition 2.9
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be an additive
\infty
-category. The
\infty
-category of
(left)
\mathscr{A}
-modules
is defined as
\m_{\mathscr{A}}:=\text{Fun}_{\oplus}(\mathscr{A}^{op},\Sp)
, the
\infty
-category of functors into
\Sp
that preserve finite direct sums.

Remark 2.10
.

By Remark C.1.5.9 in
[
Lur18
]
, the module category
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
can alternatively be defined as the stablization of
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathscr{A})
, where
\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(-)
is taking the nonabelian derived category
[
Lur09
, §5.5.8]
. Thus the stablized Yoneda functor induces a fully faithful embedding
j:\mathscr{A}\to\m_{\mathscr{A}}
. Additionally, the functor
j
exhibits
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
as the initial presentable stable
\infty
-category receiving a functor from
\mathscr{A}
that preserves finite direct sums
[
Lur17
, Proposition 4.8.2.18]
.

The objects in
\mathscr{A}
with splitting filtration are building blocks to build filtered modules:

Notation 2.11
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be an additive
\infty
-category  and
I
be one of the following integer intervals:
(-\infty,\infty)
,
(-\infty,-a]
or
[a,\infty)
. Let
\sgr_{I}\mathscr{A}\subset\gr\mathscr{A}
be the full subcategory consisting of all
X
such that
X_{i}\simeq 0
for all but finitely many
i\in I
. Define
\sfil_{I}\mathscr{A}
as the full subcategory of
\fil_{I}\mathscr{A}\subset\fil_{I}\m_{\mathscr{A}}
spanned by all complete
X
such that
\gr(X)\in\sgr_{I}\mathscr{A}
.

Example 2.12
.

Let
R
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring spectrum, and
I
be as in Notation
2.11
. The
\infty
-category
\vect^{\omega}_{R}\subset\m_{R}
of finitely generated free left
R
-modules is additive. In fact, the ordinary inclusion exhibits
\m_{R}
as the module category of
\vect^{\omega}_{R}
. Moreover, Proposition
2.17
will show that the following inclusions

\sfil_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}\hookrightarrow\fil_{I}\m_{R},\ \ \ \ \sgr_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}\hookrightarrow\gr_{I}\m_{R},

can be identified with the stablized Yoneda functors
\sfil_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}\hookrightarrow\m_{\sfil_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}}
and
\sgr_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}\hookrightarrow\m_{\sgr_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}}
.

The module category
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
is equiped with a t-structure such that the
(co)connective
modules are the functors
\mathscr{A}^{op}\to\Sp
taking value in
\Sp_{\geq 0}
(
\Sp_{\leq 0}
).

Definition 2.13
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be an additive
\infty
-category. An
\mathscr{A}
-module
M
is said to be

(1)
perfect
if it is a compact object in
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
;

(2)
almost perfect
if for each
n
, there exists an
n
-connected map
P_{n}\to M
with
P_{n}
being perfect;

(3)
coherent
if it is almost perfect and eventually coconnective.

The corresponding full subcategories will be denoted as
\perf_{\mathscr{A}}
,
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
and
\coh_{\mathscr{A}}
respectively.

The basic properties of almost perfect modules are listed in
[
Lur17
, Proposition 7.2.4.11]
, which holds for
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
without changing the proof.

Definition 2.14
.

An additive
\infty
-category
\mathscr{A}
is said to be
left coherent
if
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
inherits a t-structure from
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
, and it is said to be
right coherent
if
\mathscr{A}^{op}
is left coherent. When
\mathscr{A}
is both left and right coherent, we say it is
coherent
.

Example 2.15
.

Let
R
be a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring spectrum. The additive
\infty
-category
\vect^{\omega}_{R}
is coherent precisely when
R
is coherent as a ring spectrum in the sense of
[
Lur17
, Definition 7.2.4.16]
.

The perfect filtered modules can be detected from their graded pieces. For each
\mathcal{C}\in\pr^{\st}
, write the full subcategory of compact objects as
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{\sgr}^{\omega}\subset\gr\mathcal{C}
denote the full subcategory consisting of all
X
such that
X_{i}\simeq 0
for all but finitely many
i\in\mathbb{Z}
and
X_{i}\in\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
for each
i
, and
\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega}
be the full subcategory of
\fil\mathcal{C}
spanned by complete
X
such that
\gr(X)\in\mathcal{C}_{\sgr}^{\omega}
.

Lemma 2.16
.

For any
\mathcal{C}\in\pr^{\st}
, there are natural equivalences of
\infty
-categories

\text{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\fil\mathcal{C},\ \ \ \ \text{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{\sgr}^{\omega})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\gr\mathcal{C},

which identitfy
\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega}
(or
\mathcal{C}_{\sgr}^{\omega}
) with the
\infty
-category of compact filtered (resp. graded) objects in
\mathcal{C}
.

Proof.

Set
\fil_{[m,n]}\mathcal{C}:=\fil_{\geq m}\mathcal{C}\cap\fil_{\leq n}\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega,[m,n]}:=\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega}\cap\fil_{[m,n]}\mathcal{C}
. For any finite
m,n
,
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.3.5.15]
shows that there is an equivalence

\text{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega,[m,n]})\simeq\fil_{\geq m}\mathcal{C}\cap\fil_{\leq n}\mathcal{C}
. Meanwhile, the construction
\text{Ind}(-)
preserves colimits
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.5.7.10]
, which means that
\text{Ind}(\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega})\simeq\text{Ind}(\colim_{m,n}\mathcal{C}_{\sfil}^{\omega,[m,n]})\simeq\colim_{m,n}\fil_{[m,n]}\mathcal{C}\simeq\fil\mathcal{C}
. The proof is done by mutatis mutandis for the graded case.∎

Proposition 2.17
.

Take an additive
\infty
-category
\mathscr{A}
. There are natural equivalences in
\pr^{\st}

\m_{\sfil_{I}\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\fil_{I}\m_{\mathscr{A}},\ \ \ \ \m_{\sgr_{I}\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\gr_{I}\m_{\mathscr{A}},

where
I
is one of the following integer intervals:
(-\infty,\infty)
,
(-\infty,-a]
or
[a,\infty)
.

Proof.

The inclusion
\sfil\mathscr{A}\subset\fil\m_{\mathscr{A}}
factors through
(\m_{\mathscr{A}})_{\sfil}^{\omega}
, and the latter is the smallest thick full subcategory containing
\sfil\mathscr{A}
. Thus, the functor
\m_{\sfil_{I}\mathscr{A}}\to\fil_{I}\m_{\mathscr{A}}
given by the universal property is an equivalence by Lemma
2.16
and
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.2.4]
. The other cases are proved in the same way.
∎

Now we characterize the almost perfect filtered or graded modules.

Proposition 2.18
.

Suppose that
\mathscr{A}
is a coherent additive
\infty
-category.
Fix
I=(-\infty,\infty)
,
(-\infty,-a]
or
[a,\infty)
. The
\infty
-category
\sgr_{I}\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}:=\aperf_{\gr_{I}\mathscr{A}}
is spanned by all
X\in\gr_{I}\m_{\mathscr{A}}
such that
\oplus_{i}X_{i}
lies in
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
, while the
\infty
-category
\sfil_{I}\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}:=\aperf_{\sfil_{I}\mathscr{A}}
is spanned by all complete
Y\in\fil_{I}\m_{\mathscr{A}}
such that
\gr(Y)\in\sgr_{I}\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
. .

In particular, the additive
\infty
-categories
\sfil_{I}\mathscr{A}
and
\sgr_{I}\mathscr{A}
are coherent as well.

Proof.

The graded case is straightforward from Lemma
2.16
and (2) of
[
Lur17
, Proposition 7.2.4.11]
.

For each
Y\in\sfil_{I}\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
, (up to a shifting) there is a simplicial object
Y^{\bullet}
in
\sfil_{I}\mathscr{A}
such that
|Y^{\bullet}|\simeq Y
, as shown in (5) of
[
Lur17
, Proposition 7.2.4.11]
. Then, since
\gr Y^{\bullet}\in\sgr_{*}\mathcal{A}
,
\gr Y\simeq|\gr Y^{\bullet}|
is almost perfect. For completeness, it suffices to note that each term of
Y^{\bullet}
is complete, and
\pi_{n}(Y)\cong\pi_{n}(Y^{\bullet}_{\leq n+1})
with
Y^{\bullet}_{\leq n+1}
being the
(n+1)
-skeletal object.

Conversely, take any completely filtered
Y
with
\gr(Y)\in\sgr_{I}\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
. The truncation
\tau_{\leq n}Y
for each
n
is a finite sequence of coherent modules, which is then almost perfect. This implies that
Y
itself is almost perfect as well.
∎

Example 2.19
.

If
R
is a coherent connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring spectrum, then
\sfil_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}
and
\sgr_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}
are coherent.

We close this subsection with a recollection of spectral Mackey functors, which are useful for constructing (filtered) derived PD
\infty
-operads. Let
G
be a finite group. Its
effective Burnside
\infty
-category

A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)
has finite
G
-sets as objects, and the morphism spaces are the
\eoo
-spaces of spans
X\leftarrow Z\rightarrow Y
of finite
G
-sets
[
Bar17
, 3.6]
. The effective Burnside
\infty
-category
A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)
is pointed by the empty set, and it admits direct products calculated by the sums of
G
-sets. In the light of
[
BGS19
, 2.15]
, the product of
G
-sets gives rise to a symmetric monoidal structure on
A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)
. Additionally, each span
X\leftarrow Z\rightarrow Y
can be regarded as a point in
\map_{A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)}(X,Y)
as well as
\map_{A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)}(Y,X)
. This induces a natural equivalence
A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)^{op}\simeq A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)
.

Definition 2.20
.

The
\infty
-category
\Sp^{G}
of
spectral Mackey functors of
G
or
genuine
G
-spectra

\Sp^{G}:=\text{Fun}^{\oplus}(A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)^{op},\Sp)\subset\text{Fun}(A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)^{op},\Sp)

is defined as the full subcategory of direct-product-preseriving functors from
A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)
to
\Sp
.

The
Burnside
\infty
-category
A(G)
of
G
is the universal additive
\infty
-category receiving a functor from
A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)
that preserves finite direct sums. The
\infty
-category A(G) has finite
G
-sets as objects, and the mapping space
\map_{A(G)}(X,Y)
is the group completion
\Omega B\map_{A^{\mathrm{eff}}(G)}(X,Y)
. There are natural equivalences
\Sp^{G}\simeq\m_{A(G)}
and
\Sp^{G}_{\geq 0}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(A(G))
by the universal properties. Additionally, the adjunction of inclusion and truncation

i:\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(A(G))\rightleftarrows\Sp^{G}:\tau_{\geq 0}

can be identified with the adjunction of Mackey stablization
[
Bar17
, 7.1]
. For this reason, we write the spectral Mackey functor
represented by some finite
G
-set
X
as
\infsus X
.

Following
[
BGS19
, 3.8]
, the
\infty
-category
\Sp^{G}
is also endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure. For each finite
G
-set
X
, the genuine
G
-spectrum
\Sigma^{\infty}_{+}X
has itself as a dual in
\Sp^{G}
, and the coevaluation and evaluation are given by the following spans

*\leftarrow X\xrightarrow{\text{diag}}X\times X,\ \ \ \ X\times X\xleftarrow{\text{diag}}X\rightarrow*.

Notation 2.21
.

Let
G
be a finite group and
A
an abelian group. The
constant Mackey functor
\underline{A}
sends finite
G
-set
X
to
\map(X/G,A)
, the abelian group of
A
-valued function on the orbit set. Given a span
f
of finite
G
-sets
X\leftarrow Z\rightarrow Y
,
\underline{A}(f)
is the composite of the pullback along
Z\to X
and the summation on the fibres of
Z\rightarrow Y
. This assignment is promoted to a sifted-colimit-preserving functor
\m_{\mathbb{Z},\geq 0}\to\Sp^{G}
by left Kan extension, which is lax symmetric monoidal
[
BCN21
, Lemma 2.14]
.

Definition 2.22
.

Given a connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring spectrum
R
over
\mathbb{Z}
, by
\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}
denote
the
\infty
-category of left
\underline{R}
-modules
in
\Sp^{G}
. The
\infty
-category
R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
of
finitely generated free
\underline{R}
-modules
is the full subcategory of
\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}
consisting of the objects of the form
\underline{R}\otimes\infsus X
, where
X
is a finite
G
-set.

Remark 2.23
.

The objects of
R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
projectively generate
\m^{G}_{\underline{R},\geq 0}
. There are equivalences

\m_{R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\m^{G}_{\underline{R}},\ \ \ \ \m_{\sfil_{I}R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}\simeq\fil_{I}\m^{G}_{\underline{R}},\ \ \ \ \m_{\sgr_{I}R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}\simeq\gr_{I}\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}.

Supposing that
R
is coherent, the additive
\infty
-categories
R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
,
\sfil_{I}R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
and
\sgr_{I}R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
are all coherent due to
[
BCN21
, Lemma 2.11]
and Proposition
2.17
.

2.2
Pro-coherent modules and right-left extension

Now we sketch out the framework of pro-coherent modules, following
[
BM19
]
[
BCN21
, §2]
, which provides us with a good duality theory of almost perfect modules. We also emphasize its compatibility with filtrations.

Definition 2.24
.

For a coherent additive
\infty
-category
\mathscr{A}
, the
\infty
-category of
(left) pro-coherent
\mathscr{A}
-modules
is defined as
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}:=\text{Ind}(\coh^{op}_{\mathscr{A}^{op}})
.

Remark 2.25
.

(1) A functor
F:\aperf_{\mathscr{A}^{op}}\to\Sp
is said to be
convergent
if
F(X)\simeq\lim_{n}F(\tau_{\leq n}X)
holds for every
X\in\aperf_{\mathscr{A}^{op}}
. The
\infty
-category
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
can be identified with
\text{Fun}_{ex,conv}(\aperf_{\mathscr{A}^{op}},\Sp)
, the
\infty
-category of covergent exact functors. Following this,
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
carries a left complete, accessible
t
-structure such that
M\in\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
is connective if and only if the associated exact convergent functor
M:\aperf_{\mathscr{A}^{op}}\to\Sp
is right exact.

(2) The evaluation functor
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}^{op}}\times\mathscr{A}\to\Sp
induces an adjunction

\iota:\m_{\mathscr{A}}\rightleftarrows\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}:\nu,

which exhibits
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
as a right completion of
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
[
BCN21
, Proposition 2.26]
.

Example 2.26
.

Set
\QC^{\vee}_{R}:=\QC^{\vee}_{\vect^{\omega}_{R}}
for
R
, a coherent connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring spectrum. By Lemma
2.16
and Proposition
2.18
, there are natural categorical equivalences

\QC^{\vee}_{\sfil_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}}\simeq\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R},\ \ \ \ \QC^{\vee}_{\sgr_{I}\vect^{\omega}_{R}}\simeq\gr_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}.

When
R
is eventually coconnective,
\perf_{R^{op}}
can be identified with a full subcategory of
\coh_{R^{op}}
, then
\iota:\m_{R}\hookrightarrow\QC^{\vee}_{R}
is fully faithful. The same thing holds in filtered or graded context as well.

Example 2.27
.

Take a coherent connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-algebra
R
over
\mathbb{Z}
and a finite group
G
. There is

\QC^{\vee}_{\sfil_{I}R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}\simeq\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]},\ \ \ \ \QC^{\vee}_{\sgr_{I}R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}\simeq\gr_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}.

Observe that for finite
G
-sets
X
and
Y
, the mapping space between the free
\underline{R}
-modules

\displaystyle\map_{\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}}(\underline{R}\otimes\infsus X,\underline{R}\otimes\infsus Y)\simeq R^{(X\times Y)/G}

is a product of finite copies of
R
. As a result, if
R
is again eventually coconnective, there is an inclusion
\perf_{R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]^{op}}\hookrightarrow\coh_{R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]^{op}}
. It implies that the functor
\iota:\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}\to\QC^{\vee}_{{R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]}}
is fully faithful.
If
R
is discrete,
R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
is equivalent with the full additive subcategory of ordinary
R[G]
-modules spanned by
R[G/H]
for
H<G
.

Right-left extension is the fundamental method of constructing operations in pro-coherent context. Here we quote some useful results from
[
BM19
]

[
BCN21
]
for the convenience of readers:

Definition 2.28
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be a coherent additive
\infty
-category. The
\infty
-category
\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
of
dually almost perfect
\mathscr{A}
-modules
is defined as the essential image of the Yoneda embedding
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}^{op}}^{op}\hookrightarrow\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
. By
\aperf_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0}^{\vee}
denote the full subcategory of
\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
consisting of the functors co-represented by some connective
X\in\aperf^{op}_{\mathscr{A}^{op}}
.

Every
F:\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0}\to\mathcal{V}
is equivalent to
F^{op}:\aperf_{\mathscr{A},\geq 0}\simeq\aperf^{\vee,op}_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0}\xrightarrow{F}\mathcal{V}^{op}
. Here,
F
is said to be
regular
if
F^{op}
is convergent. We recall Proposition 2.40 in
[
BCN21
]
:

Proposition 2.29
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be a coherent additive
\infty
-category and
\mathcal{V}
an
\infty
-category with sifted colimits. Then the restriction induces an equivalence

\mathrm{Fun}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathcal{V})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathrm{Fun}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0},\mathcal{V}),

where the right-hand side consists of regular functors that preserves finite geometric realizations, and the inverse is left Kan extension.

Remark 2.30
.

Let
\text{End}^{\dap,\eqslantless 0}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}})
be the
\infty
-category of sifted-colimit-preserving endofunctors that preserve
\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0}
. The former proposition induces a strictly monoidal equivalence

\text{End}^{\dap,\eqslantless 0}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}})\simeq\text{End}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0}),

which is crucial for the construction of the partition Lie algebroid monad.

Many sifted-colimit-preserving functors are induced from
locally polynomial functors
using the above proposition and left Kan extension.

Definition 2.31
.

A functor
F:\mathscr{A}\to\mathscr{B}
between two additive
\infty
-categories is said to be

(1)
of degree
\leq 0

if
F
is constant;

(2)
of degree
\leq n
if the difference
\text{fib}(F(X\oplus-)\to F(-))
is of degree
\leq(n-1)
for any
X\in\mathscr{A}
;

(3)
locally polynomial
if
F
arises as the colimit of a sequence
F_{1}\to F_{2}\to\ldots
of functors in
\text{Fun}(\mathscr{A},\mathscr{B})
, such that each
F_{i}
is of finite degree, and that the sequence
F_{1}(X)\to F_{2}(X)\to\ldots
is eventually constant for any
X\in\mathscr{A}
.

Given a locally polynomial functor
F:\mathcal{A}\to\mathcal{B}\subset\QC^{\vee}_{\mathcal{B}}
, right Kan extension gives rise to
F^{R}\in\text{Fun}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0},\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}})

[
BCN21
, Proposition 2.46]
. Then, left Kan extension gives rise to a sifted-colimit-preserving functor
F^{RL}:\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}\to\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}}
by Proposition
2.29
, which is called the
right-left derived functor
of
F
.

Right-left extension is a functorial procedure. Write
\mathcal{A}dd^{\text{coh,poly}}\subset\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}
the
\infty
-category of coherent additive
\infty
-categories and locally polynomial functors, and
\mathcal{P}r^{\text{st},\Sigma}\subset\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}
the
\infty
-category of presentable stable
\infty
-categories and sifted-colimit-preserving functors (strictly larger than
\pr^{\st}
). Then, we recall Theorem 2.52 in
[
BCN21
]
:

Theorem 2.32
.

There is a natural transformation of symmetric monoidal functors

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{A}dd^{\coh,\mathrm{poly}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{P}r^{\st,\Sigma}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\m}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\QC^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\iota}

sending each locally polynomial functor to its right-left derived functor.

Proposition 2.55 in
[
BCN21
]
gives a good duality theory of almost perfect modules:

Proposition 2.33
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be a coherent additive
\infty
-category endowed wih a non-unital symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
preserving finite direct sums. Assume that the induced
\otimes
on
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
admits an eventually connective unit
\mathbbb{1}
, and each object in
\mathscr{A}
is dualizable with the dual object existing in
\mathscr{A}
. Then taking duals gives rise to an equivalence,

(-)^{\vee}:=\hom_{\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}}(-,\mathbbb{1}):\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\aperf^{\vee,op}_{\mathscr{A}}.

Example 2.34
.

Consider a coherent additive
\infty
-category
\mathscr{A}
, e.g.
\vect^{\omega}_{R}
or
R[\mathcal{O}_{G}]
, the functors
(-)_{n}
,
[-]_{n}
,
F_{n}
and
\gr
are extended to the pro-coherent context. The extended functors coincide with those obtained directly from filtration and grading by Example
2.26
or
2.27
.

Example 2.35
.

Fix a finite group
G
and a coherent connective
\mathbb{E}_{1}
ring
R
over
\mathbb{Z}
. The
genuine
G
-fixed points functor

(-)^{G}:\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}\to\m_{R}
is evaluating at
*\cong G/G
. It admits a left adjoint
\triv_{G}:\m_{R}\to\m^{G}_{\underline{R}}
, which is defined by
\triv_{G}(M)\simeq\underline{R}\otimes_{R}M
. One can check pointwisely that
\triv_{G}
preserves small limits. Then it has a left adjoint
(-)_{G}
from the adjoint functor theorem, which is addressed as
derived
G
-orbits functor
. The three functors can be extended to pro-coherent context as shown in
[
BCN21
, Example 2.51]
, and the right-left extension is compatible with filtration and grading.

Example 2.36
.

Let
\mathscr{O}^{\otimes}\to N(\mathcal{F}in_{*})
be an
(\infty,1)
-operad in the sense of
[
Lur17
, Definition 2.1.1.10]
, and
\mathscr{A}
be an
\mathscr{O}
-algebra object in
\mathcal{A}dd^{\text{coh,poly}}
. Then one obtains the following
\mathscr{O}
-monoidal functors

\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{(-)_{0}}\fil\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\wedge}}\fil^{\cpl}\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}\xrightarrow{\gr}\gr\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}.

For example, taking
\mathscr{A}
as
\vect^{\omega}_{R}
for
R
a coherent connective
\eoo
-ring spectrum and
\mathscr{O}^{\otimes}
as
N(\mathcal{F}in_{*})
, we obtain symmetric monoidal structure on different pro-coherent
R
-module categories.

This section is closed with a criterion for a given
V\in\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
being presented by an ordinary module. We use it to transfer our results in the pro-coherent context back to the usual context.

Proposition 2.37
.

Assume that
R
is an eventually coconnective coherent
\mathbb{E}_{1}
-ring spectrum, so that
\fil\m_{R}
can be regarded as a full subcategory of
\fil\QC^{\vee}_{R}
by the embedding
\iota
. Then a filtered pro-coherent
R
-module
V
lies in
\fil\m_{R}
if it is (filtration) complete and
\gr(V)
lies in
\gr\m_{R}

Proof.

Since
\iota
exhibits
\fil\m_{R}
as a right completion of
\fil\QC^{\vee}_{R}
, it suffices to prove that the natural morphism

\iota\nu V\simeq\colim_{i}\tau_{\geq i}V\to V
in
\fil\QC^{\vee}_{R}
is an equivalence.

First note that, using Milnor exact sequence,
\tau_{\geq i}V
is complete and eventually stable on each
\pi_{n}
varying
i
, which means that
\colim_{i}\tau_{\geq i}V
is also complete. Next, we prove that
\gr\iota\nu V\simeq\gr V
. For each
n
, there is a cofibre sequence of exact functors

F_{n+1}\to F_{n}\to\gr_{n}
, which are the right-left derived functors of their restrictions to
\fil\vect^{\omega}_{R}
. Here
F_{n}
commutes with
\tau_{\geq i}
. Applying
\colim_{i}
on

\tau_{\geq i}F_{n+1}(V)\to\tau_{\geq i}F_{n}(V)\to\gr_{n}(\tau_{\geq i}V)
, there is
\colim_{i}\gr_{n}(\tau_{\geq i}V)\simeq\iota\mu\gr_{n}V\simeq\gr_{n}V
. Meanwhile,
\colim_{i}\gr_{n}(\tau_{\geq i}V)\simeq\gr_{n}(\colim_{i}\tau_{\geq i}V)\simeq\gr_{n}(\iota\nu V)
.

∎

2.3
PD
\infty
-operads and filtered algebras

Operad theory is a general framework for studying various types of algebras with homotopy-coherent higher operations (e.g.
\eoo
-algebras or
L_{\infty}
-algebras) by describing all the
n
-ary operations and their relations
[
LV12
]
. The
\infty
-operads are a fully homotopy-coherent version of operads that parametrise algebras in general presentable symmetric monoidal
\infty
-categories, see
[
Bra17
, §4.1.2]
or
[
Hau22
]
. In this subsection, we briefly recall the basics of
\infty
-operads and then pass to their divided power analogues,
spectral
or
derived PD
\infty
-operads
(introduced in
[
BCN21
, §3]
), which permit us to define commutative algebras and the corresponding “Lie algebras” in pro-coherent modules. We emphasize the functoriality of the constructions to obtain the theory of filtered algebras via PD
\infty
-operads.

Construction 2.38

(Recollection of symmetric sequences)
.

Let
\text{B}\Sigma:=(\fin^{\simeq},\amalg)
be the category of finite sets and bijections, together with the symmetric monoidal structure given by disjoint unions. For any
\mathcal{E}\in\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{L})
, the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{E}
-valued symmetric sequences

\sseq(\mathcal{E}):=\text{Fun}(\text{B}\Sigma,\mathcal{E})

is endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes
by Day convolution.

We signify by
\mathbbb{1}\in\sseq(\mathcal{E})
the functor that sends
[1]\in B\Sigma
to the unit of
\mathcal{E}
and
[n]\in B\Sigma
to the initial object for all
n\neq 1
. The symmetric monoidal
\infty
-category
\sseq(\mathcal{E})
is freely generated by
\mathbbb{1}
in
\calg_{\mathcal{E}/}(\mathcal{P}r^{L})
. Thus, there is an equivalence
\mathrm{End}^{L,\otimes}_{\mathcal{E}/}(\sseq(\mathcal{E}))\xrightarrow{\simeq}\sseq(\mathcal{E})
obtained by evaluating at
\mathbbb{1}
. The
composition product

\circ
on
\sseq(\mathcal{E})
is defined as the opposite of functor composition.

There is another symmetric monoidal structure, denoted as
\levtimes
, on
\sseq(\mathcal{E})
defined
levelwise
. The composition product can be expressed by the following formula

M\circ N\simeq\mathop{\bigamalg}\limits_{r\in\mathbb{N}}(M(r)\otimes N^{\otimes r})_{h\Sigma_{r}}.

(2)

This formula shows that the inclusion
\mathcal{E}\subset\sseq(\mathcal{E})
into pure arity
0
equips
\mathcal{E}
with a left
(\sseq(\mathcal{E}),\circ)
-tensored structure. Additionally, those symmetric sequences
M
with
M(0)
being initial in
\mathcal{C}
form a monoidal full subcategory
\sseq^{\geq 1}(\mathcal{C})\subset\sseq(\mathcal{C})
with respect to
\circ
.

The assignments
(\sseq(-),\otimes)
and
(\sseq(-),\levtimes)
give rise to endofunctors of
\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{L})
, due to the universal property. Similarly, there is a functor
(\sseq(-),\circ):\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{L})\to\alg(\mathcal{P}r^{\Sigma})
.

Example 2.39
.

When
\mathcal{E}
equals to
\m_{R}
for certain
\eoo
-ring spectrum
R
, the
\infty
-category
\sseq_{R}:=\sseq(\m_{R})
consists of ordinary symmetric sequences of
R
-module spectra. By the functoriality of
\sseq(-)
, the symmetric monoidal functors
(-)_{0}
and
\gr
induces a sequence of functors

\sseq_{R}\to\sseq(\fil\m_{R})\to\sseq(\gr\m_{R}),

which is symmetric monoidal with respect to
\otimes
and
\levtimes
, and monoidal with respect to
\circ
. In particular, the
\infty
-categories
\fil_{I}\m_{R}
and
\gr_{I}\m_{R}
are endowed with natural left
(\sseq^{\geq 1}_{R},\circ)
-tensored structure, where
I=(-\infty,\infty)
,
(-\infty,-a]
or
[a,\infty)
for
a\geq 0
. The functors of modules
(-)_{0}
,
[-]_{0}
and
\gr_{0}
are enhanced into
(\sseq^{\geq 1}_{R},\circ)
-linear functors.

Similarly, the symmetric monoidal functors
F_{0}:\fil_{\geq 0}\m_{R}\to\m_{R}
and
\colim:\fil_{\leq 0}\m_{R}\to\m_{R}
are endowed with natural
(\sseq_{R},\circ)
-linear structures, and can be restricted to
(\sseq^{\geq 1}_{R},\circ)
-linear functors
F_{1}:\fil_{\geq 1}\m_{R}\to\m_{R}
and
\colim:\fil_{\leq-1}\m_{R}\to\m_{R}
.

Definition 2.40
.

An
\infty
-operad over
R
is an associative algebra object in
(\sseq_{R},\circ)
. The
\infty
-category of
\infty
-operads over
R
is written as
\op_{R}
. For a left
(\sseq_{R},\circ)
-tensored
\infty
-category
\mathcal{M}
and an
\infty
-operad
\mathcal{P}
,
a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra in
\mathcal{M}
is a left
\mathcal{P}
-module object in
\mathcal{M}
. Let
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{M})
be the
\infty
-category of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras in
\mathcal{M}
.

Example 2.41
.

The unit of
\levtimes
in
\sseq_{R}
is the
\infty
-operad
\eoo
of (unital) commutative algebra, where
\eoo(i)\simeq R
for each
i\in\mathbb{N}
. Similarly, the
\infty
-operad
\neoo
of non-unital commutative algebra is the same with
\eoo
at arity
\geq 1
, but
\neoo(0)\simeq 0
. Write
\alg_{\neoo}(-)
as
\calg^{nu}(-)
for short. There are adjunctions of
\neoo
-algebras following Example
2.39

\hbox to358.34pt{\vbox to24.48pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 179.16682pt\lower-12.55083pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

{{}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{{}}}{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{{}}{}{}}

where
\adic
is formally defined by the adjoint functor theorem. This diagram satisfies a pleasant property that
\gr\circ\adic(A)
is freely generated by
\gr_{1}\circ\adic(A)
for each
A\in\calg^{nu}_{R}(\m_{R})
.

Before diving into PD
\infty
-operads, it is worth recalling that commutative rings have two natural generalizations in derived geometry,
\eoo
-ring spectra and simplicial commutative rings. We start by reviewing the
spectral PD
\infty
-operads
that are suitable for
\eoo
-ring spectra.

Construction 2.42

(
\mathscr{A}
-pro-coherent symmetric sequences)
.

Let
\mathscr{A}
be an additive
\infty
-category. For any
r\geq 0
, there is a free-forgetful adjunction of the permutation group
\Sigma_{r}
-actions

(-)[\Sigma_{r}]:\mathscr{A}\rightleftarrows\text{Fun}(\Sigma_{r},\mathscr{A}):\text{forget}

such that
\text{forget}(A[\Sigma_{r}])\simeq A^{\oplus r}
if
r\geq 1
and
\text{forget}(A[\Sigma_{0}])\simeq A
. The full subcategory of
\text{Fun}(\Sigma_{r},\mathscr{A})
spanned by all the free
\Sigma_{r}
-modules is denoted as
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma_{r}]
.

Next, assume that
\mathscr{A}
is coherent and endowed with a symmetric monoidal structure
\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}
which preserves finite direct sums in each variable. The module category
\m_{\mathscr{A}}
is promoted into an object in
\calg(\mathcal{P}r^{L})
, which means that
\sseq_{\mathscr{A}}:=\sseq(\m_{\mathscr{A}})
makes sense. Moreover, the
\infty
-category
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma_{r}]
can be naturally embedded into
\sseq_{\mathscr{A}}
by regarding any object in
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma_{r}]
as a symmetric sequence concentrating at arity
r
.

Denote by
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]
the full subcategory
\oplus_{r}\mathscr{A}[\Sigma_{r}]\subset\sseq_{\mathscr{A}}
. It is coherent as an additive
\infty
-category, because the t-structure of
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
can be transferred to
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}[\Sigma_{r}]}
. The pro-coherent modules of
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]
are called
\mathscr{A}
-pro-coherent symmetric sequences
. Write
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]}
as
\sseq^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

Lemma 2.43
.

The full subcategory
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]\subset\sseq_{\mathscr{A}}
is closed under the tensor product of Day convolution
\otimes
, the levelwise tensor product
\levtimes
and the composition product
\circ
. Furthermore,
\otimes
and
\levtimes
are additive in each variable, and
\circ
is additive in the first variable and locally polynomial.

Proof.

Note that
V[\Sigma_{m}]\otimes U[\Sigma_{n}]\simeq(V\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}U)[\Sigma_{m+n}]
. Thus
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]
is closed under
\otimes
. At the same time, if
m=n
,
V[\Sigma_{n}]\levtimes U[\Sigma_{n}]
is a
n
copies of
(V\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}U)[\Sigma_{n}]
, so
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]
is also closed under
\levtimes
. The closedness of
\mathscr{A}[\Sigma]
under
\circ
is now implied by the formula (1).

The tensor products
\otimes
and
\levtimes
are additive in each variable by their nature. The formula (1) shows that
\circ
is additive in the first variable. Finally,
\circ
is locally polynomial by

M\circ N\simeq\colim_{n}(\mathop{\amalg}_{0\leq r\leq n}(M(r)\otimes_{\mathscr{A}}N^{\otimes r})_{\Sigma_{r}}).

∎

Let
\mathcal{A}dd^{\text{coh},\oplus}
be the
\infty
-category of coherent additive
\infty
-categories with additive functors.

Proposition 2.44
.

Construction
2.42
gives rise to a functor
\sseq^{\vee}_{-}:\calg(\mathcal{A}dd^{\text{coh},\oplus})\to\mathcal{P}r^{\st}
, which is functorially endowed with: two closed symmetric monoidal structures
\otimes
and
\levtimes
, and a monoidal structure
\circ
preserving sifted colimits in each variable and small colimits in the first variable.

Additionally, there is a lax and oplax monoidal structure on
\levtimes:\sseq^{\vee}_{-}\times\sseq^{\vee}_{-}\to\sseq^{\vee}_{-}
with respect to
\circ
.

Proof.

The symmetric sequence categories
\sseq_{-}\simeq\m_{-[\Sigma]}
are equiped with functorial operations from Construction
2.38
. Applying Theorem
2.32
to them, the right-left extension produces the desired
\otimes
and
\circ
, and a non-unital symmetric monoidal structure
\levtimes
. Besides, the transformation
\iota:\sseq_{-}\to\sseq^{\vee}_{-}
sends
\eoo
to a
\levtimes
-unit in
\sseq_{-}
. The lax and oplax structures on
\levtimes
are given by Proposition 3.9 in
[
BCN21
]
, whose proof also works here without extra effort.
∎

The functoriality of
\sseq^{\vee}_{-}
gives rise to natural actions of
\sseq^{\vee}_{R}
on filtered and graded modules.

Notation 2.45
.

Let
R
be a coherent connective
\eoo
-algebra. Define the
\infty
-category of
pro-coherent symmetric sequences over
R
as
\sseq^{\vee}_{R}:=\sseq^{\vee}_{\vect^{\omega}_{R}}
, which agrees with the definition in
[
BCN21
]
. Similarly, we call
\sseq_{\fil,R}^{\vee}:=\sseq^{\vee}_{\sfil\vect^{\omega}_{R}}
(resp.
\sseq_{\gr,R}^{\vee}:=\sseq^{\vee}_{\sgr\vect^{\omega}_{R}}
) the
\infty
-category of
filtered (resp. graded) pro-coherent symmetric sequences over
R
. Besides, let
\sseq^{\vee,\geq 1}_{R}\subset\sseq^{\vee}_{R}
denote the full subcategory spanned by the pro-coherent symmetric sequences without nullary operations.

Construction 2.46
.

Following Example
2.39
, the right-left derived functors

\sseq^{\vee}_{R}\to\sseq_{\fil,R}^{\vee}\to\sseq_{\gr,R}^{\vee}

are monoidal with respect to
\otimes
,
\levtimes
and then
\circ
. This exhibits
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
,
\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
and
\gr_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
as left
(\sseq^{\vee,\geq 1}_{R},\circ)
-tensored
\infty
-categories, where
I=(-\infty,\infty)
,
(-\infty,-a]
or
[a,\infty)
for
a\geq 0
. Moreover, we have the
\sseq^{\vee,\geq 1}_{R}
-linear functors
(-)_{0}
,
[-]_{0}
,
\gr
and

F_{1}:\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\QC^{\vee}_{R},\ \ \ \ \colim:\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\QC^{\vee}_{R}.

Definition 2.47
.

A
spectral PD
\infty
-(co)operad over
R
is an (co)associative (co)algebra object in
(\sseq^{\vee}_{R},\circ)
. The
\infty
-category of PD
\infty
-(co)operads over
R
is
\op^{\pd}_{R}
(resp.
co\op^{\pd}_{R}
).

Let
\mathcal{M}
be a left
(\sseq^{\vee}_{R},\circ)
-tensored
\infty
-category. For a PD
\infty
-(co)operad
\mathcal{P}
(
\mathcal{Q}
), the
\infty
-category
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{M})
(
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\mathcal{M})
) of
\mathcal{P}
-algebras (
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras) in
\mathcal{M}
is defined as the
\infty
-category of left
\mathcal{P}
-modules (resp. left
\mathcal{Q}
-comodules) objects in
\mathcal{M}
.

Example 2.48
.

The functor
\iota
(
2.25
) sends
\neoo
to a PD
\infty
-operad (still denoted as
\neoo
). It induces a variant of Example
2.41

Definition 2.49
.

Let
R
be a simplicial commutative ring and
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]:=\oplus_{r\geq 0}R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]
(Definition
2.22
) be the sum additive
\infty
-category. The
\infty
-category of derived symmetric sequences
is defined as

\sseq^{\gen}_{\underline{R}}:=\m_{R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]}\simeq\prod_{r\geq 0}\m^{\Sigma_{r}}_{\underline{R}}.

Similarly, define the
\infty
-category of pro-coherent derived symmetric sequences
as

\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{\underline{R}}:=\QC^{\vee}_{R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]}
, while the
\infty
-categories of
filtered or graded
pro-coherent derived symmetric sequences refer to

\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{\fil,\underline{R}}:=\QC^{\vee}_{\sfil R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]}\simeq\fil\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{\underline{R}},\ \ \ \ \sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{\gr,\underline{R}}:=\QC^{\vee}_{\sgr R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]}\simeq\gr\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{\underline{R}}.

The following technical lemma is useful for constructing operations on
\sseq^{\gen}_{R}
and its variants.

Lemma 2.50
.
3
3

3

We give an alternative proof fixing
[
BCN21
, Lemma 3.59]
with the help of Nuiten.

The assignment
R\mapsto R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
determines a sifted-colimit-preserving functor
\mathrm{SCR}\to\mathcal{A}dd^{\oplus}
.

Proof.

It suffices to show that at each arity
r
, the assignment
R\mapsto R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]
can be enhanced into a functor
f_{r}:\mathrm{SCR}\to\mathcal{A}dd^{\oplus}
preserving sifted colimits. Fix
r
. Let
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
be the set of isomorphism classes of finite
\Sigma_{r}
-sets. Observe that
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]
has
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
as the set of objects for each
R\in\mathrm{SCR}
, and recall that the category
\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}}_{\Delta}
of small simplicial categories with fixed objects set
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
admits a simplicial model structure from
[
DK80
, 7.2]
. Thus the assignment
R\mapsto R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]
gives rise to a functor
F_{1}:\mathrm{SCR}\to N_{\Delta}(\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}}_{\Delta})
of
\infty
-categories, where
N_{\Delta}(-)
is taking the simplicial nerve.

The functor
F_{1}
is actually sifted-colimit-preserving. Indeed, recall that for each
R\in\mathrm{SCR}
and
X,Y
finite
\Sigma_{r}
-sets, the mapping space
\map_{R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]}(\underline{R}\otimes\infsus X,\underline{R}\otimes\infsus Y)\simeq R^{(X\times Y)/\Sigma_{r}}
preserves sifted colimits with respect to
R
. At the same time, the
\infty
-category
N_{\Delta}(\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}}_{\Delta})
is naturally equivalent with
\alg_{\mathfrak{S}_{r}^{cat}}(\mathcal{S})
, where
\mathfrak{S}_{r}^{cat}
is the
\mathfrak{S}_{r}\times\mathfrak{S}_{r}
-colored
(\infty,1)
-operad of catenary composition of ordered pairings in
\mathfrak{S}_{r}

[
GH15
, 2.1]
. Therefore, it suffices to apply
[
Lur17
, Proposition 3.2.3.1]
.

Write
\mathcal{C}at_{\Delta}
Bergner’s model category of small simplicial categories
[
Ber07
, Theorem 1.1]
, and
\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}/}_{\Delta}
the coslice model category of small simplicial categories receiving a functor from
\mathfrak{S}_{r}
regarded as a discrete category. There is a left Quillen functor
\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}}_{\Delta}\to\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}/}_{\Delta}
. As a result, the functor

F_{2}:\mathrm{SCR}\xrightarrow{F_{1}}N_{\Delta}(\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}}_{\Delta})\to N(\mathcal{C}at^{\mathfrak{S}_{r}/}_{\Delta})\simeq(\mathcal{C}at_{\infty})_{N(\mathfrak{S}_{r})/}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

is sifted-colimit-preserving by Proposition 4.4.2.9 in
[
Lur09
]
.

It only remains to prove that the inclusion
i:\mathcal{A}dd^{\oplus}\to\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}
creates sifted colimits, since
F_{2}
is equivalent with
i\circ f_{r}
. Write
\mathcal{C}at^{\pi}_{\infty}
the (not full) subcategory of small
\infty
-categories admitting finite products with finite-product-preserving functors. The inclusion
i
factors through
\mathcal{C}at^{\pi}_{\infty}
,

i:\mathcal{A}dd\xrightarrow{j^{\prime}}\mathcal{C}at^{\pi}_{\infty}\xrightarrow{j^{\prime\prime}}\mathcal{C}at_{\infty}

where
j^{\prime}
is a colocalization
[
GGN16
, Corollary 2.10]
,
j^{\prime\prime}
is the inclusion. The question is reduced to showing that
j^{\prime\prime}
creates sifted colimits.

Take an arbitrary sifted diagram
\phi:K\to\mathcal{C}at^{\pi}_{\infty}
. The existence of finite products in each term can be internally reformulated as the following map of
K
-diagrams

\phi\rightleftarrows*_{K},\ \ \ \ \Delta:\phi\rightleftarrows\phi\times\phi:-\times-,

(where
*_{K}
is the singleton valued constant diagram) together with the unit and counit transformations which satisfy the triangle identities, see
[
RV16
, §1]
. In other words, termwise having finite products is encoded by a finite diagram in
\mathcal{C}at^{K}_{\infty}
compatible with sifted colimits. Thus the colimit of
j^{\prime\prime}\circ\phi
has a natural lifting in
\mathcal{C}at^{\pi}_{\infty}
, which is nothing but
\colim_{K}\phi
.

∎

Construction 2.51

(Monoidal structures on
\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{R}
)
.

When
R
is a discrete commutative ring,
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
is a full subcategory of
\sseq(\m^{\heartsuit}_{R})\subset\sseq_{R}
. Then one can check that
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
is closed under the operations
\otimes
,
\levtimes
and
\circ
. The composition product
\circ
is expressed as

X\circ Y\simeq\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}(X(r)\otimes_{R}Y^{\otimes r})_{\Sigma_{r}},

where
(-)_{\Sigma_{r}}
is taking ordinary orbits. At each arity
r
, taking
R
-linear dual determines a contravariant autoequivalence
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]\simeq R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma_{r}}]^{op}
. It induces another monoidal structure as follows

X\bcirc Y:=(X^{\vee}\circ Y^{\vee})^{\vee}\simeq\bigoplus_{r\geq 0}(X(r)\otimes_{R}Y^{\otimes r})^{\Sigma_{r}},

where
(-)^{\Sigma_{r}}
is taking the usual fixed points. For each morphism
R\to S
, the transition functor
S\otimes_{R}(-):R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]\to S[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
is compatible with all the operations
\otimes
,
\levtimes
,
\circ
and
\bcirc
. Recall that
\mathrm{SCR}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\Sigma}(\mathrm{Poly})
where
\mathrm{Poly}
is the 1-category of finitely generated polynomial rings over
\mathbb{Z}
. Thus, using Lemma
2.50
, the operations can be defined on
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
for any
R\in\mathrm{SCR}
by the same manipulation on bases. Moreover, they can be extended to
\sfil R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
and
\sgr R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
as well.

Applying Theorem
2.32
to a coherent simplicial commutative ring
R
, we encounter an abundance of monoidal structures
\otimes
,
\levtimes
,
\circ
and
\bcirc
on the following
\infty
-categories,

\sseq^{\gen}_{R}\xrightarrow{\iota}\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{R}\xrightarrow{(-)_{0}}\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{R,\fil}\xrightarrow{\gr}\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{R,\gr}.

(3)

Besides, these functors are symmetric monoidal with respect to
\otimes
and
\levtimes
and monoidal with respect to
\circ
and
\bcirc
. In particular, the following functors of pro-coherent modules are equiped with both
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{R},\circ)
and
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{R},\bcirc)
linear structures:

(-)_{0}:\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\fil\QC^{\vee}_{R}
,
[-]_{0}:\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\gr\QC^{\vee}_{R}
,
\gr:\fil\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\gr\QC^{\vee}_{R}
,
F_{1}:\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\QC^{\vee}_{R}
and
\colim:\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\QC^{\vee}_{R}
.

Remark 2.52
.

The levelwise product
\levtimes
admits a unit
\com
in
\sseq^{\gen}_{R}
which is given by
\com(r)\simeq\underline{R}
for every
r\in\mathbb{N}
. Then
\com
is sent to
\levtimes
-units in different contexts by line (
3
). In each context, the operation
\levtimes
is both lax and oplax with respect to
\circ
and
\bcirc
.

Definition 2.53
.

For a simplicial commutative ring
R
, the
\infty
-category of derived
\infty
-operads is defined as
\alg\big(\sseq^{\gen}_{R},\circ\big)
. Similarly, restricted derived
\infty
-operads form an
\infty
-category
\alg\big(\sseq^{\gen}_{R},\bcirc\big)
.

Example 2.54
.

The
\levtimes
-unit object
\com
admits a natural derived
\infty
-operad structure, whose algebras in
\m_{R}
are nothing but
derived algebras
over
R
in the sense of
[
Rak20
, §4.2]
. When
\com
is regarded as a restricted operad, it parametrises
derived divided power algebras
.

The
\infty
-category of
R
-derived algebras (in ordinary, filtered or graded modules) admits a cocartesian symmetric monoidal structure, which is given by
\otimes_{R}
on the level of underlying modules,
[
Rak20
, Proposition 4.2.27]
. The same claim also applies to pro-coherent derived algebras.

Definition 2.55
.

Let
R
be a coherent simplicial commutative ring. The
\infty
-category
\op^{\gen,\vee}_{R}
(
co\op^{\gen,\vee}_{R}
) of
derived pro-coherent
\infty
-(co)operads
is defined as the
\infty
-category of the (co)associative (co)algebras in
(\sseq^{\vee}_{R},\circ)
. Parallelly, define the
\infty
-category
\op^{\gen,\pd}_{R}
(
co\op^{\gen,\pd}_{R}
) of
derived PD
\infty
-(co)operads
as the
\infty
-category of the (co)associative (co)algebras in
(\sseq^{\vee}_{R},\bcirc)
.

3
Filtered PD Koszul duality

Recall that
derived partition Lie algebras
are sophisticated algebraic objects designed to classify derived formal moduli problems in general characteristics
[
BM19
, §4.2, §5.2]
. In this section, we present a filtered PD Koszul duality for derived partition Lie algebras in two steps: First, we use a categorical bar-cobar adjunction to obtain the adjunction between filtered non-unital derived algebras and filtered
\br(\com^{nu})
-coalgebras (§
3.1
). Then we show that taking
R
-linear dual induces an adjunction between filtered
\br(\com^{nu})
-coalgebras and filtered
\br(\com^{nu})^{\vee}
-algebras (§
3.2
), namely
filtered derived partition Lie algebras
. It turns out that, the
dually almost perfect
derived partition Lie algebras (with the constant filtrations) are fully faithfully embedded into the
\infty
-category of
filtered non-unital derived algebras (Corollary
3.33
).

Our method in this section is very formal so that it applies to every (spectral PD, derived pro-coherent or derived PD)
\infty
-operad satisfying condition (
\dagger
) in Construction
3.20
. As a by-product, we also classify
dually almost perfect
spectral partition Lie algebras using filtered
\neoo
-algebras (Corollary
3.32
).

3.1
Refined bar-cobar adjunction for PD
\infty
-operads

\mathcal{C}
be a pointed monoidal
\infty
-category and
\mathcal{M}
be a left
\mathcal{C}
-tensored
\infty
-category. Suppose that both
\mathcal{C}
and
\mathcal{M}
have all geometric realizations and totalizations. A categorical bar-cobar construction established in
[
Lur17
, §5.2.2]
and refined in
[
BCN21
, §3.4]
produces a commutative diagram
