where
\br(A,M)
is given by
(1\otimes_{A}1,1\otimes_{A}M)
for a left module object
(A,M)\in\mathrm{LMod}(\mathcal{M})
. By the virtue of its functoriality, we can establish a connection between filtered algebras and coalgebras. In this section, we use another notion of higher operads introduced in
[
Lur17
, §2]
and refer them as
(\infty,1)
-operads to avoid ambiguity.

Construction 3.1

(Following Construction
2.46
,
2.51
)
.

Let
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
be the
(\infty,1)
-operad in
[
Lur17
, Definition 4.2.1.7]
, whose categorical algebra
(\mathcal{C},\mathcal{M})
consists of a monoidal
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}
and a left
\mathcal{C}
-tensored
\infty
-category
\mathcal{M}
. The underlying category of
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
is a discrete set
\{\mathfrak{a},\mathfrak{m}\}
.

In this section, suppose that
R
is a coherent connective
\eoo
-ring spectrum, and
\mathcal{C}
is a pointed monoidal
\infty
-category that is equivalent to either
(\sseq^{\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\circ)
,
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{\underline{R},1//1},\circ)
or
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{\underline{R},1//1},\bcirc)
(
R
is further assumed to be a simplicial commutative ring in the latter two cases). The action of
\mathcal{C}
on
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
can be encoded by a cocartesian fibration of
(\infty,1)
-operads
\mathscr{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
such that
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{a}}\simeq\mathcal{C}
and
\mathscr{C}_{\mathfrak{m}}\simeq\QC^{\vee}_{R}

The
\infty
-category of left module objects
\mathrm{LMod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
of
\mathscr{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
is the
\infty
-category of
(\infty,1)
-operadic sections. There is a natural projection
\pi:\mathrm{LMod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})\to\alg(\mathcal{C})
whose fibre over
{\mathcal{P}}
is
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
, where
\mathcal{P}
is an augmented PD (derived pro-coherent, derived PD)
\infty
-operad.

The cocartesian fibrations of
(\infty,1)
-operads
(\fil_{I}\mathscr{C})^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes},(\gr_{I}\mathscr{C})^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
are similarly defined for the actions of
\mathcal{C}
on
\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
and
\gr_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
respectively. In the same way, the actions of
\mathcal{C}^{op}
are encoded by
(\mathscr{C}^{op})^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
. The
\infty
-category of left comodule objects are defined as
\mathrm{LComod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R}):=\mathrm{LMod}(QC^{\vee,op}_{R})^{op}
, while
\mathrm{LComod}(\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
and
\mathrm{LComod}(\gr_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
are defined using the same method. The comodule categories admit projections to
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
.

Theorem 3.2
.

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LMod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LMod}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alg(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{co\alg(\mathcal{C})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LMod}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\br}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cobr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\br}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cobr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\br}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cobr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\br}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cobr}

where the rows are adjunctions. Furthermore, the diagram satisfies the following properties:

(1)

The
\br
s preserve cocartesian edges relative to the projections to the
\infty
-categories of (co)algebras.

(2)

The front square exhibits
presentable fibrations
over
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
, while the back square exhibits presentable fibrations over
\alg(\mathcal{C})
(in the sense of
[
Lur09
, Definition 5.5.3.2]
).

(3)

The
F_{1}
s and
\gr
s are
morphisms of presentable fibrations
, which means that they preserve both cartesian and cocartesian edges.

The proof of this theorem is broken into small pieces as follows to make it more readable. We first prove (2) and (3), then return to the commutativity and (1).

Remark 3.3

(Back square)
.

In the context of Construction
3.1
, it is well known that the algebra category of each (generalised)
\infty
-operad is presentable. Here we briefly recall how to prove it. Since
\circ
is compatible with sifted colimits, Lemma 3.2.3.4 in
[
Lur17
]
shows that each vertex in the back square is an accessible
\infty
-category; in particular, for each
\mathcal{P}\in\alg(\mathcal{C})
, the algebra category
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
is accessible
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.6.6]
. The pushouts of
B\leftarrow A\rightarrow C
in
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
can be constructed explicitly using bar resolutions by free algebras. The same reasoning works for filtered and graded algebras. For each morphism
\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}
in
\alg(\mathcal{C})
, the cocartesian edges over it are given by
\mathcal{Q}\otimes_{\mathcal{P}}-
. Thus the back square consists of presentable fibrations over
\alg(\mathcal{C})
. Additionally, the functors
F_{1}
and
\gr
are morphisms of presentable fibrations, since they come from small-colimit-preserving
\mathcal{C}
-linear functors.

We need some categorical preparation to treat the front square.

Lemma 3.4
.

Let
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{O}^{\otimes}
be a cocartesian fibration of
(\infty,1)
-operads, where
\mathcal{O}^{\otimes}
is small as a simplicial set. The
\infty
-category of operadic sections
\alg_{/\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{M})
is
op-accessible
(i.e. its opposite is accessible) if the following conditions are satisfied:

•

for each
X\in\mathcal{O}^{\otimes}
, the fibre
\mathcal{M}_{X}
is op-accessible;

•

for each
f:X\to Y
in
\mathcal{O}^{\otimes}
, the induced functor
f_{!}:\mathcal{M}_{X}\to\mathcal{M}_{Y}
is filtered-limit-preserving.

Proof.

The thrust of proof is
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.7.11]
. Since the functor
(-)^{op}
of taking opposite
\infty
-category is an autoequivalence of
\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
, we can take
\mathscr{C}
as the (not full) subcategory
opAcc
of
\widehat{\mathcal{C}at}_{\infty}
of op-accessible
\infty
-categories with filtered-limit-preserving functors, which satisfies the conditions of
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.7.11]
by
[
Lur09
, Remark 5.4.7.13]
. Consider the categorical fibration
\mathcal{M}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{O}^{\otimes}
and let
\mathcal{E}
be the set of inert morphisms in
\mathcal{O}
. The operadic sections
\alg_{/\mathcal{O}}(\mathcal{M})
belongs to
opAcc
by
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.7.11]
.
∎

Proposition 3.5
.

Following the notation of Construction
3.1
, the
\infty
-categories
\mathrm{LComod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
,
\mathrm{LComod}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
,
\mathrm{LComod}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
and
{co\alg(\mathcal{C})}
are all presentable.

Proof.

Unpacking the definition,
\mathrm{LComod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
is
\alg_{/\mathcal{LM}}(\mathscr{C}^{op})^{op}
, which is accessible according to Lemma
3.4
. Furthermore,
\alg_{/\mathcal{LM}}(\mathscr{C}^{op})
admits small limits by
[
Lur17
, Corollary 3.2.2.5]
and the fact that
\mathcal{C}
and
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
are presentable. Therefore,
\mathrm{LComod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
is presentable. The proof is the same for the other three
\infty
-categories.
∎

Proposition 3.6
.

For each (spectral PD, derived pro-coherent or derived PD)
\infty
-cooperad
\mathcal{Q}
, the
\infty
-categories of
\mathcal{Q}
-coalgebras
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
,
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
and
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
are presentable.

Proof.

Consider the homotopy pullback of
\infty
-categories

\hbox to190.7pt{\vbox to53.02pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 95.34785pt\lower-26.50893pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}
{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-95.34785pt}{-20.3492pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -131.93 -28.16)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{}}}{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
{
{}{}{}}{}{
{}{}{}}

where
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
is accessible by
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.6.6]
and Proposition
3.5
. Moreover,
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
admits all small colimits, because the forgetful functor
co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})\to\QC^{\vee}_{R}
creates small colimits. The filtered and graded are proved mutatis mutandis.
∎

Proposition 3.7

(Front square)
.

The left comodule categories are presentable fibrations over
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
, while the functors
F_{1}
and
\gr
are morphisms of presentable fibrations

\hbox to357.03pt{\vbox to54.97pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 178.51376pt\lower-26.67747pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{

\lxSVG@closescope {

{{{{{}}{

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Proof.

The projection
\pi:\mathrm{LComod}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})\to co\alg(\mathcal{C})
is a cocartesian fibration such that, for each morphism
p:\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{Q}
in
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
, the cocartesian edges over
p
are given by the forgetful functor of left comodules. Note that forgetful functors here are small-colimit-preserving and each fibre is presentable, so the right adjoints of forgetful functors produce abundant locally cartesian edges by the adjoint functor theorem. Thus
\pi
is in fact a presentable fibration. The same proof also works for the filtered and graded cases. The functor
F_{1}
here commutes with forgetful functors along arrows in
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
, i.e. preserves the cocartesian edges. At the same time, the corresponding
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
-monoidal functor of the underlying module categories admits an oplax left adjoint
[
Lur17
, Corollary 7.3.2.7]
, which means that
F_{1}
has a left adjoint relative to
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
commuting with the forgetful functors. Taking the right adjoints,
F_{1}
commutes with the right adjoints of forgetful functors, i.e. preserves cartesian edges. The proof for
\gr
is by mutatis mutandis.

∎

Proof of Theorem
3.2
.

Apply Theorem 3.26 in
[
BCN21
]
to the cocartesian fibrations of
(\infty,1)
-operads in Construction
3.1
:
\mathscr{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
,
\fil_{\geq 1}\mathscr{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
,
\gr_{\geq 1}\mathscr{C}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
and their opposite counterparts. This establishes everything that does not mention
F_{1}
and
\gr
. The assertions (2) and (3) are verified in Remark
3.3
and Propostion
3.7
.

It only remains to demonstrate that
F_{1}
and
\gr
commute with
\br
and
\cobr
. Expanding the categorical construction in
[
BCN21
, §3.4]
, a
\mathcal{C}
-linear functor
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
gives rise to a morphism of pairings of
\infty
-categories
\tilde{f}:\mathrm{LMod}(\text{Tw}(\mathcal{M}))\to\mathrm{LMod}(\text{Tw}(\mathcal{N}))
. For each
(A,M)\in\mathrm{LMod}(\mathcal{M})
, the left universal object
u
lying over it can be understood as
(A,M)\to\br(A,M)=:(C,N)
, and its image
\tilde{f}(u)
can be written as an arrow
(A,fM)\to(C,fN)
receiving a unique morphism from the left universal object
(A,fM)\to\br(A,fM)=:(C^{\prime},N^{\prime})
. According to
[
Lur17
, Theorem 5.2.2.17]
, both
C
and
C^{\prime}
are calculated by
|\br_{\bullet}(1,A,1)|
in
\mathcal{C}
, while the underlying morphism of
fN\to N^{\prime}
in
\mathcal{N}
is

f|\br_{\bullet}(1,A,M)|\to|\br_{\bullet}(1,A,fM)|
. If
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
is assumed to be sifted-colimit-preserving, then
\tilde{f}(u)
is left universal as well, which means that
f
commutes with
\br

[
Lur17
, Proposition 5.2.1.17]
. Dually, if
f:\mathcal{M}\to\mathcal{N}
preserves sifted limits, we have that
f
commutes with
\cobr
. Finally, recall that
F_{1}:\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\QC^{\vee}_{R}
and
\gr:\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
preserve all small limits and colimits, and they are
\mathcal{C}
-linear.
∎

Consider
\mathcal{P}\in\alg(\mathcal{C})
and choose
\mathcal{M}
among
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
,
\fil\QC^{\vee}_{R}
and
\gr\QC^{\vee}_{R}
. The functor

\br:\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{M})\to co\alg_{\br(\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{M})

admits a right adjoint, which is given by the following composition

co\alg_{\br(\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{M})\xrightarrow{\cobr}\alg_{\cobr\circ\br(\mathcal{P})}(\mathcal{M})\xrightarrow{\phi}\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\mathcal{M}),

where
\phi
is the forgetful functor along
\mathcal{P}\to\cobr\circ\br(\mathcal{P})
. Here,
\phi
can be omitted for
Koszul (generalized)
\infty
-operads
.

Definition 3.8
.

An augmented (spectral PD, pro-coherent derived or derived PD)
\infty
-operad
\mathcal{P}
is said to be
Koszul
if the unit morphism

\mathcal{P}\to\cobr\circ\br(\mathcal{P})
is an equivalence.

The following result is proven for spectra by Ching in
[
Chi12
, Theorem 2.15]
, cf. also
[
BH
]
for the general case:

Proposition 3.9
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{P}\in\alg(\mathcal{C})
is
reduced
, i.e. it satisfies the following conditions:

(1) there is no nullary operation, i.e.
\mathcal{P}(0)\simeq 0
;

(2) the unit map induces a natural equivalence
R\simeq\mathcal{P}(1)
at arity 1;

then
\mathcal{P}
is Koszul.

Example 3.10
.

Let
R
be a connective
\eoo
-ring spectrum. The
\infty
-operad
\neoo
of augmented commutative
R
-algebra is Koszul as a spectral PD
\infty
-operad. When
R
is further a simplicial commutative ring,
\com^{nu}
is Koszul both as a derived pro-coherent
\infty
-operad and a derived PD
\infty
-operad.

3.2
Refined duality for PD
\infty
-(co)operads

Now we show that taking
R
-linear dual induces an adjunction between coalgebras and algebras that respects the filtrations.

Notation 3.11
.

If
R
is a coherent connective
\eoo
-ring spectrum, choose
\mathscr{A}
from
\vect^{\omega}_{R}[\Sigma]
,
\sfil\vect^{\omega}_{R}[\Sigma]
or
\sgr\vect^{\omega}_{R}[\Sigma]
. The
R
-linear dual functor of spectral pro-coherent symmetric sequences
is given by the internal hom-object
(-)^{\vee}:=\hom_{(\QC_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee},\levtimes)}(-,\eoo)
.

When
R
is a coherent simplicial commutative ring, let
\mathscr{B}
be one of
R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
,
\sfil R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
or
\sgr R[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
. The
R
-linear dual functor of derived pro-coherent symmetric sequences
is defined as
(-)^{\vee}:=\hom_{(\QC_{\mathscr{B}}^{\vee},\levtimes)}(-,\com)
.

Proposition 3.12

(Lax monoidal structure of
(-)^{\vee}
)
.

Following Notation
3.11
, we have:

(1) The
R
-linear dual functor
(-)^{\vee}:\QC_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee,op}\to\QC_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee}
is lax monoidal with respect to
\circ
. Moreover, it restricts to a contravariant monoidal equivalence

\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{op}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
.

(2) The derived
R
-linear dual functor gives rise to lax monoidal functors

(-)^{\vee}:(\QC^{\vee,op}_{\mathscr{B}},\circ)\to(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}},\bcirc),\ \ \ \ (-)^{\vee}:(\QC^{\vee,op}_{\mathscr{B}},\bcirc)\to(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}},\circ)

which form a contravariant adjunction. Furthermore, this adjunction restricts to the monoidal contravariant equivalences

(\aperf_{\mathscr{B}}^{op},\circ)\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}},\bcirc)
and
(\aperf_{\mathscr{B}}^{op},\bcirc)\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\aperf^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}},\circ)
.

Proof.

The lax monoidal structure in (1) is established by Lemma
3.13
below. In the meanwhile, each
R[\Sigma_{r}]
is dualizable and has itself as a dual object as a finite free
R
-module. Thus Proposition
2.33
implies an equivalence

\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee,op}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}
. This equivalence is monoidal with respect to
\circ
, because it is given by left Kan extension and
\circ
commutes with sifted colimits.

For (2), we start with the monoidal equivalence
F^{op}:=(-)^{\vee}:(\mathscr{B}^{op},\circ)\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\mathscr{B},\bcirc)
. Left Kan extension produces a monoidal equivalence
F^{R,op}:(\aperf_{\mathscr{B}}^{\vee,op},\circ)\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\aperf_{\mathscr{B}},\bcirc)
, since
\bcirc
commutes with sifted colimits. Then right Kan extension produces a lax monoidal right adjoint
F^{RL,op}:(-)^{\vee}:(\QC^{\vee,op}_{\mathscr{B}},\circ)\to(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{B}},\bcirc)

[
BCN21
, Lemma 2.58]
. The other direction is done in the same way.

∎

Lemma 3.13
.

cf.
[
BCN21
, Proposition 3.47]

Let
\mathcal{E}
be a presentable
\infty
-category endowed with two monoidal structures
\otimes
and
\boxtimes
, while
\boxtimes
is closed and symmetric. Suppose that
\mathcal{E}\times\mathcal{E}\xrightarrow{\boxtimes}\mathcal{E}
is oplax with respect to
\otimes
, and that the unit
1_{\boxtimes}
of
\boxtimes
is an associative algebra in
(\mathcal{E},\otimes)
. Then

(-)^{\vee}:=(\hom_{(\mathcal{E},\boxtimes)}(-,1_{\boxtimes}):\mathcal{E}^{op}\to\mathcal{E})
is lax monoidal with respect to
\otimes
.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{E}
is essentially small, Yoneda embedding induces a monoidal functor
j:\mathcal{E}\hookrightarrow\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E}^{op},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{large}})
with respect to
\otimes
and Day convolution tensor product. The composite
j\circ(-)^{\vee}
corresponding to

\mathcal{E}^{op}\times\mathcal{E}^{op}\xrightarrow{\boxtimes}\mathcal{E}^{op}\xrightarrow{\map(-,1_{\boxtimes})}\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{large}}

by the canonical equivalence
\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E}^{op},\mathrm{Fun}(\mathcal{E}^{op},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{large}}))\simeq\text{Fun}(\mathcal{E}^{op}\times\mathcal{E}^{op},\mathcal{S}_{\mathrm{large}})
. Here
\map(-,1_{\boxtimes})
is lax monoidal, since
j
is monoidal and
1_{\boxtimes}
is an
\otimes
-algebra. Thus
\map(-\boxtimes-,1_{\boxtimes})
is a lax monoidal functor. The construction of Day convolution
[
Lur17
, §2.2.6]
provides a commutative diagram

∎

We can regard
R
as a filtered (graded) module by the embedding
(-)_{0}
(
[-]_{0}
) into weight
0
. The
R
-linear dual of a filtered module
M
is determined by

F_{n}(M^{\vee})\simeq(\mathop{\colim}_{k\to-\infty}F_{k}M/F_{-n+1}M)^{\vee},

(4)

which is
always
complete. Taking
R
-linear dual of graded modules sends
N_{\star}
to
N^{\vee}_{-\star}
. These formulae show that there is a commuting diagram in
\mathcal{A}dd^{\text{coh,poly}}

(5)

Notation 3.14
.

Adopt the same notation as Construction
3.1
. When
\mathcal{C}=(\sseq^{\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\circ)
,
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\circ)
or
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\bcirc)
, set
\mathcal{C}_{d}
as
(\sseq^{\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\circ)
,
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\bcirc)
or
(\sseq^{\gen,\vee,\geq 1}_{R,1//1},\circ)
respectively.
The
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
-monoidal
\infty
-categories that encode the action of
\mathcal{C}_{d}
on
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
,
\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
or
\gr_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
are denoted as
\mathscr{C}^{\otimes}_{d}
,
(\fil_{\leq-1}\mathscr{C}_{d})^{\otimes}
or
(\gr_{\leq-1}\mathscr{C}_{d})^{\otimes}
respectively.

Proposition 3.15
.

There exists a commutative diagram of
(\infty,1)
-operads over
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\mathscr{C}^{op})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathscr{C}_{d}^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\fil_{\geq 1}\mathscr{C}^{op})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\fil_{\leq-1}\mathscr{C}_{d})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\gr_{\geq 1}\mathscr{C}^{op})^{\otimes}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(\gr_{\leq-1}\mathscr{C}_{d})^{\otimes}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\const}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee}}

(6)

such that:

(1) on the fibres over
\mathfrak{a}
, the vertical arrows are identities of
\mathcal{C}
or
\mathcal{C}_{d}
, the horizontal arrows are equivalent to the R-linear dual functor
\mathcal{C}^{op}\xrightarrow{(-)^{\vee}}\mathcal{C}_{d}
defined in Notation
3.11
;

(2) on the fibres over
\mathfrak{m}
, the diagram is given by right-left derived functors of (
5
).

Proof.

The existence of the bottom vertical arrows is clear, since
\gr:\fil_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\gr_{I}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
is both
\mathcal{C}
-linear and
\mathcal{C}_{d}
-linear. Now observe that
(-)_{1}
admits a right adjoint

F_{1}:\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}\to\QC^{\vee}_{R}
, which is
\mathcal{C}
-linear by Construction
2.46
and
2.51
. Then
(-)_{1}
is promoted to a right ajdoint
(-)_{1}:{(\mathscr{C}^{op})^{\otimes}}\to{(\fil_{\geq 1}\mathscr{C}^{op})^{\otimes}}
relative to
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}

[
Lur17
, Corollary 7.3.2.7]
, which is an operadic map satisfying (1) and (2). Applying the same procedure to
\colim\dashv\const
gives the other lax
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
-monoidal functor
\const:\mathscr{C}_{d}^{\otimes}\to(\fil_{\leq-1}\mathscr{C}_{d})^{\otimes}
.

Restricting the lax monoidal functors in Proposition
3.12
leads to the expected horizontal arrows. The commutativity of (
6
) can be checked by considering the underlying functors.

∎

(7)

Proof.

This diagram is constructed by considering the operadic sections of (
6
), which consists of small-limit-preserving functors using
[
Lur17
, Proposition3.2.2.1]
. Since the vertices on the left-hand side are all op-presentable
\infty
-categories (Proposition
3.5
), the arrows in (
7
) starting from them are all right adjoints. Meanwhile, it is known that the vertices on the right-hand side are presentable
\infty
-categories. Observing that the three right vertical arrows preserve sifted colimits
[
Lur17
, Proposition 3.2.3.1]
, we conclude the proof using (2) in
[
Lur09
, Corollary 5.5.2.9]
.
∎

Notation 3.17
.

Denote the adjoints of
(-)^{\vee}
in (
7
) as
(-)^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
.

The functors
(-)^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
are formally deduced from the adjoint functor theorem. However, they can be made explicit for dually almost perfect algebras.

Corollary 3.18
.

The above adjunctions restrict to the following commutative diagram,

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}_{\ap}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{LMod}_{\dap}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}_{\ap}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{LMod}_{\dap}(\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathrm{LComod}_{\ap}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{{\mathrm{LMod}_{\dap}(\gr_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{(co\alg_{\ap}(\mathcal{C}))^{op}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alg_{\dap}(\mathcal{C})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\const}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee},\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee},\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee},\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\vee},\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\gr}

where
\ap
(
\dap
) means that the underlying module is (dually) almost perfect, and the inverses of
(-)^{\vee}
are given by
(-)^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
. In particular, the underlying module of
(-)^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
is given by
(-)^{\vee}
for dually almost perfect objects.

Proof.

Restricting the diagram in Proposition
3.15
to almost perfect (on the left) or dually almost perfect objects (on the right), this induces
\mathcal{LM}^{\otimes}
-monoidal equivalences. Then conclude by taking operadic sections.
∎

Definition 3.19
.

For an augmented (spectral PD, derived pro-coherent or derived PD)
\infty
-operad
\mathcal{P}
over
R
, its
PD Koszul dual
\infty
-operad
is defined as
\kd(\mathcal{P}):=\br(\mathcal{P})^{\vee}
.

If
A
is a
\mathcal{P}
-algebra object lies in
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
(or
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
,
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
), its
PD Koszul dual
is the
\kd(\mathcal{P})
-algebra object
\kd(A):=\br(A)^{\vee}
lies in
\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
(resp.
\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
,
\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\gr_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
).

3.3
Filtered Koszul duality for algebras

We assemble the results from the former two subsections together to relate the
\mathcal{P}
-algebras and
\kd(\mathcal{P})
-algebras via an adjunction that respects filtrations. The target is to establish that, under condition (
\dagger
) stated below, the
\infty
-category of dually almost perfect
\kd(\mathcal{P})
-algebras can be fully faithfully embedded into that of filtered
\mathcal{P}
-algebras,

\aqf:\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}\hookrightarrow\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}
(Theorem
3.25
). In particular, this theorem establishes a filtered Koszul duality for dually almost perfect partition Lie algebras.

Construction 3.20
.

Following Construction
3.1
and fix a
\mathcal{P}\in\alg(\mathcal{C})
satisfying the condition

(†)
\mathcal{P}
is reduced and connective, while
\br(\mathcal{P})
is almost perfect,

so that the unit morphisms
\mathcal{\mathcal{P}}\to\cobr\circ\br(\mathcal{P})
and
\br(\mathcal{P})\to((\br(\mathcal{P}))^{\vee})^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
(Notation
3.17
) are equivalences. Then, Theorem
3.2
and Corollary
3.16
induce a commuting diagram of adjoints

for any

. For general
A\in\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
, conclude by noting that
A
is equivalent to
|\br_{\bullet}(id,\fr_{\mathcal{P}}\circ\text{forget}_{\mathcal{P}},A)|
.

The next proposition shows that
\aqf
is truly a refinement of
\aq
.

Proposition 3.22
.

The functor
\aqf
is a sifted-colimit-preserving lift of
\aq
along
F_{1}
.

Proof.

Recall that the sifted colimits of algebra objects are calculated by that of the underlying modules, which results in that
\const
is sifted-colimit-preserving. Since
(-)^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
and
\cobr
are right adjoints, the functor
\aqf\simeq\cobr\circ(-)^{\text{\textexclamdown}}\circ\const
sends sifted colimits to sifted limits. Because (
8
) commutes, we have
F_{1}\aqf\simeq\aq\circ\colim\circ\const\simeq\aq
.
∎

Our main interest of (
8
) is that it applies to partition Lie algebras:

Definition 3.23
.

cf.
[
BCN21
, Definition 3.54, 3.88]

If
R
is a coherent connective
\eoo
-ring spectrum, the
spectral PD
\infty
-operad
\lie
of spectral partition Lie algebras over
R
is defined as
\kd(\neoo)
. When
R
is a coherent simplicial commutative ring, the
derived PD
\infty
-operad
\dlie
of derived partition Lie algebras over
R
is defined as
\kd(\com^{nu})
.

In these cases, the functor
\aq
(
\aqf
) is preferably written as
\ce
(
\cet
), which means
Chevalley-Eilenberg functor (with completed Hodge filtration)
for partition Lie algebras.

Then we deduce a filtered PD Koszul duality through
\aqf
under some finiteness conditions:

Notation 3.24
.

(1) Let
\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}\subset\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
be the full subcategory of complete algebras
A
such that the natural map
\fr_{\mathcal{P}}([\gr_{1}A]_{1})\simeq\gr(A)
is an equivalence of graded
\mathcal{P}
-algebras, and
\gr_{1}(A)
is an almost perfect
R
-module. Here
\qf
stands for
quasi-free
and
\afp
means
almost finitely presented
.

(2) A
\kd(\mathcal{P})
-algebra is said to be
dually almost perfect
if the underlying module is a dually almost perfect
R
-module. Such algebras form a full subcategory
\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}\subset\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
.

Theorem 3.25

(Filtered PD Koszul duality)
.

Assume that
P\in\alg(\mathcal{C})
satisfies the condition (†). The André-Quillen functor with Hodge filtration
\aqf
induces an equivalence

\aqf:\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}\simeq\alg^{\fil,op}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}},

whose homotopy inverse is given by restricting the following composite

\dD:\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})\xrightarrow{\kd}\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}\xrightarrow{\colim}\alg_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}.

Proof.

The core of this proof is calculating
\aqf(L)
for
L\in\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}
(see Lemma
3.30
), which is divided into several lemmas.

Recall that
\Delta
is the 1-category of simplices and let
\Delta_{sur}\subset\Delta
be the subcategory of surjections. Let
\Delta^{\leq n}
(or
(\Delta_{sur})^{\leq n}
) be the full subcategory of
\Delta
(resp.
\Delta_{sur}
) spanned by the objects of size no more than
n+1
. For an
\infty
-category
\mathcal{X}
, the
\infty
-category of
(semi-)cosimpilicial objects in
\mathcal{X}
is
\text{Fun}(N(\Delta),\mathcal{X})
(resp.
\text{Fun}(N(\Delta_{sur}),\mathcal{X})
). A (semi-)cosimpilicial object is said to be
n
-skeletal
if it is a right Kan extension of its restriction to
N((\Delta_{sur})^{\leq n})
.

Caveat 3.26
.

Our notion of semi-cosimplicial objects is
not
standard. It is only used to facilitate determining skeletal cosimplicial objects. Despite of the following lemma,
\Delta_{sur}\subset\Delta
is
not
coinitial.

Lemma 3.27
.

Let
\mathcal{X}
be an
\infty
-category. A cosimplicial object in
\mathcal{X}
is
n
-skeletal precisely when its restriction to
N(\Delta_{sur})
is
n
-skeletal as a semi-cosimplicial object.

Proof.

This lemma can be regarded as a dual version of
[
Lur09
, Lemma 6.5.3.8]
.
It is sufficient to prove that, for each
[m]\in\Delta
, the simplicial nerve of

(\Delta_{sur})_{[m]/}\times_{\Delta_{sur}}(\Delta_{sur})^{\leq n}\subset\Delta_{[m]/}\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\leq n}

is coinitial as a morphism of
\infty
-categories. Applying
[
Lur09
, Theorem 4.1.3.1]
, it suffices to show that, for each object
\theta:[m]\to[k]
of
\Delta_{[m]/}\times_{\Delta}\Delta^{\leq n}
, considering the category
C
of all factorizations

[m]\xrightarrow{\theta^{\prime}}[k^{\prime}]\xrightarrow{\theta^{\prime\prime}}[k]

such that
\theta^{\prime}
is a surjection and
k^{\prime}\leq n
, its simplicial nerve
N(C)
is weakly contractible. However,
C
has a final object (where
\theta^{\prime\prime}
is injective), so the lemma is proved.

∎

Set
D^{n}:=N((\Delta_{sur})_{[n]/}\times_{\Delta_{sur}}(\Delta_{sur})^{\leq n-1})
. The next lemma controls the convergence of the cobar construction for reduced
\infty
-cooperads.

Lemma 3.28
.

Assume that
\mathcal{Q}\in co\alg(\mathcal{C})
is
reduced
, i.e.
\mathcal{Q}(0)\simeq 0
and
\mathcal{Q}(1)\simeq R
. The cosimplicial diagram
\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)
is locally skeletal in the sense that the following difference

\Omega_{n}:=\text{fib}\big(\mathcal{Q}^{\circ n}\to\lim_{D^{n}}\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)|_{D^{n}}\big)

is trivial at each arity
p\leq n
. In particular, the cobar construction
\cobr(\mathcal{Q})\simeq\tot\big(\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)\big)
, at each chosen arity, is a finite totalization.

Proof.

This claim holds for
n=1
. We do induction on
n
. Take a cube
N(\Delta^{1})^{\times n}
and decorate each vertex
(a_{0},\ldots,a_{n})
with
\mathcal{A}_{1}\circ\ldots\circ\mathcal{A}_{n}
, where
\mathcal{A}_{i}\simeq\mathcal{Q}
if
a_{i}=0
and
\mathcal{A}_{i}\simeq 1
if
a_{i}=1
, and the edges of
N(\Delta)^{\times n}
are morphisms given by applying the counit map of
\mathcal{Q}
at the right position. The diagram
\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)|_{D^{n}}
can be reformulated as the diagram of the decorated edges
(0,\ldots,0)\to(a_{1},\ldots,a_{n})
such that
\sum|a_{i}|\neq 0
. This rephrasing leads to a commuting diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ n}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{D^{n}}\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)|_{D^{n}}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{Q}^{\circ(n-1)}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces({\lim_{D^{n-1}}\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)|_{D^{n-1}})\circ\mathcal{Q}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\lim_{D^{n-1}}\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)|_{D^{n-1}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\beta}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\alpha}

where the square is cartesian in
\mathcal{C}
. The
n
th difference
\Omega_{n}
fits into a fibre sequence,

\Omega_{n}\to\text{fib}(\alpha)\xrightarrow{\gamma}\text{fib}(\beta),

where
\gamma
is equivalent to
\Omega_{n-1}\circ\mathcal{Q}\xrightarrow{id_{\Omega_{n-1}}\circ\epsilon}\Omega_{n-1}
with
\epsilon
being the counit of
\mathcal{Q}
. By induction hypothesis,
\Omega_{n}
is trivial at arity
p\leq n
. Then the second assertion follows from Lemma
3.27
.

∎

Lemma 3.29
.

Supposing that
\mathcal{Q}\in co\alg(\mathcal{C})
is reduced, the commutative diagram of adjunctions

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alg_{\cobr(\mathcal{Q})}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{co\alg_{\mathcal{Q}}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cobr}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{forget}_{\cobr(\mathcal{Q})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\fr_{\cobr(\mathcal{Q})}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces e_{!}}

arising from Theorem
3.2
induces a natural equivalence
\cobr(\mathcal{Q})\circ(-)\simeq e^{*}e_{!}
of monads.

Proof.

By Corollary 5.8 and Corollary 8.9 in
[
Hau20
]
, there is a morphism of monads
\cobr(Q)\circ(-)\simeq\mathrm{forget}_{\cobr(\mathcal{Q})}\circ\fr_{\cobr(\mathcal{Q})}\to e^{*}e_{!}
, whose underlying morphism of endofunctors is given by

\tot\big(\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)\big)\circ V\to\tot\big(\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},e_{!}V)\big),

for each
V\in\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
. We claim that this arrow is an equivalence. Indeed, at each weight
p\geq 1
,

\tot\big(\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},1)\big)\circ V(p)\to\big(\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\mathcal{Q},e_{!}V)\big)(p)

(9)

only the components of arity
\leq p
are concerned since
\mathcal{Q}
has no nullary operation, which means that both sides in (
9
) are finite totalizations as shown in Lemma
3.28
. The product
\circ
(or
\bcirc
) preserves finite totalizations
[
BM19
, Proposition 3.37]
. Thus (
9
) is an equivalence for all
p
and
V
.

∎

Lemma 3.30
.

For each
L\in\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}
, the filtered André-Quillen algebra
\aqf(L)
lies in
\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}
, and
\gr(\aqf(L))
is freely generated by
[L^{\vee}]_{1}
.

Proof.

The underlying module of
C:=(\const(L))^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
is
(L^{\vee})_{1}
by Corollary
3.18
, which belongs to
\sfil_{\geq 1}\aperf_{R}
. The cosimplicial diagram
\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\br(\mathcal{P}),C)
consists of almost perfect (then complete by Proposition
2.18
) terms. Thus
\aqf(L)\simeq\tot\cobr^{\bullet}(1,\br(\mathcal{P}),C)
is complete.

Now we determine the
\br(\mathcal{P})
-coalgebra structure of
\gr(C)
using a dual version of
[
Lur17
, Theorem 4.7.1.34]
. Let
\mathcal{C}_{\red}
be the full subcategory of
\mathcal{C}
in Construction
3.1
spanned by reduced objects, i.e.
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{C}
such that
\mathcal{Q}(0)\simeq 0
and
\mathcal{Q}(1)\simeq R
naturally. The
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{\red}
inherits from
\mathcal{C}
a monoidal structure and a left action on
\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R}
. The
(co)endomorphism category

\mathcal{C}_{\red}[[L^{\vee}]_{1}]
in the sense of
[
Lur17
, Definition 4.7.1.1]
consists of the pairs
(\mathcal{Q},\Delta)
such that
\mathcal{Q}\in\mathcal{C}_{\red}
and
\Delta:[L^{\vee}]_{1}\to\mathcal{Q}\circ[L^{\vee}]_{1}
. The natural monoidal projection
p:\mathcal{C}_{\red}[[L^{\vee}]_{1}]\to\mathcal{C}_{\red}
is a left fibration
[
Lur17
, Lemma 4.7.1.36]
having constant fibres
\mathrm{End}_{R}(L^{\vee})
. By the natural augmentation
\mathcal{Q}\to\mathbbb{1}
in
\mathcal{C}_{\red}
, we have a second projection
q:\mathcal{C}_{\red}[[L^{\vee}]_{1}]\to p^{-1}(\mathbbb{1})\simeq\mathrm{End}_{R}(L^{\vee})
. The projections
p
and
q
together induce a product
\mathcal{C}_{\red}[[L^{\vee}]_{1}]\simeq\mathcal{C}_{\red}\times\mathrm{End}_{R}(L^{\vee})
.

This product and Theorem 4.7.1.34 in
[
Lur17
]
show that the space of
\br(\mathcal{P})
-coalgebra structures on
[L^{\vee}]_{1}
is equivalent to that of
\mathbbb{1}
-coalgebras, which is contractible
[
Lur17
, Proposition 4.2.4.9]
. Therefore, there is a cocartesian edge
[L^{\vee}]_{1}\xrightarrow{e_{!}}\gr(C)
in
\mathrm{LComod}(\gr_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
lying over the coaugmentation
\mathbbb{1}\to\br(\mathcal{P})
in
co\alg(\mathcal{C})
(given by forgetful functor of coalgebras). Here we have a morphism
\eta:\fr_{\mathcal{P}}([L^{\vee}]_{1})\to\cobr(e_{!}[L^{\vee}]_{1})\simeq\gr(\aqf(C))
adjoint to
id_{\gr(C)}
by Theorem
3.2
. Lemma
3.29
shows that the arrow
\eta
is an equivalence.

∎

The remaining task is to show that
\dD|_{\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}}
is a homotopy inverse of
\aqf|_{\alg^{\dap}_{\mathcal{P}}}
. Take any
A\in\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}
, the underlying module of
\gr(\br(A))
is
[\gr_{1}A]_{1}
due to Theorem
3.2
, so
\kd(A)
has
((\gr_{1}A)^{\vee})_{-1}
as the underlying module, which is dually almost perfect. Thus the functor
\dD
induces a functor
\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}\to\alg^{\dap,op}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}
. Since
\const
in (
8
) is fully faithful, there is a contravariant adjunction

\aqf:\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}\rightleftarrows\alg^{\fil,op}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}:\dD
.
For each
L\in\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}
, the unit map
\eta:L\to\dD\circ\aqf(L)
is given by applying
\const\circ(-)^{\vee}
to

a:\br\circ\cobr(C)\to C
with
C:=(\const(L))^{\text{\textexclamdown}}
. Meanwhile, the calculation in Lemma
3.30
shows that
\gr(a)
is an equivalence. The completeness of
R
-linear dual (
4
) shows that
\eta
is an equivalence as well. In the meanwhile, for each
A\in\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}
, the unit map
\xi:A\to\aqf\circ\dD(A)
is a morphism of complete algebras and
\gr(\xi)
is homotopic with
\gr(A)\to\cobr\circ\br(\gr(A))
by Proposition
3.18
, which is an equivalence by Lemma
3.30
, so
\xi
is also an equivalence.

∎

Remark 3.31
.

Suppose that
R
is eventually coconnective so that
\iota:\m_{R}\hookrightarrow\QC^{\vee}_{R}
is a fully faithful embedding. If
\mathcal{P}
is a (spectral, derived or restricted derived)
\infty
-operad, then
\alg^{\fil}_{\mathcal{P},\mathrm{qfafp}}
can be identified with a full subcategory of
\alg_{\mathcal{P}}(\fil_{\geq 1}\m_{R})
by Proposition
2.37
. Under this condition, the filtered André-Quillen functor
\aqf
embeds
\alg^{\dap}_{\kd(\mathcal{P})}
(contravariantly) into the
\infty
-category of ordinary
\mathcal{P}
-algebras with a decreasing filtration.

In particular, Theorem
3.25
applies to partition Lie algebras:

Corollary 3.32
.

Let
R
be a coherent connective
\eoo
-ring spectrum. The Chevalley-Eilenberg functor
\cet
with Hodge filtration induces a fully faithful embedding

\cet:\aplie\hookrightarrow\calg^{nu}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}

from the
\infty
-category of dually almost perfect spectral partition Lie algebras, into the
\infty
-category of
R
-augmented filtered pro-coherent
\eoo
-algebras. Furthermore, the essential image of
\cet
consists of completely filtered pro-coherent ring spectra
A
, such that
\sym_{R}[\gr_{1}A]_{1}\simeq\gr A
naturally.

Corollary 3.33
.

Let
R
be a coherent simplicial commutative ring. The Chevalley-Eilenberg functor
\cet
with Hodge filtration induces a fully faithful embedding

\cet:\apdlie\hookrightarrow\dalg^{nu}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{op}

from the
\infty
-category of dually almost perfect derived partition Lie algebras into that of
R
-augmented filtered pro-coherent derived algebras. The essential image of
\cet
consists of such
A
that is complete and has
\lsym_{R}[\gr_{1}A]_{1}\simeq\gr A
naturally.

This section is closed by a technique corollary for later usage:

Corollary 3.34

(Totalization of free algebras)
.

Let
R
be a coherent simplicial commutative ring, and
M\to N^{\bullet}
be a totalization diagram existing in
\aperf_{R}
. Then there is an induced totalization
\lsym_{R}[M]_{1}\simeq\tot\lsym_{R}[N^{\bullet}]_{1}
of graded derived algebras.

Proof.

There is a geometric realization
M^{\vee}\simeq|(N^{\bullet})^{\vee}|
existing in
\aperf^{\vee}_{R}
, which can also be regarded as a geometric realization of abelian partition Lie algebras. Thus, there is
\cet(M^{\vee})\simeq\tot\cet((N^{\bullet})^{\vee})
by Theorem
3.25
. Conclude by considering the associated graded algebras.
∎

3.4
Application: Lie algebra of Frobenius kernel over
\mathbb{F}_{2}

Set
k=\mathbb{F}_{2}
the field with two elements. The group of second roots of unity
\mu_{2}=\mathrm{Spec}(k[x]/(x^{2}-1))
is an infinitesimal flat group of interest. Now, we explicitly determine all the homotopy operations of its derived partition Lie algebra
\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}}
, where
\{2\}
suggests the existence of
2
-restrictions.

We begin by explaining the geometric meaning behind each term. As the fibre of the Frobenius
\varphi:\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}
, the group scheme
\mu_{2}
has
\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}}\simeq k\oplus\Sigma^{-1}k
as its tangent complex at the identity. This complex carries a natural
\kdlie
-structure such that
\ce(\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}})\simeq k[x]/(x^{2}-1)
,
[
BM19
, Theorem 4.20]
. Further, Theorem 4.23 in
[
BM19
]
shows that
\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}}
is a group object in derived partition Lie algebras. Let
\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}}
denote the
\kdlie
-algebra
B\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}}
, which is the tangent of
B\mu_{2}
. Conversely,
\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}}\simeq\Omega\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}}
. In particular, the underlying module of
\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}}
is
\Sigma k\oplus k
.

Proposition 3.35
.

(1) The homotopy operations on a
\kdlie
-algebra
L
concentrating in degree
0
and
1
consist of
([-,-],(-)^{\{2\}})
a restricted Lie structure on
\mathfrak{g}_{1}:=\pi_{1}(L)
, a
\mathfrak{g}_{1}
-representation structure on
\mathfrak{g}_{0}:=\pi_{0}(L)
and a new additive operation
R^{1}:\mathfrak{g}_{1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{0}
.

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}_{1}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{g}_{-1}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[-,-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\{2\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces R^{1}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[-,-]}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(-)^{\{2\}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces[\mathfrak{g}_{1},-]}

(2) The homotopy operations on a
\kdlie
-algebra
L
concentrating in degree
0
and
-1
consist of a symmetric bilinear map
[-,-]
and a weight
2
operation
(-)^{\{2\}}
satisfying

(x+y)^{\{2\}}=(x)^{\{2\}}+[x,y]+(y)^{\{2\}}.

Proof.

We only carry out the proof of (1). The same reasoning applies to (2) immediately.

Recall that the homotopy operations of
\kdlie
-algebras are encoded by the homology of free algebras. Since
L
concentrates at degree
0,1
, it suffices to consider
\pi_{i}(\kdlie(\Sigma k^{\otimes{l_{1}}}\oplus k^{l_{2}}))
for
i=0,1
. Following
[
BM19
, Theorem 7.3]
,
\pi_{*}(\kdlie(\Sigma k^{\otimes{l_{1}}}\oplus k^{l_{2}}))
has a basis indexed by sequences
(i_{1},\ldots,i_{k},w)
. Here
w\in B(n_{1},\ldots,n_{l_{1}},m_{1},\ldots,m_{l_{2}})
is a
Lyndon word
with
n_{j}
(
m_{j}
) denoting how many times each letter at degree
1
(or
0
) appears, and
|w|:=1-m_{1}-\ldots-m_{l_{2}}
. Besides, the integers
i_{k}
satisfy: (1)
0\leq i_{k}\leq|w|
; (2) for all
1\leq j<k
,
0\leq i_{j}<2i_{j+1}
. Such a sequence has degree
|w|-l_{2}
. In particular,
\pi_{1}(\kdlie(\Sigma k^{\otimes{l_{1}}}\oplus k^{l_{2}}))
is spanned by the sequences in the form of
(1,\ldots,1,(x))
that all integers (if
k\geq 1
) are
1
and
x
is a letter of degree
1
. As for
\pi_{0}(\kdlie(\Sigma k^{\otimes{l_{1}}}\oplus k^{l_{2}}))
, the basis has two types of vectors: (1)
(0,1,\ldots,1,(x))
with
|x|=1
; (2)
(w)
, where
w
is a Lyndon word containing exactly one (counting the multiplicity) letter at degree
0
.

Now, we consider some “atomic” operations: let
[-,-]
denote the operations
\mathfrak{g}_{1}\times\mathfrak{g}_{i}\to\mathfrak{g}_{i}
(
i=0,1
) represented by
((xy))
with
|x|=1
and
|y|=0,1
,
(-)^{\{2\}}
denote the operation
\mathfrak{g}_{1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{1}
represented by the sequence
(1,(x))
with
|x|=1
, and
R^{1}
denote the operation
\mathfrak{g}_{1}\to\mathfrak{g}_{0}
represented by the sequence
(0,(x))
with
|x|=1
.

Then, we find the relations between
[-,-]
,
(-)^{\{2\}}
and
R^{1}
. In fact, Theorem 1.1 in
[
Zha22
]
describes thoroughly the relations between the homotopy operations of
\klie
-algebras. To pass to
\kdlie
, recall that the embedding
k[\Sigma]\hookrightarrow k[\mathcal{O}_{\Sigma}]
induces a monoidal embedding
\sseq^{\vee}_{k}\to\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{k}
, whose right adjoint maps
\com^{nu}
to
\neoo
. Therefore, the unit map provides a morphism
f:\neoo\to\com^{nu}
with
\neoo
identified with its Borel derived operad. The PD Koszul dual of
f
gives rise to a morphism of monads
\kd(f):\kdlie\to\klie
. At the same time, a direct calculation shows that, for each finitely dimensional graded
k
-space
V
,
\kdlie(2)\circ V\to\klie(2)\circ V
is dual to the natural map

c_{V}:(\sym^{2}_{k}V^{\vee})[1]\to(\lsym^{2}_{k}V^{\vee})[1].

For
V=k
,
\pi_{*}(c_{V})
is obviously surjective. For
V=\Sigma k
,
\pi_{*}(\lsym^{2}_{k}\Sigma^{-1}k)
is spanned by a generator
\tau^{2}
at degree
-2
and a generator
\st\tau
at degree
-1
given by Steenrod square. Thus,
\pi_{*}(c_{\Sigma k})
is also surjective. Thus,
\kdlie(2)\circ(\Sigma k^{\oplus l_{1}}\oplus k^{\oplus l_{2}})\to\klie(2)\circ(\Sigma k^{\oplus l_{1}}\oplus k^{\oplus l_{2}})
is injective on the level of homotopy groups. Further, the operations
[-,-]
,
(-)^{2}
and
R^{1}
are sent to the corresponding operations via
\kd(f)
in
[
Zha22
, Theorem 1.1]
, which satisfy the statement in this proposition.

Moreover, notice that the homotopy operations for
\klie
-algebras represented by
(1,\ldots,1,w)
with
|w|=1
,
(0,1,\ldots,1,w)
with
|w|=1
and
(w)
with
|w|=0
are non-null composites of
[-,-]
,
(-)^{\{2\}}
and
R^{1}
. Therefore, they actually come from the homotopy operation of
\kdlie
-algebras and spanned by the “atomic” operations.

To prove (2), it suffices to note that
c_{\Sigma^{-1}k}
is surjective on the level of homotopy groups as
\lsym^{2}_{k}(k[1])\simeq 0
and use the same reasoning.

∎

A
\kdlie
-algebra
L
with perfect underlying module is determined by
\cet(L)
(Theorem
3.25
). The binary operations on
\pi_{*}(L)
are evident from this perspective.

Lemma 3.36
.

The composition map
m:(\kdlie(2)\otimes L^{\otimes 2})^{\Sigma_{2}}\to L
of a perfect
\kdlie
-algebra
L
is dual to the natural connection
d_{L}:L^{\vee}\to\lsym^{2}_{k}(L^{\vee})[1]
via
\cet(L)
.

Proof.

Set
H:=\kdlie((L)_{-1})
the free filtered
\kdlie
-algebra, and consider the composition map
m^{\sfil}:H\to\const(L)
of filtered
\kdlie
-algebras. Endow both sides with a second constant filtration, and then apply the Chevalley-Eilenberg functor in the bi-filtered context, we obtain a morphism of bi-complete algebras

\Delta:F_{\bullet}F_{*}\ce(L)\to F_{\bullet}F_{*}\ce(\kdlie(L)),

where
\gr_{\bullet}\Delta
is
\lsym(m^{\fil,\vee}):\lsym^{\bullet}_{k}(L^{\vee})_{1}\to\lsym^{\bullet}_{k}H^{\vee}
. The connection map of
\bullet
-filtrations give rise to a commutative square of filtered modules

Then, notice that there is a symmetric monoidal left adjoint
\oplus:\gr\QC^{\vee}_{k}\to\fil\QC^{\vee}_{k}
sending the free graded algebra
H^{\sgr}:=\kdlie([L]_{-1})\simeq\gr H
to
H
. Therefore, there is a splitting structure on
F_{*}\ce(H)\simeq\widehat{\oplus}\ce(H^{\sgr})
. Besides, there is an equivalence
\widehat{\oplus}\ce(H^{\sgr})\simeq\sqz([L^{\vee}]_{1})
by the freeness of
H^{\sgr}
and Proposition
3.9
. It means that
\alpha_{1}\simeq 0
and
\alpha_{2}
is the natural equivalence, i.e.
d_{L}\simeq\alpha_{2}\circ m^{\vee}
.

∎

Example 3.37

(Lie structure of
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\{2\}}
)
.

Let
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\{2\}}
denote the
\kdlie
-algebra corresponding to
B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
by
[
BM19
, Theorem 4.23]
, which has
\Sigma k
as the underlying module. Since
B\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}\simeq|\widehat{\mathbb{G}}^{\times\bullet}_{m}|
, the filtered Chevalley-Eilenberg algebra
A:=\cet(\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{m}))
is the totalization of a cosimplicial resolution
k[[x]]^{\widehat{\otimes}\bullet}
with all variables of weight
1
, which exhibits the formal group law of
\widehat{\mathbb{G}}_{m}
. In particular, the coface maps between the terms of degree
\leq 3
are as follows:

k\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-1.72218pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\end{subarray}}k[[t]]\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-1.72218pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-1.72218pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\end{subarray}}k[[x_{1},x_{2}]]\mathrel{\begin{subarray}{c}\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-1.72218pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-1.72218pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\\[-1.72218pt]
\textstyle\rightarrow\end{subarray}}k[[y_{1},y_{2},y_{3}]]

(10)

t
is sent to
x_{2}
,
x_{1}+x_{2}+x_{1}x_{2}
,
x_{1}
by the three coface maps, and
(x_{1},x_{2})
is mapped to
(y_{2},y_{3}),(y_{1}+y_{2}+y_{1}y_{2},y_{3}),(y_{1},y_{2}+y_{3}+y_{2}y_{3})
or
(y_{1},y_{2})
respectively by the four coface maps.

The homology of graded pieces
\pi_{*}\gr(A)
is known by
[
Pri73
, Theorem 4.0.1]
. In fact, it is the polynomial algebra
\pi_{*}\gr(A)\cong k[\tau,\sq\tau,\sq^{\circ 2}\tau,\ldots]
, where
\sq^{\circ n}\tau
is of degree
-1
and weight
2^{n}
. In particular, there is
\gr_{2}(A)\simeq k\tau^{2}\oplus k\sq\tau
. Considering the
\gr_{1}
piece of (
10
), there is one non-null class
[t]
representing
\tau
. The
\gr_{2}
piece of (
10
) has two classes
[t^{2}]
of degree
-1
and
[x_{1}x_{2}]
of degree
-2
, which represent
\sq\tau
and
\tau^{2}
respectively. Moreover, there is
\partial t=x_{1}x_{2}
in (
10
), which means that the connection map
d:\gr_{1}A\to\gr_{2}A[1]
sends
\tau=[t]
to
\tau^{2}=[x_{1}x_{2}]
. The dual of
d
shows that
\mathrm{Lie}(\mathbb{G}_{m})^{\{2\}}
has null Lie bracket and an identical restriction map, which recovers the ordinary restricted Lie algebra of
\mathbb{G}_{m}
(up to a shifting).

Proposition 3.38
.

The homotopy operations of
\mathrm{Lie}(\mu_{2})^{\{2\}}
, having the underlying module
\Sigma\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}}\simeq kD_{1}\oplus kD_{0}
(
|D_{i}|=i
), can be described as

3.36
shows that it suffices to understand the connection

The homotopy group of
\gr\cet(L)
is
k[\upsilon,\tau,\sq\tau,\ldots]
by
[
Pri73
, Theorem 4.0.1]
, where
\upsilon=[D_{0}^{\vee}]
is of degree
0
and
\tau=[D_{1}^{\vee}]
is still of degree
-1
. The right vertical face of the above cube implies that
\pi_{-1}(d):\tau\mapsto\tau^{2}
following Example
3.37
. The map
\pi_{0}(d):k\upsilon\to\pi_{-1}(\gr_{2}\cet(L))
is encoded by the front vertical face post composed with
d
, regarded as a loop in
\map(k\tau^{\prime},\gr_{2}\cet(L)[1])
. The above cube is an equivalence between this
2
-morphism with another one given by the upper horizontal face post composed by the natural inclusion
\gr_{2}A[1]\to\gr_{2}\cet(L)[1]
, since all the other faces are null-homotopic through the triviality of
F_{*\geq 1}k
. Then, recall that
\varphi:k\tau^{\prime}\to k\tau
is null-homotopic by the explicit model in Example
3.37
and the lifting of
\varphi
along
\gr_{2}A\to F_{1}/F_{3}A\to\gr_{1}A\simeq k\tau
. Therefore, the upper horizontal face represents a map
k\tau^{\prime}\to\gr_{2}A
agreeing with the lifting of
\varphi
, which is
\tau^{\prime}=[t]\mapsto[t^{2}]=\st\tau
following Example
3.37
. It means that
\pi_{0}(d):\upsilon\mapsto\sq\tau
, or dually
R^{1}:D_{1}\mapsto D_{0}
and
[D_{1},D_{0}]=0
.
∎

A similar but easier process shows that
\cet(\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}})=(k[\upsilon,\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^{2}),d)
with
|\varepsilon|=1
, where the graded mixed structure
d
sends
\varepsilon
to
\upsilon
. It means that
\mathbb{T}_{\mu_{2}}\simeq kD_{0}\oplus kD_{-1}
has vanishing Lie bracket and
(D_{0})^{\{2\}}=D_{-1}
.

4
Partition Lie algebroids and Koszul duality

We define
(derived) partition Lie algebroids over
B
(relative to
R
), in the spirit of
[
BW20
]
, as the algebras of a sifted-colimit-preserving monad
T
acting on
\QC^{\vee}_{B}
(Definition
4.4
). Here
T
receives a monad morphism from
\bdlie
. Their interplay with filtrations will be detailed in §
4.2
.

Our purpose is to establish a “many-object” generalization of Corollary
3.33
. We present in Theorem
4.26
an equivalence between
dually almost perfect
partition Lie algebroids with a special types of complete filtered
R
-derived algebras, named as
foliation-like algebras
(Notation
4.15
), which generalize the derived foliations in the sense of
[
TV23a
, §1.2]
into general characteristics.

4.1
Partition Lie algebroids

Before introducing partition Lie algebroids, we review the basics of commutative algebras in pro-coherent modules via derived symmetric sequences (Definition
2.49
).

Recollection 4.1
.

Consider the
\infty
-category of coherent simplicial commutative rings
\mathrm{SCR}^{\scoh}\subset\mathrm{SCR}
. From Theorem
2.32
, Lemma
2.50
and the adjoint functor theorem, the (pro-coherent) derived symmetric sequences with different coefficient algebras are encoded in the following diagram

Here, the transfers along arrows
R\to B
in
\mathrm{SCR}^{\scoh}
give rise to the base change
B\otimes_{R}
for (pro-coherent) derived symmetric sequences, whose right adjoints are restrictions of scalars.

Both the transfers and the natural transformation
\iota
are symmetric monoidal with respect to
\otimes
and
\levtimes
, monoidal with respect to
\circ
and
\bcirc
. In particular, they always preserve
\com
, as the unit of
\levtimes
and an associative algebra of
\circ
. Then, for each
R\to B
in
\mathrm{SCR}^{\scoh}
, there is an adjunction

B\otimes_{R}(-):\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})\rightleftarrows\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{B}):\mathrm{rest}.

Moreover, this adjunction is monadic by Barr-Beck-Lurie’s theorem, which identifies
\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{B})
with the slice category
\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{B/}
.

Now, we construct a monad
T
governing the partition Lie algebroids.

Construction 4.2
.

The square zero extension of
B
-modules gives rise to functors into different algebra categories

\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}:\QC^{\vee}_{B}\xrightarrow{\sqz_{B}}\dalg^{nu}(\QC^{\vee}_{B})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{rest}}\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B},

where
\sqz_{B}
has a left adjoint
\cot_{B}
given by
\br
in Construction
3.20
, and
\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}
has a left adjoint
\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}:=\cot_{B}(B\otimes_{R}-)
called the
relative cotangent fibre
. Composing with the
B
-linear dual defines a natural but unsatisfying monad on
\QC^{\vee}_{B}

\overline{T}:=(-)^{\vee}\circ\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}\circ\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}\circ(-)^{\vee}.

We are going to rectify
\overline{T}
into a sifted-colimit-preserving monad using right-left extension.

Lemma 4.3
.

For each
V\in\aperf^{\vee}_{B}
, there is a functorially
splitting
fibre sequence
\bdlie(V)\to\overline{T}(V)\to\mathbb{T}_{B/R}(:=\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{B/R})
in
\QC^{\vee}_{B}
.

Proof.

Observe that there is a natural transformation
\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}\circ\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}\to\cot_{B}\circ\sqz_{B}
given by the counit map of
(B\otimes_{R}-)\dashv\mathrm{rest}
. It induces a natural cofibre sequence

\mathbb{L}_{B/R}\simeq\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}\circ\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}(0)\to\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}\circ\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}(V^{\vee})\to\cot_{B}\circ\sqz_{B}(V^{\vee}).

Indeed, applying
\map_{\QC^{\vee}_{B}}(-,M)
on it gives a homotopy pullback

where
f:B\to\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}(M)
is given by
0\to M
. Taking
B
-linear dual gives the wanted fibre sequence as
\bdlie(V)\simeq(\cot_{B}\circ\sqz_{B}(V^{\vee}))^{\vee}
canonically
[
BCN21
, Proposition 3.53]
. The splitting is given by applying
\overline{T}
on the retraction
0\to V\to 0
.
∎

Assuming that the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/R}
is almost perfect, the above lemma shows that
\overline{T}|_{\aperf^{\vee}_{B}}\in\mathrm{End}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf_{B}^{\vee})
. Additionally,
\bdlie|_{\aperf^{\vee}_{B}}\to\overline{T}|_{\aperf^{\vee}_{B}}
is a morphism of monads, i.e. a morphism of algebras in
\mathrm{End}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf_{B}^{\vee})
with respect to the composition product
[
Hau20
, Corollary 5.8, 8.9]
. Then, left Kan extension induces a morphism of sifted-colimit-preserving monads
\bdlie\to T
acting on
\QC^{\vee}_{B}
, Remark
2.30
.

Definition 4.4
.

The
\infty
-category
{\lagd}
of
(derived) partition Lie algebroids over
B
(relative to
R
)
is defined as the
\infty
-category
\alg_{T}(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
of
T
-algebras.

Construction 4.5
.

The forgetful functor along
\bdlie\to T
maps
\fr_{T}(0)
(an initial object of
\lagd
) to a
\bdlie
-algebra given by
\kd(B\otimes_{R}B)
(
3.19
). Therefore, there is a functor

\mathrm{fib}:\lagd\to(\bdlie)_{\kd(B\otimes_{R}B)/}

of taking the “
fibres
”, where
\alg_{\bdlie}(\QC^{\vee}_{B})
is written as
\bdlie
for short.

B
-modules, we obtain a commuting diagram of right adjoints

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

(11)

where
\mathrm{cofib}
is a categorical equivalence sending each
f:\mathbb{T}_{B/R}\to M
to
\rho:\mathrm{cofib}(f)\to\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]
. We call the top horizontal arrow the
forgetful functor
of partition Lie algebroids, whose left adjoint
\fr_{\lagd}
is called the free partition Lie algebroid functor.

A partition Lie algebroid can be then informally written as
\rho:\mathfrak{g}\to\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]
. The forgetful functor of
T
-algebras is nothing but taking the fibre of
\rho
.

Next, we construct a Chevalley-Eilenberg functor
C^{*}
for partition Lie algebroids.

Notation 4.6
.

A partition Lie algebroid
L
is said to be
dually almost perfect
if
\mathrm{forget}(L)
belongs to
(\aperf^{\vee}_{B})_{/\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]}
. Let
\aplagd\subset\lagd
be the full subcategory of dually almost perfect partition Lie algebroids, which is naturally equivalent to
\alg_{T}(\aperf_{B}^{\vee})
by Lemma
4.3
.

Denote by
\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}}
the full subcategory of
\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}
consisting of such
A(\to B)
that the relative cotangent fibre
\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}(A)
is an almost perfect
B
-module, where
\mathrm{wafp}
means
weakly almost finitely presented
.

Proposition 4.7

(Chevalley-Eilenberg functor)
.

There is an adjunction of
\infty
-categories

C^{*}:\lagd\rightleftarrows\big(\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}\big)^{op}:\mathfrak{D}

such that
\mathrm{forget}\circ\mathfrak{D}
maps
A\to B
to
\mathbb{T}_{B/A}[1]\to\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]
, where the left adjoint functor
C^{*}
is called the
Chevalley-Eilenberg functor
of partition Lie algebroids.

Proof.

We start with the adjunction
\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}\circ(-)^{\vee}:\aperf^{\vee}_{R}\rightleftarrows(\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}})^{op}:(-)^{\vee}\circ\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}
of full subcategories guaranteed by Lemma
4.3
. It gives rise to the following adjoint by abstract nonsense

\bar{C}^{*}:\aplagd\simeq\alg_{\overline{T}}(\aperf^{\vee}_{B})\rightleftarrows(\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}})^{op}:\bar{\mathfrak{D}}.

Define
\mathfrak{D}:\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}\to(\lagd)^{op}
as the left Kan extension of

\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}}\xrightarrow{\bar{\mathfrak{D}}}(\aplagd)^{op}\hookrightarrow(\lagd)^{op}.

We claim that
\mathfrak{D}
is small-colimit-preserving. Since
\sqz_{\mathit{R//B}}
respects filtered colimits, its left adjoint
\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}
preserves compactness, thus
\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}}
includes the full subcategory of compact objects
\mathcal{C}^{\omega}
. By the uniqueness of Kan extension,
\mathfrak{D}
is equivalent to the left Kan extension of
\bar{\mathfrak{D}}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\omega}}
, and further
\bar{\mathfrak{D}}|_{\mathcal{C}^{\omega}}
preserves finite colimits. Observe that
\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}
is presentable and
(\lagd)^{op}
is cocomplete (Corollary
4.9
as below), thus one may use the reasoning of
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.5.1.9]
to say that
\mathfrak{D}
is small-colimit-preserving, and it admits a right adjoint
C^{*}
.

Finally, note that
\mathrm{forget}\circ\bar{\mathfrak{D}}(A\to B)\simeq\big(\mathbb{T}_{B/A}[1]\to\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]\big)
for
A\to B
in
\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}}
. It also holds in general, since the algebraic cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{B/-}
is small-colimit-preserving.
∎

We then compare the above Chevalley-Eilenberg functor
C^{*}
with
\ce
for
B
-partition Lie algebras, defined in §
3.3
. To do so, we require some categorical preparation.

Lemma 4.8
.

Let
\mathcal{C}
be a presentable
\infty
-category and
T
a sifted-colimit-preserving monad on it. Then the
\infty
-category
\alg_{T}(\mathcal{C})
is presentable.

Proof.

Since
\mathcal{C}\simeq\text{Ind}_{\kappa}(\mathcal{C}_{0})
for some regular cardinal
\kappa
and small
\infty
-category
\mathcal{C}_{0}
, the
\infty
-category
\text{End}_{cont,\kappa}(\mathcal{C})\simeq\text{Fun}(\mathcal{C}_{0},\mathcal{C})
of
\kappa
-continuous endofunctors is accessible
[
Lur09
, Proposition 5.4.4.3]
. Since
\mathcal{C}
is left tensored by
\text{End}_{cont,\kappa}(\mathcal{C})
,
\alg_{T}({\mathcal{C}})
is accessible
[
Lur17
, Corollary 3.2.3.5]
. Now it remains to check that
\alg_{T}
admits all small colimits. But
T
is sifted-colimit-preserving, then
\alg_{T}(\mathcal{C})
admits sifted colimits
[
Lur17
, Corollary 4.2.3.5]
, and the forgetful functor to
\mathcal{C}
creates sifted colimits. The coproduct of two free algebras and the initial object are clear. The coproduct of two general algebras can be found as follows: for each
A_{i}\in\alg_{T}(\mathcal{C})
,
i=1
or
2
, we have
A_{i}\simeq|\br_{\bullet}(T,T,A_{i})|
by Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem, then
A_{1}\coprod_{\alg_{T}}A_{2}
can be obtained by the geometric realization of the diagonal of
T\big(\br_{\bullet}(id,T,A_{1})\coprod_{\mathcal{C}}\br_{\bullet}(id,T,A_{2})\big)
.
∎

Corollary 4.9
.

The
\infty
-category
\lagd
of partition Lie algebroids is presentable.

Lemma 4.10
.

Consider a cocomplete
\infty
-category
\mathcal{D}
. Let
\mathscr{A}
be a coherent additive
\infty
-category, and
T
be a sifted-colimit-preserving monad on
\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}
which preserves the full subcategory
\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee}
. Write
T^{\prime}:=T|_{\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee}}
. If the following
F^{0}
satisfies that
f^{0}:=F^{0}\circ\fr_{T^{\prime}}
belongs to
\text{Fun}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee},\mathcal{D})

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alg_{T^{\prime}}(\aperf_{\mathscr{A},\eqslantless 0}^{\vee})}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathcal{D}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\alg_{T}{\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces F}

then there exists uniquely a sifted-colimit-preserving filler
F
up to a contractible space of choices.

Proof.

Remind us that
f^{0}
can be uniquely (up to homotopy) extended into
f\in\text{Fun}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathcal{D})

[
BCN21
, Proposition 2.40]
. The idea is to transfer this extension to
\alg_{T}{(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}})
. Here we generalize the reasoning of
[
Hol23
, Construction 2.2.17]
.

Regard
\text{Fun}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathcal{D})

\big(
resp.
\text{Fun}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee},\mathcal{D})
\big)
as a right tensored
\infty
-category of
\text{End}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}})

\big(
resp.
\text{End}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee})
\big)
by composition of functors, and write the corresponding
(\infty,1)
-operad as
\mathcal{F}^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{RM}^{\otimes}
(resp.
(\mathcal{F^{\dap}})^{\otimes}\to\mathcal{RM}^{\otimes}
). Then, left Kan extension induces a fully faithful embedding
(\mathcal{F^{\dap}})^{\otimes}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{F}^{\otimes}
of
(\infty,1)
-operads by the uniquess of extension. Here follows

\text{RMod}_{T^{\prime}}\big(\text{Fun}_{\sigma,reg}(\aperf_{\mathscr{A}}^{\vee},\mathcal{D})\big)\hookrightarrow\text{RMod}_{T}\big(\text{Fun}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathcal{D})\big)

a fully faithful embedding which takes
f^{0}=F^{0}\circ\fr_{T^{\prime}}
to its left Kan extension
f
with a canonical right
T
-module structure. At the same time, a direct calculation shows that the assignment
\Phi\mapsto\Phi\circ\fr_{T}
induces a categorical equivalence

-\circ\fr_{T}:\text{Fun}_{\Sigma}(\alg_{T}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}}),\mathcal{D})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\text{RMod}_{T}\big(\text{Fun}_{\Sigma}(\QC^{\vee}_{\mathscr{A}},\mathcal{D})\big)

(12)

whose inverse
B
can be described as
B(\phi)\simeq|\br_{\bullet}(\phi,T,\text{forget})|
. The promised filler in the statement can be taken as
B(f)
.

∎

Corollary 4.11

(Comparison of
\ce
and
C^{*}
)
.

There is a commuting square of adjunctions

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where
\ce\dashv\kd
is given in Construction
3.20
, and
\bdlie
refers to
\alg_{\bdlie}(\QC^{\vee}_{B})
. Additionally, the underlying module of
\rho_{0}(\mathfrak{h})
is
(\mathfrak{h}\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1])
.

Proof.

Recall that forgetting along
\bdlie\to T
induces the left vertical right adjoint
\mathrm{fib}
, which creates sifted colimits by considering the underlying modules. Therefore it admits a left adjoint
\rho_{0}
by the adjoint functor theorem. For each free
\mathfrak{h}=\bdlie(V)
, one can see that
\rho_{0}(\mathfrak{h})
is
\fr_{T}(V)
, which has the underlying module as
\bdlie(V)\xrightarrow{0}\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]
, Lemma
4.3
. For general
\mathfrak{h}
, it suffices to consider the bar resolution
\br_{\bullet}(id,\bdlie,\mathfrak{h})
.

Now we verify that
C^{*}\circ\rho_{0}\simeq\mathrm{restrict}\circ\ce
. Recall that
(-)^{\vee}\circ\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}
factorizes into
(-)^{\vee}\circ\cot_{B}
and
B\otimes_{R}(-)
, then there is a commutative diagram of left adjoints

by the generality of adjoints and their monads, where
\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}}_{\text{aug}}
is defined in the same way like
\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}}
but for augmented

-algebras. Observe that the above diagram can be recovered by restrict the diagram stated in the corollary. Therefore, Lemma
4.10
implies that
C^{*}\circ\rho_{0}\simeq\mathrm{restrict}\circ\ce
, since both ends are left adjoints and their restrictions to
\apbdlie
are homotopic.
∎

Remark 4.12
.

There is a global version of Definition
4.4
in
[
BMN
]
. Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme, and
\QC^{\vee}(X):=\mathrm{Ind}(\coh^{op}(X))
be the
\infty
-category of pro-coherent sheaves. There exists a sifted-colimit-preserving monad
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta}
acting on
\QC^{\vee}(X)_{/\mathbb{T}_{X/R}[1]}
obtained by gluing up the monad of partition Lie algebroids on each affine chart
[
BMN
, Theorem 4.2]
.

We recall their construction as follows: Let
\scr^{\scoh,\laft}_{R}\subset\scr_{R}
be the (not full) subcategory of all coherent
R
-algebras and almost finitely presented morphisms, and
\QC^{\vee}_{/\mathbb{T}[1]}
be the
\infty
-category of the pairs
(B,M\to\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1])
, where
B\in\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}
and
M\in\QC^{\vee}_{B}
. In the light of
[
BMN
, Definition 4.16]
, there is a unique relative monad
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta}
(which they denote as
\mathrm{Lie}^{\pi}_{\Delta}
)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QC^{\vee}_{/\mathbb{T}[1]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\QC^{\vee}_{/\mathbb{T}[1]}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta}}

that preserves sifted colimits fibrewise and specializes to the monad of the free-forgetful adjunction in Notation
4.6
on each fibre. They also prove that the resulting projection
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{/R,\Delta}\to\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}
is a cartesian fibration, and
B\mapsto\lagd
has étale descent
[
BMN
, Proposition 4.18]
. Then,
\mathrm{LieAlge}^{\pi}_{X/R,\Delta}
is defined as

\lim_{\mathrm{Spec}(B)\subset X}\lagd.

4.2
Filtered Chevalley-Eilenberg functor

We first discuss some properties of (completed) Hodge-filtered derived infinitesimal cohomology, which is useful for constructing the filtered Chevalley-Eilenberg functor.

Construction 4.13
.

There is a natural transformation of small-limit-preserving functors

(F_{0}\to\gr_{0}):\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{\fil,R}\to\sseq^{\gen,\vee}_{R},

where both
F_{0}
and
\gr_{0}
are lax monoidal with respect to
\circ
, and this arrow respects the lax monoidal structure. Moreover, this natural transformation sends
\com
(regarded as a filtered derived
\infty
-operad concentrating at degree 0) to the identity of
\com
as an
\infty
-operad.

The above abstract nonsense induces a right adjoint
(F_{0}\to\gr_{0})
as follows

\infcohnewfunctor:\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}\rightleftarrows\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\QC^{\vee}_{R}):(F_{0}\to\gr_{0}),

where the left adjoint is called
Hodge-filtered derived infinitesimal cohomology
. We also write
\infcohnewfunctor(S\to B)
as
\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}_{B/S}
or
\infcohnewfunctor(B/S)
for convenience. It will be shown in Remark
4.16
that
\infcohnewfunctor
is independent from the base ring
R
.

Remark 4.14
.

The notion of
Hodge-filtered derived infinitesimal cohomology
follows Antieau’s ongoing work
[
Ant
]
. We will discuss its relation to Grothendieck’s infinitesimal cohomology at the end of this section.

Notation 4.15
.

(1) Write
\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\QC^{\vee}_{R})
as
\dafilr
for short. For each
B\in\mathrm{SCR}_{R}
, the
\infty
-category
\dafilbr
is defined by a cartesian diagram of
\infty
-categories

Intuitively,
\dafilbr
is the
\infty
-category of the filtered
R
-algebras
A
with a chosen
\gr_{0}A\simeq B
.

(2) A filtered algebra
A\in\dafilr
is said to be
foliation-like
if
A
is complete,
B:=\gr_{0}A
is connective and
\lsym_{B}[\gr_{1}A]_{1}\to\gr A
is a natural equivalence of graded algebras. Here, the
B
-module
\mathbb{L}_{A}:=\gr_{1}A[1]
is called the cotangent complex of
A
. If
\mathbb{L}_{A}
is an almost perfect
B
-module, we say that
A
is
almost perfect
.

For each
B\in\mathrm{SCR}_{R}
, the
\infty
-category
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R}
of
foliation-like algebras over
B
relative to
R
is by definition the full subcategory of foliation-like objects in
\dafilbr
.

where we temporarily emphasize the base ring of
\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}
. The unit map of
\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}^{(R^{\prime})}\dashv(F_{0}\to\gr_{0})
gives rise to a natural transformation

\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}^{(R)}\circ\mathrm{rest}\to\mathrm{rest}\circ\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}^{(R^{\prime})},

which we claim to be an equivalence: for each
(S\to B)\in\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R^{\prime}})^{\Delta^{1}}
, the filtered algebra
\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}^{(R)}(B/S)
naturally admits a
S
-algebra structure and then naturally a
R^{\prime}
-algebra as well. Therefore, we have a natural arrow
\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}^{(R^{\prime})}(B/S)\to\mathrm{F^{H}_{*}}\mathbbb{\Pi}^{(R)}(B/S)
that is an inverse of the above natural transformation.

The next lemma shows that
(\infcohnewfunctor(B/-))^{\wedge}
are naturally foliation-like over
B
relative to
R
.

Lemma 4.17
.

The graded algebra
\gr\infcohnewfunctor(B/S)
is naturally equivalent to
\lsym_{B}[\mathbb{L}_{B/S}[-1]]_{1}
the freely generated graded
B
-algebra.

\infcohnewfunctor\dashv(F_{0}\to\gr_{0})

B\to\gr_{0}\infcohnewfunctor(B/S)
. The universal property of algebraic cotangent complex
5

a pro-coherent version of
[
Rak20
, Construction 4.4.10]
.

where
\gr\dashv\triv
, and the bottom arrow has a left adjoint sending
f:N_{0}\to N_{1}
to
[N_{1}]_{0}\oplus[\mathrm{fib}(f)]_{1}
. The commutativity of the left adjoints then gives rise to an equivalence natural in
f:N_{0}\to N_{1}

\lsym_{R}[N_{1}]_{0}\otimes_{R}\lsym_{R}[\mathrm{fib}(f)]_{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\gr\infcohnewfunctor(\lsym_{R}N_{1}/\lsym_{R}N_{0}).

Notice that
\mathbb{L}_{\lsym_{R}N_{1}/\lsym_{R}N_{0}}
is naturally equivalent to
\lsym_{R}N_{1}\otimes_{R}\mathrm{cofib}(f)
, so the above line is equivalent to
c(\lsym_{R}(N_{1})/\lsym_{R}(N_{0}))
. For the general case, it sufficient to note that both ends of
c(B/S)
are sifted-colimit-preserving.
∎

Corollary 4.18
.

The adjunction
\infcohnewfunctor:\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})^{\Delta^{1}}\rightleftarrows\dafilr:(F_{0}\to\gr_{0})
is relative to
\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})
in the sense of
[
Lur17
, §7.3.2]
, where the left-hand side projects by
\mathrm{ev}_{1}:(A_{0}\to A_{1})\mapsto A_{1}
and the right-hand side projects by
\gr_{0}
.

Corollary 4.19
.

For each
B\in\mathrm{SCR}_{R}
, there is an adjunction

\infcohnewfunctor(B/-):\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}\rightleftarrows\dafilbr:F^{0}.

In particular, the derived infinitesimal derived cohomology
\infcohR
is an initial object in
\dafilbr
.

Remark 4.20
.

One can also see that the adjunction

\infcohnewfunctor(-/R):\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})\rightleftarrows\dafilr:\gr^{0}

exhibits a colocalization. Then,
\dafilbr
can be regarded as a full subcategory of
(\dafilr)_{\infcohR/}
. More generally, consider a colocalization
L
of an
\infty
-category

The categorical fibre over
Lc\in\mathcal{C}
induces a fully faithful embedding
\mathcal{C}\times_{L\mathcal{C}}\{Lc\}\hookrightarrow\mathcal{C}_{Lc/}
.

The same trick also demonstrates that
\dafilbr
can be embedded into
\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}
.

Next, we construct the monad of filtered partition Lie algebroids and compare it with the unfiltered one
T
(
4.4
).

Construction 4.21
.

R\to B
be a morphism in
\mathrm{SCR}^{\scoh}
such that
\mathbb{L}_{B/R}
is almost perfect over
B
. The above discussion permits us to have a commuting diagram as follows

\dalg^{nu}(\fil_{\geq 1}\QC^{\vee}_{B})
is identified with a full subcategory of
\dalg^{aug}(\fil_{\geq 0}\QC^{\vee}_{B})
,
\adic
is formally given by the adjoint functor theorem. Write the composite of the bottom adjunctions as
\cotrfil\dashv\sqzrfil
. Composing with
B
-linear dual, there is a monad
\overline{S}
acting on
\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{B}

\overline{S}:=(-)^{\vee}\circ\cotrfil\circ\sqzrfil\circ(-)^{\vee}.

At the same time, the counit map
\infcohnewfunctor(B/-)\circ F_{0}\to id_{\dafilbr}
induces a morphism of monads

\overline{S}\to\const\circ\overline{T}\circ\colim=:\overline{T}^{\fil}.

(13)

Next, we rectify
\overline{S}
into a sifted-colimit preserving monad through a procedure parallel to the construction of
T
in §
4.1

Lemma 4.22
.

Consider the initial object
\infcohR
and the final object
B
in
\dafilbr
, their relative cotangent fibres are given by
\cotrfil(\infcohR)\simeq 0
and
\cotrfil(R)\simeq(\mathbb{L}_{B/R})_{1}
.

Proof.

By definition,
\cotrfil(\infcohR)
is initial and then null-homotopic. As for
B\in\dafilbr
, notice that
\infcohnewfunctor(B/B)\simeq B
(Remark
4.16
), which implies that
\cotrfil(B)\simeq\big(\cot_{B}(B\otimes_{R}B)\big)_{1}\simeq(\mathbb{L}_{B/R})_{1}
.
∎

Let
\sfil_{\leq-1}\aperf^{\vee}_{B}
be the full subcategory of dually almost perfect objects in
\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{B}
. Using the above lemma and the same reasoning as Lemma
4.3
, for each
V\in\sfil_{\leq-1}\aperf^{\vee}_{B}
, there is a natural morphism of

fibre sequences in

Secondly,
T^{\fil}:=\const\circ T\circ\colim
is a monad
[
BCN21
, Lemma 3.10]
, and the middle vertical arrow in (
14
) extends to a map of sifted-colimit-preserving monads
S\to T^{\fil}
. Furthermore, it gives rise to a sequence of right adjoints

\alg_{T}(\QC^{\vee}_{B})\xrightarrow{\const}\alg_{T^{\fil}}(\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{B})\xrightarrow{\mathrm{forget}}\alg_{S}(\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{B}).

Lemma 4.23
.

The left adjoint
\phi:\lagdf\to\lagd
of the above composite is given by
\colim:\fil^{\vee}_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{B}\to\QC^{\vee}_{B}
on the level of underlying modules, which is a localization functor.

Proof.

Note that the left adjoint of
\const
is
\colim
on the level of underlying modules by Proposition 4.1.9 in
[
Rak20
]
. In the free case,
\phi(\fr_{S}(V))\simeq\colim(\fr_{T^{\fil}}(V))
for each
V\in\fil_{\leq-1}\QC^{\vee}_{B}
, while it follows from (
14
) that
\colim\circ S\simeq T\circ\colim
, so the first statement is true for free
S
-algebras. For general
L\in\lagdf
, it suffices to consider its bar resolution
\br_{\bullet}(id,\fr_{S}\circ\text{forget},L)
and observe that every functor here respects sifted colimits. At the end, the full faithfulness of
\lagd\to\lagdf
is implied by
\colim\circ\const\simeq id
.
∎

Thirdly, there is a Chevalley-Eilenberg functor in the filtered context compatible with that in Proposition
4.7
:

.

There is a commutative square of adjunctions of
\infty
-categories

where
\mathrm{fib}\circ\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}(A)\simeq\big(\const(\mathbb{T}_{B/R})\to\cotrfil(A)^{\vee}\big)
.

Proof.

Set
\aplagdf\subset\lagdf
the full subcategory of filtered partition Lie algebroids with dually almost perfect underlying modules, and
\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafpf}}\subset\dafilbr
the full subcategory of such
A
that
\cotrfil(A)
is almost perfect. The top adjunction is constructed from

\aplagdf\simeq\alg_{\overline{S}}(\sfil_{\leq-1}\aperf^{\vee}_{B})\rightleftarrows(\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafpf}})^{op}

in the same way as in Proposition
4.7
, but everything is replaced by the filtered counterpart.

Now it remains to check the commutativity. The difficulty is that there is no
a priori
comparing transformation between the two paths. However, there is a commuting square of right adjoints
