since
\overline{T}^{\fil}
and
\overline{S}
are defined by some adjunctions in Construction
4.21
. Noting that
\cotrfil\circ\infcohnewfunctor(B/A)\simeq(\cot_{\mathit{R//B}}(A))_{1}
concentrates at degree
1
, the functor
\psi
naturally factorizes into

(\mathcal{C}^{\text{wafp}})^{op}\xrightarrow{\mathfrak{D}}\alg_{T}(\aperf^{\vee}_{B})\xhookrightarrow{\const}\alg_{T^{\fil}}(\sfil_{\leq-1}\aperf^{\vee}_{B}).

Then, there is dually
C^{*}\circ\colim|_{\aplagdf}\simeq F_{0}\circ C^{*}_{\fil}|_{\aplagdf}
. By Lemma
4.10
, the square of left adjoints in the statement commutes.
∎

Definition 4.25
.

The composite
\tc:\lagd\hookrightarrow\lagdf\xrightarrow{C^{*}_{\fil}}(\dafilbr)^{op}
is called
the (completely) Hodge-filtered Chevalley-Eilenberg functor of partition Lie algebroids
.

Then, we go to the main theorem of this section.

Theorem 4.26
.

Let
R\to B
be a map of coherent simplicial commutative rings such that
\mathbb{L}_{B/R}
is an almost perfect
B
-module. The Hodge-filtered Chevalley-Eilenberg functor
\tc
induces a categorical equivalence

\tc:\aplagd\xrightarrow{\simeq}(\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap})^{op},

sending
dually almost perfect
partition Lie algebroids to
almost perfect
foliation-like algebras (Notation
4.15
(2)). Moreover, there is
\gr(\tc(L))\simeq\lsym_{B}[\mathrm{forget}(L)^{\vee}]_{1}
for each
L\in\aplagd
.

Proof.

We begin with the fact that
C^{*}_{\fil}
takes value in complete algebras.

Lemma 4.27
.

For each
L\in\lagdf
,
C^{*}_{\fil}(L)
is complete as a filtered algebra.

Proof.

For
L\simeq\fr_{S}(V)
with
V\in\sfil_{\leq-1}\aperf^{\vee}_{B}
, there is
C^{*}_{\fil}(L)\simeq\sqzrfil(V^{\vee})
, which is surely complete. The general case follows from the fact that
\lagdf
is generated by such
L
with sifted colimits, and
C^{*}_{\fil}
takes colimits to limits.
∎

Now we calculate
\tc
and check the full faithfulness in two steps.

Pointed case:

L:=\rho_{0}(\mathfrak{h})
6
6

6

\rho_{0}
is introduced in Corollary
4.11
for some
\mathfrak{h}\in\alg_{\bdlie}(\aperf^{\vee}_{B})
.

Observe that
\tc(L)\simeq\mathrm{rest}\circ\cet(\mathfrak{h})
is foliation-like and
\gr\cet(\mathfrak{h})\simeq\lsym_{B}[\mathfrak{h}^{\vee}]_{1}
(Corollary
3.33
). Next we verify that
\eta_{L}:\const(L)\to\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(L)
is an equivalence. Consider the following commutative diagram:

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

Since
\rho_{0}(0)\simeq\fr_{T}(0)
, we know that
\mathrm{fib}(\rho_{0}(0))\simeq\mathbb{L}_{B/R}^{\vee}
and the upper line is a fibre sequence. Besides, the right vertical arrow is an equivalence because of
\const(\fr_{T}(0))\simeq\fr_{T^{\fil}}(0)\simeq\mathfrak{D}^{\fil}(B)
. The left vertical equivalence is given by Corollary
3.33
. In the meanwhile,
\mathrm{fib}\circ\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(L)
is equivalent with
\kd(B\otimes_{\infcohR}B\otimes_{B}\cet(\mathfrak{h}))
by Proposition
4.24
, which implies that the lower line is also a fibre sequence. Hence
\mathrm{fib}(\eta_{L})
(and
\eta_{L}
itself) is an equivalence.

General case:

L\in\aplagd
.

Recall that Corollary
4.11
provides a monadic adjunction between
\bdlie
and
\lagd
. Here
L
can be written as the geometric realization of

H_{\bullet}:=\br_{\bullet}(\rho_{0}\circ\mathrm{fib},\rho_{0}\circ\mathrm{fib},L)
. Since
H_{\bullet}
is termwise pointed, and
\mathrm{forget}(H_{n})\simeq\mathrm{fib}(L)\oplus(\mathbb{L}^{\vee}_{B/R})^{\oplus n}
, the graded pieces of
\tc(L)
can be expressed as a totalization of freely generated graded algebras

\gr(\tc(H_{\bullet}))\simeq\lsym_{B}\big[\mathrm{fib}(L)^{\vee}\oplus(\mathbb{L}_{B/R})^{\bullet}\big]_{1}
following the pointed case. Observing that the module of generators concentrates at weight
1
,
\gr(\tc(H_{\bullet}))
is determined by its weight
1
component. Thus this cosimplicial diagram could be obtained by applying
\lsym_{B}\circ[-]_{1}
to the Čech conerve of
\mathrm{forget}(L)^{\vee}\to\mathrm{fib}(L)^{\vee}
. Therefore, we have the expected equivalence
\gr(\tc(L))\simeq\lsym_{B}[\mathrm{forget}(L)^{\vee}]_{1}
using Corollary
3.34
.

Next, we show that
\eta:\const(L)\to\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(L)
is an equivalence. Here
\eta
factorizes into

\const(L)\simeq|\const(H_{\bullet})|\xrightarrow{\alpha}|\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(H_{\bullet})|\xrightarrow{\beta}\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(|H|_{\bullet})\simeq\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(L).

The map
\alpha
is an equivalence from the pointed case. The map
\beta
is the
B
-linear dual of
\cotrfil(\tc(L))\to\tot\cotrfil(\tc(H_{\bullet}))
, whose associated graded map is an equivalence by Corollary
3.34
. Since
B
-linear dual is invariant under completion,
\beta
itself is also an equivalence.

Essential image of
\tc|_{\aplagd}
:

Take an arbitrary
A\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap}\subset\dafilbr
. It is clear that
\mathrm{fib}(\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}(A))\simeq\big(\text{cofib}(\mathbb{L}_{R/k}[-1]\to\gr_{1}A)\big)^{\vee}
is dually almost perfect, i.e.
L_{A}:=\colim\circ\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}(A)
belongs to
\aplagd
. It is remained to show that
\delta_{A}:A\to\tc(L_{A})
is an equivalence. Since both ends are complete and quasi-free with almost perfect
\gr_{1}
components, it suffices to consider
\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}(\delta_{A})
. However, we already have the equivalence
\const(L_{A})\simeq\mathfrak{D}_{\fil}\circ\tc(L_{A})
as above.

∎

Remark 4.28
.

Let
X/R
be a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme. This theorem can be improved into an equivalence between the
sheaves of almost perfect foliation-like algebras
and dually almost perfect partition Lie algebroids on
X
. In the next section, we globalize this theorem with a more geometric method.

We end this section with a discussion on the derived infinitesimal cohomology. Here, Notation
4.15
slightly differs from that in
[
Ant
]
, since Antieau works with ordinary modules rather than pro-coherent modules. But, if
R
is eventually coconnective
, the two notions of Hodge-completed derived infinitesimal cohomology would coincide following Proposition
2.37
, and better, all almost perfect foliation-like algebras live in
\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\m_{R})
. Thus, let us keep this assumption here.

Now, we go to a proposition that justifies the name of
\tc
:

Proposition 4.29
.

Consider a coherent
S\in\mathrm{SCR}_{R//B}
such that
\mathbb{L}_{B/S}
is an almost perfect
B
-module. The foliation-like algebra
\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap}
admits a natural
S
-algebra structure, by which it can be identified with
(\infcohnewfunctor(B/S))^{\wedge}\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/S,\ap}
, the Hodge-completed derived infinitesimal cohomology of
B
relative to
S
.

Proof.

By the adjunction
(-)_{0}:\dalg(\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}\rightleftarrows\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\QC^{\vee}_{R})_{/B}:F^{0}
, the unit map
S\to C^{*}\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)
gives rise to a natural map
\alpha_{S}:S\to\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)
. According to Remark
4.20
, the map
\alpha_{S}
exhibits
\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)
as an object in
\mathcal{D}^{\fil}_{B/S}
. Since
\infcohnewfunctor(B/S)
is initial in
\mathcal{D}^{\fil}_{B/S}
, there is a canonical comparison map
\beta_{S}:(\infcohnewfunctor(B/S))^{\wedge}\to\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)
.

Now, let’s verify that
\beta_{S}
is an equivalence. For each
A\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap}
, set
L_{A}:=\colim\circ\mathfrak{D}(A)\in\aplagd
. Theorem
4.26
gives a natural equivalence
A\simeq\tc(L_{A})
, which implies that
F^{0}(A)\simeq C^{*}(L_{A})
. Furthermore, we have the functorial equivalences of mapping spaces for
A

\map_{\dalg_{R//B}}(S,F^{0}A)\simeq\map_{\lagd}(L_{A},\mathfrak{D}(S))\simeq\map_{\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,\ap}}(\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S),A).

The morphisms
S\to F^{0}(\infcohnewfunctor(B/S))^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{F^{0}(\beta_{S})}C^{*}\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)
correspond to

\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S)\xrightarrow{\gamma_{S}}(\infcohnewfunctor(B/S))^{\wedge}\xrightarrow{\beta_{S}}\tc\circ\mathfrak{D}(S),

where
\beta_{S}\circ\gamma_{S}
is an identity. At the same time, there is
\gamma_{S}\circ\beta_{S}\simeq id_{(\infcohnewfunctor(B/S))^{\wedge}}
by the universal property of
\infcohnewfunctor(B/S)
.
∎

When
S
is discrete and
B
is a smooth
S
-algebra,
(\infcohnewfunctor(B/S))^{\wedge}
is equivalent with Grothendieck’s infinitesimal cohomology as proved in
[
TV23b
, Theorem 3.2]
(using Theorem
5.13
as a dictionary). More generally, in the view of
[
Toë20
, §3]
,
\tc(L)
induces a Hodge filtration on the (complete) foliated infinitesimal cohomology by
L
. It might also be worth mentioning that, when
R
is over
\mathbb{Q}
, derived infinitesimal cohomology agrees with derived de Rham cohomology.

5
Towards infinitesimal derived foliations

We have established an equivalence between partition Lie algebroids and foliation-like algebras under some finiteness conditions (Theorem
4.26
). In §
5.1
, we spell out their geometrization, namely
infinitesimal derived foliations
. Finally, §
5.2
is devoted to the proof of the main theorem (
5.15
). Familiarity with the geometry of filtrations is assumed, referring to
[
Mou21
]
for details.

5.1
Infinitesimal derived foliations

To motivate the construction of infinitesimal derived foliations, we transform the foliation-like algebras into terms of homotopy-coherent cochain complexes.

Recall that, for each
\mathcal{E}\in\calg(\pr^{\st})
, there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{E}\simeq\dg_{-}\mathcal{E}
(Theorem
2.7
), which identifies the completely filtered
\eoo
-algebras and homotopy-coherent
\eoo
-dg-algebras. The next proposition elaborates on a derived analogue.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
R
be a (coherent) simplicial commutative ring,
\dD^{\vee}_{-}
be the derived Hopf algebra from Remark
2.8
and
\mathcal{E}
be
\m_{R}
(or
\QC^{\vee}_{R}
). There is a commuting square of symmetric monoidal functors

\hbox to237.04pt{\vbox to59.04pt{\pgfpicture\makeatletter\hbox{\hskip 118.52002pt\lower-29.51836pt\hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke=#000000} \lxSVG@begingroup@{fill=#000000} \lxSVG@setlinewidth{\the\pgflinewidth}\lxSVG@begingroup@{stroke-width=0.4pt} \lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\nullfont\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@closescope \hbox to0.0pt{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {}{}{}{{}}\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\hbox{\hbox{{\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} {{}{}{{{}}{{}}{{}}{{}}}{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{}{{
{}{}}}{
{}{}}

where one can see that
f
preserves small colimits, since
\gr
and the forgetful functor of comodules are conservative left adjoints. Since every
\infty
-category here is presentable,
f
is a left adjoint and the above diagram gives rise to a morphism of comonads on
\dalg(\gr\mathcal{E})
,
\alpha:\gr\circ\triv\to\dD^{\vee}_{-}\otimes-
. Forgetting the derived algebra structure commutes with both
\gr
and
\triv
by Theorem
3.2
. Besides, the forgetful functor, as a symmetric monoidal functor, also commutes with
\dD^{\vee}_{-}\otimes-
. Therefore, by considering the underlying modules,
\alpha
is an equivalence
[
Rak20
, Theorem 3.2.14]
. Finally, using Barr-Beck-Lurie theorem,
\gr:\dalg(\fil^{\cpl}\mathcal{E})\to\dalg(\gr\mathcal{E})
is comonadic, which implies that
f
is an equivalence.
∎

Remark 5.2
.

(1) The
\infty
-category
\dg_{-}\m_{R}
has a twin
\dg_{+}\m_{R}
[
Rak20
, Definition 5.1.4]
. Although these twin
\infty
-categories are equivalent in
\pr^{\st}
by the shearing functor
[+2*]
, their derived algebra objects differ subtly away from characteristic
0
. For an in-depth study of
\dg_{+}\m_{R}
and its algebras, see
[
MRT22
]
(in the term of graded mixed complexes) and
[
Rak20
]
.

(2) In characteristic
0
, the derived algebras are equivalent to
\eoo
-algebras (both modeled as cdgas). Let
R
be a connective cdga over
\mathbb{Q}
. The shearing functor
[+2*]
induces an auto-equivalence of
\dalg_{R}(\gr\m_{R})
, where
\dD^{\vee}_{-}\simeq[R]_{0}\oplus[R[1]]_{-1}
is sent to a graded Hopf cdga
\dD^{\vee}_{+}\simeq[R]_{0}\oplus[R[-1]]_{-1}
. Then, there is an equivalence

\dalg(\fil^{\cpl}\m_{R})\simeq\mathrm{LComod}_{\dD^{\vee}_{-}}(\dalg(\gr\m_{R}))\xrightarrow{[+2*],\simeq}\mathrm{LComod}_{\dD^{\vee}_{+}}(\dalg(\gr\m_{R})),

(15)

where the right-hand side is denoted as
\epsilon-\mathrm{cdga}^{\sgr}_{R}
and modeled by graded mixed cdgas in
[
TV23a
]
.

Let
B
be a connective cdga finitely presented over
\mathbb{C}
. Following Proposition
2.37
and
5.1
, (
15
) identifies
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/\mathbb{C}}
with the
\infty
-category of graded mixed algebras
(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet},d)
over
\mathbb{C}
with a chosen
\mathcal{A}^{0}\simeq B
such that the natural map
\sym_{B}\mathcal{A}^{1}\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{A}^{\bullet}
of graded cdgas is an equivalence. Therefore, the derived foliations over
B
in the sense of
[
TV23a
, Definition 1.2.1]
form a full subcategory of
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/\mathbb{C}}
via (
15
), which is spanned by such
(\mathcal{A}^{\bullet},d)
that
\mathcal{A}^{1}[-1]
is a connective perfect
B
-module.

Remark 5.3
.

In the
R
-linear context,
\dD^{\vee}
is free as a derived algebra. Indeed, the map of modules
[R[1]]_{-1}\to[R]_{0}\oplus[R[1]]_{-1}\simeq\dD^{\vee}
induces a morphism of derived algebras
\fr_{\com}([R[1]]_{-1})\to\dD^{\vee}_{-}
, which turns out to be an equivalence. Further,
\mathrm{Spec}(\dD^{\vee})
is precisely the loop group
G_{0}:=0\times_{\mathbb{G}_{a}}0
of additive group with the natural
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-action.

Now we geometrize the comultiplication of
\dD^{\vee}_{-}
. Applying
[
Mou21
, §4]
, we have a symmetric monoidal equivalence

\dg_{-}\m_{R}\simeq\mathrm{LComod}_{\mathcal{O}(G_{0})}(\QC(B\mathbb{G}_{m})).

Set
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}:=\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes G_{0}
the semidirect product derived group scheme. Its classifying stack
B\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
can be regarded as a
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
-pointed derived stack.

Lemma 5.4
.

The
B\mathbb{G}_{m}
-pointed stack
B\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
serves as the relative classifying stack of
[G_{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}
, where
[G_{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
is regarded as an abelian graded group stack.

Proof.

The classifying stack of
[G_{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to B\mathbb{G}_{m}
is calculated by the geometric realization of the diagonal
\delta^{\bullet}
of the bisimplicial stack
G_{0}^{\times\bullet}\times\mathbb{G}_{m}^{\times\star}
. The transition
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}^{\times n}\to G_{0}^{\times n}\times\mathbb{G}^{\times n}_{m}
sending
(x_{1},g_{1},\ldots,x_{n},g_{n})
to
(x_{1},g_{1}^{-1}x_{2},\ldots,(g_{1}\ldots g_{n-1})^{-1}x_{n},g_{1},\ldots,g_{n})
identifies
\br_{\bullet}(pt,\mathcal{H}_{\pi},pt)
with
\delta^{\bullet}
. Taking geometric realization finishes this proof.
∎

Proposition 5.5
.
7
7

7

There is a similar result for
\dg_{+}\m_{R}
in
[
MRT22
, §4.2]
, which is harder since
B\mathrm{Ker}
(unlike
G_{0}
) is not affine.

There is an equivalence in
\calg(\pr^{st})
identifying the quasi-coherent sheaves on
B\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
with the graded representations of
[G_{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, i.e.

\QC(B\mathcal{H}_{\pi})\simeq\mathrm{LComod}_{\mathcal{O}(G_{0})}(\QC(B\mathbb{G}_{m}))
.

Proof.

The morphism
p:B\mathbb{G}_{m}\to B\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
is the geometric realization of
p^{\bullet}:G_{0}\times\mathcal{H}_{\pi}^{\times\bullet}\to\mathcal{H}_{\pi}^{\times\bullet}
, where the pullback of quasi-coherent modules induces a diagram of left adjoints
p^{\bullet,*}:\QC(\mathcal{H}_{\pi}^{\times\bullet})\to\QC(G_{0}\times\mathcal{H}_{\pi}^{\times\bullet})
. Due to
\mathcal{O}(G_{0})\simeq R\oplus R[1]
, the diagram
p^{\bullet,*}
is also right adjointable
[
Lur09
, Definition 7.3.1.1]
and the right adjoint diagram
p^{\bullet}_{*}
consisits of small-colimit-preserving functors. Taking totalization, there is a comonadic adjunction of presentable
\infty
-categories

p^{*}:\QC(B\mathcal{H}_{\pi})\rightleftarrows\QC(B\mathbb{G}_{m}):p_{*}

[
Lur09
, Proposition 3.3.3.1, 5.5.3.13]
. Note that
p^{*}
factorizes into the following left adjoints

\QC(B\mathcal{H}_{\pi})\xrightarrow{\simeq}\tot\QC([G_{0}^{\times\bullet}/\mathbb{G}_{m}])\to\mathrm{LComod}_{\mathcal{O}(G_{0})}(\QC(B\mathbb{G}_{m}))\to\QC(B\mathbb{G}_{m}),

where the left equivalence is given by Lemma
5.4
and pullbacks, the middle arrow is taking the underlying cosimplicial diagrams, and the right arrow is the forgetful functor of
\mathcal{O}(G_{0})
-comodules. Thus we have a natural morphism
\alpha:p^{*}p_{*}\to\mathcal{O}(G_{0})\otimes^{\sgr}(-)
of comonads. The cartesian diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{G_{0}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{[G_{0}/\mathbb{G}_{m}]}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces pt}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{B\mathbb{G}_{m}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces B\mathcal{H}_{\pi}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p^{0}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces p}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces q}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\prime}}

induced by Lemma
5.4
helps to rephrase the transformation
\alpha
as
p^{*}p_{*}\to q_{*}q^{*}
. Remind us that
p_{*}
is the totalization of the diagram
p^{\bullet}_{*}
, thus
\pi^{*}p^{*}p_{*}\simeq p^{0}_{*}\pi^{\prime*}q^{*}
. However,
\pi
and
\pi^{\prime}
are smooth covering, which means that
\alpha
is an equivalence using
[
HLP14
, A.1.5, A.1.10]
.
∎

We are then tempted to geometrize foliation-like algebras as derived
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
-equivariant stacks.

Definition 5.6
.

The assignment
S\mapsto\map_{\dalg(\m_{R})}(S,-)
induces a functor
\dalg(\m_{R})^{op}\to\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}\subset\mathrm{Fun}(\mathrm{SCR}_{R},\mathcal{S})
taking values in the
\infty
-category of derived
R
-stacks
[
TV08
, Lemma 2.2.2.13]
. We call it the
functor of non-connective spectra

\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
.

For
B\in\mathrm{SCR}_{R}
, there is a functor of
linear stacks

\mathbb{V}:\m^{op}_{B}\to\mathbf{dSt}_{B}
defined by

E\mapsto\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}(\lsym_{B}E)(-)\simeq\map_{B}(E,-).

The
B
-derived stack
\mathbb{V}(E)
can be equipped with a natural graded structure by posing
E
at weight
1
, which enhances
\mathbb{V}
into

\mathbb{V}:\m^{op}_{B}\to\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{B}.

Moreover, the restriction
\mathbb{V}|_{\m^{op}_{B,+}}
of the enhanced
\mathbb{V}
is fully faithful
[
Mon21
, Theorem 2.5]
, where
\m_{B,+}
is the full subcategory of eventually connective
B
-modules.

On a derived scheme
X
, there is a global version of
\mathbb{V}
sending a quasi-coherent sheaf
E
to the relative non-connective affine stack corepresented by the sheaf of algebras
\lsym_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}E
. Moreover, since essential connectivity is preserved by Zariski descent, there is a fully faithful embedding

\QC_{+}(X)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{X},

sending
essentially connective
coherent sheaves to their “stacky vector bundles”. The essential image is denoted as
\mathbf{dSt}^{lin}_{X}
, which means
linear stacks
over
X
.

Now we consider the extra differential. Recall that the forgetful functor of equivariant stacks along
\mathbb{G}_{m}\to\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
admits a right adjoint
\mathcal{L}^{\sgr}_{\pi}(-/R)
such that the underlying derived stack of
\mathcal{L}^{\sgr}_{\pi}(X/R)
is

\map_{\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}}(\mathcal{H}_{\pi},X)\simeq\map_{\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}}(G_{0}\times\mathbb{G}_{m},X)\simeq\map_{\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}}(G_{0},X).

Definition 5.7

(Definition 2.1
[
TV23b
]
)
.

On some derived scheme
X
over
R
, an
infinitesimal derived foliation (relative to
R
)
is an
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
-equivariant stack
\mathscr{F}
together with a
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-equivariant map
p:\mathscr{F}\to X
, where
\mathbb{G}_{m}
acts trivially on
X
, and
p
is equivalent to
\mathbb{V}(E)\to X
the projection of some linear stack over
X
. More categorically, the
\infty
-category of infinitesimal derived foliation on
X
(relative to
R
)
is given by a pullback of
\infty
-categories

is called
cotangent complex of the infinitesimal derived foliation

\mathscr{F}
and denoted as
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}
.

Remark 5.8

(Restriction on
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}
)
.

We do not require perfectness like in
[
TV23b
, §2]
, because almost perfectness is preferable for PD Koszul duality. Other than this, we want to include
\mathcal{L}^{\sgr}_{\pi}(X/R)
so that
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}(X/R)
always has a final object (see Corollary
5.12
). However, the eventually connectiveness is
neccessary
for defining cotangent complexes. For a counterexample, set
B:=\lsym_{R}(R[2])
and
E
as the
B
-module

\colim\big(B\xrightarrow{\times t}\Sigma^{-2}B\xrightarrow{\times t}\Sigma^{-4}B\xrightarrow{\times t}\ldots\big).

Note that each
B
-derived algebra
(A,a)
consists of a
R
-derived algebra
A
and a point of
\pi_{2}(A)
. The
B
-linear stack
\mathbb{V}(E)
is defined by the assignment
\mathbb{V}(E)\big((A,a)\big)\simeq\map_{\m_{R}}\big(E,(A,a)\big)
. This mapping space is the limit of an inverse system

\Omega^{\infty+2}A\xleftarrow{\times a}\Omega^{\infty}A\xleftarrow{\times a}\Omega^{\infty-2}A\xleftarrow{\times a}\ldots,

which is obviously contractible. However, the
B
-module
E
itself is non-trivial.

Definition 5.9
.

Let
X
be a derived scheme over
R
. An infinitesimal derived foliation
\mathscr{F}\in\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}(X/R)
is said to be
almost perfect
if its cotangent complex
\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}
is an almost perfect quasi-coherent sheaf
[
Lur18
, Definition 2.8.4.4]
. The full subcategory of almost perfect infinitesimal derived foliations is written as
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(X/k)
.

5.2
Realization of foliations

So far, we have translated foliation-like algebras into the language of homotopy-coherent chain complexes (Proposition
5.1
). Additionally, we have observed that
infinitesimal derived foliations
(Definition
5.7
) could potentially encode the foliation-like algebras in a geometric way.

In this subsection, we will confirm this expectation, at least when the cotangent complex is almost perfect. It then leads to the main theorem, Theorem
5.15
, that compares partition Lie algebroids and infinitesimal derived foliations on
locally coherent qcqs
derived schemes. We write
\dalg(\m_{R})
as
\dalg_{R}
and
\mathrm{LComod}_{\dD^{\vee}_{-}}(\dalg(\gr\m_{R}))
as
\dg_{-}\dalg_{R}
for short.

We begin with constructing a functor that realizes foliation-like algebras into infinitesimal derived foliations. Note that the functor
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}:\dalg_{R}^{op}\to\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}
is a right adjoint. In particular, it is symmetric monoidal with respect to the cartesian monoidal structures. In the light of
[
Mou21
, §3]
, there is a symmetric monoidal equivalence
\dalg(\gr\m_{R})\simeq\mathrm{LComod}_{R[t,t^{-1}]}(\dalg_{R})
and then a symmetric monoidal functor
\dalg(\gr\m_{R})\to(\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}})^{op}
induced by
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
.

Similarly, noting that
\dD^{\vee}
is a cocommutative graded Hopf derived algebra and using Proposition
5.1
,
5.5
,
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
induces a symmetric monoidal functor

\dg_{-}\dalg_{R}\to\big(\Omega_{0}\mathbb{G}_{a}-(\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}})\big)^{op}\simeq(\mathcal{H}_{\pi}-\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}})^{op},

where the latter equivalence comes from
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}=\mathbb{G}_{m}\ltimes\Omega_{0}\mathbb{G}_{a}
.

As to
\mathcal{O}:\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}\to(\dalg_{R})^{op}
, the left adjoint of
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
, it is oplax symmetric monoidal and sends
X
to a limit
\mathcal{O}(X):=\lim\limits_{\text{Spec}(A)\to X}A
in
\dalg_{R}
, where
A
ranges among connective algebras. The oplax symmetric monoidal structure refines
\mathcal{O}
into an
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
-equivariant version:

Proposition 5.10
.

From the above discussion, there is a commuting diagram of adjunctions

\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where one has a natural equivalence
\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}\circ\text{forget}\simeq\text{forget}\circ\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}
. Addtionally, given an affine
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-derived scheme
\text{Spec}(A)
, there is an equivalence
\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}(\text{Spec}(A))\simeq A
of connective graded algebras.

Proof.

It is clear by construction that the left adjoints commute. Then, by abstract non-sense, the adjoint map
[\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\times-/\mathbb{G}_{m}]\to id_{\mathcal{H}_{\pi}-\mathbf{dSt}_{\mathit{R}}}
induces a natural transformation

\tau:\text{forget}\circ\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}\to\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}\circ\text{forget}.

To show that it is an equivalence, we need the fact that
\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}(\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}(A))\simeq A
for a graded simplicial commutative ring
A
. The assertion holds obviously for free connective affine
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-schemes
\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\text{Spec}(B)\simeq\text{Spec}(B[t,t^{-1}])
. The general case is done by doing flat descent on
\br_{\bullet}(\mathbb{G}_{m},\mathbb{G}_{m},\text{Spec}(A))\to\text{Spec}(A)
.

For each
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
-stack of the form
[\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\times\text{Spec}(A)/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
, where
A
is a graded simplicial commutative ring, the unit map of
\mathrm{forget}\dashv[\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\times-/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
is given by the multiplication of
G_{0}
as graded group stack. Thus, after applying
\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}
, it becomes the comultiplication
\dD^{\vee}_{-}\otimes^{\sgr}_{R}A\to\dD^{\vee}_{-}\otimes^{\sgr}_{R}\dD^{\vee}_{-}\otimes^{\sgr}_{R}A
which is adjoint to the
id_{A[\epsilon]}
.

For general
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
-stack
X
, note that it can be written as a small colimit
\colim_{i}X_{i}
such that each
X_{i}
has the form of
[\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\times\text{Spec}(A_{i})/\mathbb{G}_{m}]
. Then observe that, the upper left forgetful functor is straightforwardly small-limit-preserving, and the left lower forgetful functor has a left adjoint, which is the opposite of
\mathcal{L}^{\sgr}_{\pi}(-/R)
. Therefore,
\tau
is always an equivalence since both sides takes small colimits to small limits.
∎

Remark 5.11
.

The functors
\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}
and
\mathcal{O}
do not commute with the forgetful functors, because
R[t,t^{-1}]
is not of finite rank over
R
. For a counterexample, consider the free
\mathbb{G}_{m}
-stack
X:=\mathbb{G}_{m}\times\widehat{\mathbb{A}}^{1}_{R,0}
generated by the formal completion of the affine line at its origin. Its graded global section (forgetting the grading) is
\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}(X)\simeq(R[[s]])[t,t^{-1}]
, but the ordinary global section is
\mathcal{O}(X)\simeq(R[t,t^{-1}])[[s]]
.

The discussion on
\infcohnewfunctor
(
4.13
-
4.20
) can be reproduced in the context of ordinary derived algebras, instead of pro-coherent derived algebras. There is a notion of
ordinary
Hodge-filtered infinitesimal cohomology
\infcoho
as the left adjoint of
\gr_{0}:\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\m_{R})\to\dalg_{R}
.

.

5.10
induces a commuting diagram of adjunctions

where
\infcohgr
satisfies that
\infcohgr\circ[-]_{0}\simeq(\infcoho)^{\wedge}
. In particular,
\mathcal{L}^{\sgr}_{\pi}(\text{Spec}(B)/R)
admits a natural infinitesimal derived foliation structure
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}((\infcohRo)^{\wedge})
, corepresented by the Hodge-completed derived infinitesimal cohomology of
\mathrm{Spec}(B)
.

For each
B\in\mathrm{SCR}_{R}
, we can define
ordinary foliation-like algebras
like in Notation
4.15
, where the only difference is that they live in
\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\m_{R})_{/B}
instead of pro-coherent derived algebras. Let
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,b}
(resp.
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,\ap}
) be the full subcategory spanned by
ordinary
foliation-like algebras
A
with
eventually connective (resp. almost perfect) cotangent complex

\gr_{1}(A)[1]
. If
R
is coherent and eventually coconnective, then the functor
\iota
in Remark
2.25
induces an equivalence
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,b}\simeq\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{B/R,b}
by Proposition
2.37
.

Theorem 5.13
.

Let
B
be a simplicial commutative ring over
R
. Taking non-connective spectra induces a functor sending foliation-like algebras to infinitesimal derived foliations, which respects the cotangent complexes

\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}}.

If
B
is further assumed to be coherent, then for each
A\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,\ap}
, the unit map
A\to\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}\circ\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}(A)
is an equivalence. Furthermore, there is a categorical equivalence

\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}:{(\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,\ap})^{op}}\xrightarrow{\simeq}{\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)}.

Proof.

Firstly, Corollary
5.12
provides the following adjunctions of undercategories

{{}{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}{}{{}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces
}{{{{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}}\lxSVG@begingroup@{_scopebegin=1} \lxSVG@transformcm{1.0}{0.0}{0.0}{1.0}{-24.57426pt}{-27.25078pt}\lxSVG@begingroup@{transform=matrix(1.0 0.0 0.0 1.0 -34 -37.71)} \pgfsys@hbox{58}\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope }}}
\lxSVG@closescope
\lxSVG@closescope {\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces}\hss}\lxSVG@discardpath\lxSVG@closescope \hss}}\lxSVG@closescope\endpgfpicture}},

where the left vertical adjunction is equivalent to
\dalg(\gr\m_{B})\rightleftarrows(\mathbb{G}_{m}-\mathbf{dSt}_{B})^{op}
.

On the other hand, the two ends of the promised realization functor can be fully faithfully embedded into the right vertices: This holds for
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
by definition. As for
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,b}
, note that
(-)^{\wedge}\circ\infcoho\dashv\gr_{0}
is a colocalization and use Remark
4.20
. Besides, since the forgetful functors commutes with the
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
’s,
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}|_{\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,b}}
takes value in
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
and respects the cotangent complexes.

Next, assuming that
B
is coherent, we demonstrate that
A\simeq\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}\circ\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}(A)
for
A\in\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,\ap}
. According to Corollary
5.12
, it is sufficient to show that
\text{forget}(A)\simeq\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}\circ\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}(\text{forget}(A))
, which is guaranteed by Lemma
5.14
as below. Conversely, for each
\mathscr{F}\in\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
,
\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}(\mathscr{F})
is equivalent to
\lsym_{B}[\mathbb{L}_{\mathscr{F}}[-1]]_{1}
, thus
\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}(\mathscr{F})
is an almost perfect foliation-like algebra. The adjunction map
\mathscr{F}\to\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\mathcal{O}^{\epsilon-\sgr}(\mathscr{F})
is then an equivalence, since the underlying map of graded stacks is, which implies that
\mathscr{F}
lies in the essential image of
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{B/R,\ap}
under
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
.

∎

Lemma 5.14
.

Let
B
be a coherent simplicial commutative ring, and
E
be an almost perfect
B
-module. Set
C:=\lsym_{B}[E]_{1}
the freely generated graded algebra, then the unit map
\eta:C\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}\circ\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}(C)
is an equivalence.

Proof.

This lemma is inspired by
[
Mou21
]
. Observe that there exists some
n\in\mathbb{N}
such that
E\in\aperf_{B,\geq-n}
. We do induction on
n
. The case
n=0
is done in the proof of Proposition
5.10
. When
n>0
, choose a map
s:B[-n]^{\oplus m}\to E
such that
\pi_{-n}(s)
is surjective. Write
B[-n]^{\oplus m}
as
P[-1]
and
\text{cofib}(s)
as
F
. Now we consider the Čech conerve
F^{\bullet}
of
E\to F
, where
F^{k}\simeq F\oplus P^{\oplus k}
belongs to
\aperf_{B,\geq-(n-1)}
.

Since
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
takes small colimits to limits, the simplicial object
\mathbb{V}(F^{\bullet})
is identified with the Čech nerve of
\mathbb{V}(F)\to\mathbb{V}(E)
. Then
q:|\mathbb{V}(F^{\bullet})|\to\mathbb{V}(E)
is
(-1)
-truncated
[
Lur09
, Proposition 6.2.3.4]
. Lemma 2.7 in
[
Mon21
]
shows that
q
is also an epimorphism and then an equivalence. Let’s look at the following commutative square

where the top arrow is an equivalence by Corollary
3.34
, the right one is an equivalence by induction hypothesis. In conclusion,
\eta
is an equivalence as well.
∎

Combining Theorems
4.26
and
5.13
, we obtain the main comparison theorem between infinitesimal derived foliations and partition Lie algebroids on derived schemes. For a global definition of partition Lie algebroids, see
[
BMN
, §4]
, or refer to Remark
4.12
for a brief recollection.

Theorem 5.15

(
Main theorem
)
.

Let
X
be a locally coherent qcqs derived scheme over an eventually coconnective coherent simplicial commutative ring
R
, and assume that
\mathbb{L}_{X/R}
is an almost perfect quasi-coherent sheaf. There is a categorical equivalence

\aplagdX\xrightarrow{\simeq}\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(X/R)

bewteen
dually almost perfect
partition Lie algebroids and
almost perfect
infinitesimal derived foliations.

Proof.

When
X=\mathrm{Spec}(B)
, the equivalence is given by
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc
(Theorem
4.26
and
5.13
).

In the global case, we need to show that
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc
is compatible with base change on both sides along finitely presented open immersions
\mathrm{Spec}(B^{\prime})\subset\mathrm{Spec}(B)
. Here, we follow the construction in
[
BMN
]
.

Relative version of
\tc
:

Set
\dalg^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}:=\mathrm{Fun}(\Delta^{1},\dalg_{R})
, regarded as an
\infty
-category over
\dalg_{R}
by the projection
\mathrm{ev}_{1}:(A_{0}\to A_{1})\mapsto A_{1}
. Let
\QC
be the
\infty
-category of all the pairs
(A,M)
, where
A
is a derived
R
-algebra and
M
is an
A
-module (a non-connective version of
[
Lur18
, Notation 25.2.1.1]
). The apparent projection
\QC\to\dalg_{R}
has a section
\mathbb{L}[-1]
given by
A\mapsto(A,\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1])
, inducing an
\infty
-category
\QC_{\mathbb{L}[1]/}
of pairs
(A,\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1]\to M)
. Then, there is an adjunction

\cot:\dalg^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\rightleftarrows\QC_{\mathbb{L}[1]/}:\sqz

(16)

relative to
\dalg_{R}
in the sense of
[
Lur17
, §7.3.2]
, where
\cot:(A_{0}\to A_{1})\mapsto\big(A_{1},\mathbb{L}_{A_{1}/R}[-1]\to\mathbb{L}_{A_{1}/A_{0}}[-1]\big)
, and
\sqz(A,\alpha:\mathbb{L}_{A/R}[-1]\to M)
has the underlying arrow in
R
-modules as
A\oplus_{\alpha}M\to A
8
8

8

Recall that
d_{DR}:A\to\mathbb{L}_{A/R}
is the universal differential. The extension module
A\oplus_{\alpha}M
is
\mathrm{cofib}(\alpha\circ d_{DR}:A[-1]\to M)
.
. Theorem 3.29 in
[
BMN
]
states that
\dalg_{R//B}\rightleftarrows(\m_{B})_{\mathbb{L}_{B/R}[-1]/}
, the fibre of (
16
) over
B\in\scr_{R}
, restricts to a comonadic adjunction of full subcategories

\cot:\dalg^{\wedge\afp}_{R//B,\geq 0}\rightleftarrows(\aperf_{B,\geq 0})_{\mathbb{L}_{B/R}[-1]/}:\sqz

where
\dalg^{\wedge\afp}_{R//B,\geq 0}
is defined in
[
BMN
, Notation 3.28]
(but we only need to know that it lies in
\scr^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\subset\dalg^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}
).

Now, let
\mathcal{M}
be the
\infty
-category of all the pairs
(B,M)
with
B\in\mathrm{SCR}^{\scoh,\laft}_{R}
(Remark
4.12
) and
M\in(\aperf_{B,\geq 0})_{\mathbb{L}_{B/R}[-1]}
, and
\mathcal{A}\subset\dalg^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}
denote the full subcategory of all
A\to B
such that
B\in\mathrm{SCR}^{\scoh,\laft}_{R}
and
A\in\dalg^{\wedge\afp}_{R//B,\geq 0}
. Then (
16
) restricts to a fibrewise comonadic adjoint pair

\cot:\mathcal{A}\rightleftarrows\mathcal{M}:\sqz

(17)

relative to
\scr^{\scoh,\laft}_{R}
.

The restriction of the relative monad
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{\Delta}
(Remark
4.12
) on
\mathcal{M}^{op}(\subset\QC^{\vee}_{/\mathbb{T}[1]})
is given by
(\cot\circ\sqz(-)^{\vee})^{\vee}
. Furthermore, the fibrewise comonadicity of (
17
) induces an equivalence
C^{*}:\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\dap}_{/R,\Delta,\eqslantless 0}\simeq\mathcal{A}^{op}
via the Chevalley-Eilenberg complex on each fibre, where
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\dap}_{/R,\Delta,\eqslantless 0}\subset\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{/R,\Delta}
is spanned by all
(B,L)
such that the underlying module of
L
lies in
\aperf^{\vee}_{B,\eqslantless 0}
.

Now, consider
\dalg^{\fil}_{R}:=\dalg(\fil_{\geq 0}\m_{R})
, which projects to
\dalg_{R}
by
\gr_{0}
.
There is a functor
\infcohnewfunctor^{or}:\dalg^{\Delta^{1}}_{R}\to\dalg^{\fil}_{R}
relative to
\scr_{R}
parallel to Construction
4.13
but in the ordinary algebraic context. Then, the reasoning of Lemma
4.17
induces a functor relative to
\scr^{\scoh,\laft}

(\infcohnewfunctor)^{\wedge}:\mathcal{A}\to\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,or}_{/R}\times_{\scr_{R}}\scr^{\scoh,\laft}\hookrightarrow\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{/R}\times_{\scr^{\scoh}_{R}}\scr^{\scoh,\laft}_{R},

and the right-hand side will be abbreviated as
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol}_{\laft/R}
. It induces a functor relative to
\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}

F^{0}:\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\dap}_{/R,\Delta,\eqslantless 0}\xrightarrow{C^{*},\simeq}\mathcal{A}^{op}\xrightarrow{(\infcohnewfunctor)^{\wedge}}\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R}

whose restriction to the fibre over
B
agrees with
\tc|_{\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\dap}_{B/R,\Delta,\eqslantless 0}}
(Definition
4.25
) by Proposition
4.29
. As Lemma
4.10
is functorial in
\mathscr{A}
,
F^{0}
extends to a fibrewise sifted-colimit-preserving functor

\tc:\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi}_{/R,\Delta}\to\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R},

which agrees with
\tc
on each fibre. Moreover, Theorem
4.26
shows that it restricts to an equivalence
\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\dap}_{/R,\Delta}\simeq\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R,\ap}
.

Relative version of
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
:

Following Proposition
5.10
, there is

\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\text{-}\mathbf{dSt}_{R}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{forget}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}\mathbf{dSt}_{R}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}}\dalg(\gr\m_{R})^{op}\xrightarrow{\gr_{0}}\dalg^{op}_{R}.

Then, we take the fibre product of
\infty
-categories

\mathcal{S}t^{\sgr}:=\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}\mathbf{dSt}_{R}\times_{\dalg^{op}_{R}}\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R},\ \ \ \ \mathcal{S}t^{\epsilon\textit{-}\sgr}:=\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\text{-}\mathbf{dSt}_{R}\times_{\dalg^{op}_{R}}\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}.

Corollary
5.12
gives rise to categorical sections
\mathrm{Spec}(-):\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}\to\mathcal{S}t^{\sgr}
and
\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(-/R):\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}\to\mathcal{S}t^{\epsilon\textit{-}\sgr}
as well as an adjunction

(\mathcal{S}t^{\epsilon\textit{-}\sgr})_{/\mathcal{L}^{\pi}}\rightleftarrows(\mathcal{S}t^{\sgr})_{/\mathrm{Spec}}
relative to
\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}
. Then, we can consider those
(B,\mathcal{F}\to\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R))\in(\mathcal{S}t^{\epsilon\textit{-}\sgr})_{/\mathcal{L}^{\pi}}
such that
\mathcal{F}
is forgotten to a linear stack over
\mathrm{Spec}(B)
, which forms a full subcategory
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}\subset\mathcal{S}t^{\epsilon\textit{-}\sgr}
. The fibre of
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}
over some
B\in\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}
is exactly
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
.

Theorem
5.13
shows that

\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R,\ap}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}}\mathcal{H}_{\pi}\text{-}\mathbf{dSt}_{R}\xrightarrow{\mathrm{forget}}\mathbb{G}_{m}\text{-}\mathbf{dSt}_{R}\xrightarrow{\mathcal{O}^{\sgr}}\dalg(\gr\m_{R})^{op}

is equivalent to
\gr
, so
\mathrm{forget}\circ\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
lifts to a relative functor
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R,\ap}\to(\mathcal{S}t^{\sgr})_{/\mathrm{Spec}}
whose image contains only linear stacks. Meanwhile, every fibre of
\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R,\ap}
has a final object in the form of
(B,(\infcohR)^{\wedge})
. Therefore,
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}
lifts to a functor
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}:\dalg^{\mathcal{F}ol,op}_{\laft/R,\ap}\to\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}
relative to
\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}
fibrewise agreeing with Theorem
5.13
, and its essential image
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}
consists of all
(B,\mathcal{F})
such that
\mathbb{L}_{\mathcal{F}}
is an almost perfect
B
-module.

Base change structure of
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc
:
In summary, we have constructed an equivalence

\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc:\mathrm{LieAlgd}^{\pi,\dap}_{/R,\Delta}\simeq\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}

of
\infty
-categories over
\scr^{\scoh,\laft,op}_{R}
. The left-hand side is a cartesian fibration, and
B\mapsto\aplagd
has étale descent
[
BMN
, Proposition 4.18]
. In particular, the cartesian arrows are in the form of
(B^{\prime},\mathbb{T}_{B^{\prime}/R}[1]\times_{\mathbb{T}_{B/R}[1]\otimes_{B}B^{\prime}}L\otimes_{B}B^{\prime})\to(B,L)
. The right-hand side also has sufficient cartesian edges. For each
\mathrm{Spec}(B^{\prime})\to\mathrm{Spec}(B)
, the pullback of
\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}
sending
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
to the fibre product of
\mathcal{H}_{\pi}
-stacks
\mathcal{F}\times_{\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)}\mathcal{L}^{\pi}(\mathrm{Spec}(B^{\prime})/R)
. Now, we see that
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc
respects the cartesian edges, i.e.
\mathrm{Spec}^{\textit{nc}}\circ\tc
is compatible with base change. Additionally,
B\mapsto\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}_{\ap}(\mathrm{Spec}(B)/R)
has étale descent by considering the cotangent complex. Thus, for each locally coherent qcqs derived scheme
X
, there is an equivalence
\aplagdX\simeq\mathcal{F}ol^{\pi}(X/R)
by gluing up the equivalences on the affine charts.
